A queued task has not started, so nothing of it can be stopped: its panel carries Remove where a started task's has Stop, and Remove asks first, with Remove from queue and Cancel, then takes the task out of the queue and keeps its brief, through the same `POST /api/tasks/lifecycle` stop action a started task's Stop sends.
The supervisor starts ready queued work by itself in the Tasks order, and Start and the CEO's `goblins spawn` share the [brief readiness contract](commands/brief.md).
Start on a queued task's panel sends `POST /api/tasks/start` with the task's ID, guarded by the Host, Origin and token checks like every other change, and the supervisor dispatches it through `goblins spawn <id> --project <p> --brief <data/<id>/brief.md> --harness <h> [--model <m>] [--effort <e>] [--mode <m>]`, the same binary the CEO runs.
The project is the brief's `## Project` line, or the backlog row's repo; harness, model and effort are what the backlog row names, such as `(harness: codex, model: gpt-6-astra)`, then what the brief names on lines of their own under its Delivery heading, such as `effort: high`, then the fleet's defaults, `claude` on `claude-opus-5-5` at `xhigh` (the default model and effort apply to Claude Code only).
The mode is the one the brief names under its Delivery heading, such as `mode: direct-PR`, which is the one brief readiness validated; the backlog row's mode is what a brief the start itself writes is given.
A line under any other heading of the brief names none of the four, so task text that holds a line such as `mode: direct-PR` changes nothing, on a first start or on the one after a failed spawn.
The mode so named is a request to [delivery intake](contract/delivery.md#delivery), which selects the task's mode at its start: a narrower one with neither a qualifying assessment nor a CEO-authorized exception starts the task in no-mistakes, and the spawn says why.
A task with no brief yet gets one written from its backlog row, and the CEO is told through the wake queue, before `goblins spawn` runs.
It is refused with 409 and the reason, before anything runs, when the task already has a live task record, is not queued, already finished (its last report, live or archived, was done, its pull request or one of its branch merged, or the CEO retired it and no brief was written for it since, which its card says in place of Start), waits on a blocker its backlog row names, or names no project or a harness, mode, model or effort `goblins spawn` cannot take; a board started without a dispatcher, such as a test fixture's, refuses every Start.
The board sends a refusal to the CEO through `POST /api/ceo/report` and shows no line for it.
One goblin starts or resumes at a time, and only with 5 GB of memory and of commit and the disk floor free, so a Start or a Resume clicked while another runs, or while memory, commit or disk is short, is accepted at once and waits its turn: the Overlord's clicks run oldest first, ahead of the scheduler's own picks, each as soon as the start or resume before it ends and memory and disk allow (the Overlord, 2026-10-08: "it took multiple clicks with an error message").
The snapshot marks such a task `asked`, its Start shows as `starting` and its Resume as phase `resuming`, and while memory or disk is short the card says Starts at 5 GB free or Resumes at 5 GB free.
A Start on its way, from the click until the supervisor answers, reads the same, so the card the Overlord clicked says Starting, or Starts at 5 GB free while memory is short, where he clicked it, and a task whose Start waits its turn stays in Tasks.
A Start or a Resume clicked again while one is on its way or waits its turn is accepted and changes nothing, and a Pause or Stop takes back a Resume that still waits.
One that can no longer run when its turn comes, such as a goblin the CEO resumed meanwhile, is dropped, and one that fails goes to the CEO as `start failed:` or `resume failed:`.
A Resume its relaunch refuses before it starts a terminal, as for another validation run owning its branch, leaves the goblin paused as it was, with its reason and what resumes it.
Its card stays where it was with the refusal as its `action_error`, its status log gains one `lifecycle-refused:` line, the CEO is told once, and the scheduler leaves it for a Resume that is clicked or typed, so it never holds the slot ahead of the queue.
Validation identity checks and policy handling on Resume follow the [pipeline resume contract](pipeline.md#resuming-a-paused-task).
After a resume or switch, the board and fleet read lifecycle health from the current lifecycle record, so an old monitor reading cannot keep the previous session's pause or launch state alive.
The supervisor waits for `goblins spawn` to end, which confirms the goblin works before it returns, and never cuts it short, since a killed spawn strands its task.
From the moment the supervisor starts a task, a Start's or its scheduler's own, the snapshot marks it `starting` and not `asked` and is published at once, whatever the snapshot's `scheduling` says, and the board takes the task out of Tasks into In progress, where the supervisor puts it once `goblins spawn` ends, at the top for the Overlord's own Start, which the snapshot marks `overlord_start`, and at the bottom for the scheduler's own: it reads Starting at a working dot on its card, its panel, its terminal pane and its canvas node until the supervisor has evidence of what its goblin does, which can follow the end of `goblins spawn` by some seconds, or for two minutes from its session's start at most.
While a start or a resume runs, the supervisor looks for its task record and terminal host every 250 milliseconds and publishes a change the moment either is written, so the board opens the new terminal at once rather than at the next snapshot.
Then it tells the CEO through the wake queue, as `goblins notify` does, with a notify keyed by the task: `started: the Overlord started this from the board, ...` after which the task goes to the top of the attention order, or `started: the supervisor started the next queued task ...` after which it joins the bottom of the attention order, under every goblin In progress lists, or a refusal handled under the [dispatch contract](contract/dispatch.md#the-fleet-never-idles); none of these notifies is a question.
For a start failure, the snapshot's `start_error` carries the last line `goblins spawn` printed: the task stays in Tasks and reads Start failed at a red dot, never a yellow one, on its card and its panel, with that line behind its panel's Details.
An accepted Start answers 202 with the revision from which every snapshot shows it, and the board opens the new goblin's panel once the snapshot shows its session; an older snapshot that still carries the last Start's failure neither shows that failure on the card nor stops the wait.
Completed lists verified delivery, and within the last week the newest 20 entries of history: tasks cleanup finished, from a status log left without its record or one the archive holds, and pull requests merged into a fleet repository, read from merge commits on origin's default branch of this home and each checkout under the projects root, locally.
History is rebuilt away from the supervisor's loop, so it never delays native events, the heartbeat or a snapshot: at start, within 10 seconds of a task record being removed, a gate seeing its task merge or a goblin reporting done, and otherwise every minute.
A rebuild reads a checkout with git again only when a fetch or a remote change rewrote its git files, and one git cannot read is tried again after 30 minutes.
A merge commit several of those repositories hold, such as a fork's copy of its upstream's history, is listed once, under the repository its pull request was opened in: the one whose origin publishes that pull request's head, `refs/pull/<number>/head`, as the merge's second parent.
Each origin's pull request heads come from one `git ls-remote` call, which is kept and made again only for a pull request it does not list or lists with another head, such as one listed while it was still open, at most every 10 minutes, so a fork carrying a week of its upstream's merges costs one call per repository; when no origin says, the merge goes to the first repository listed, this home first, and a failed call is reported.
A merged pull request whose live task already shows the merge, in phase merged or done, or whose live goblin's latest report is done with that pull request, appears only on that task's card.
A live goblin whose latest report is done, whose pull request merged, and whose deploy, where one ran, succeeded, is done and verified and moves to Completed at once, before its cleanup: the Overlord, 2026-10-15, "make sure you dont have retired landed deployed goblins still a part of in progress, that should be completed".
One still deploying, or whose deploy failed or was cancelled, stays In progress with its deploy in its panel, and so does a gate task merged but awaiting landed-content verification whose goblin has not reported done.
A delivered goblin that takes a prompt after its done report, such as one resumed to look for more work, is at work and stays In progress, Working, while the turn that prompt started runs, whatever its gate proved; once that turn ends with no newer report it is Completed again.
Its panel in Completed offers no Pause, Resume or Stop, since nothing of it runs to pause or stop, and cleanup retires its goblin.
Any other merged pull request keeps its Completed card, even when a live task reported it earlier: a goblin that went back to work, or that is blocked, failed or waiting on a question, keeps its own proven state on its card.
A history card is its pull request link, since it has no live worktree to review.
A finished task finds its merge among every merge of the week, not only the newest 20, so an older merge still marks it merged.
A finished task's GitHub pull request that no merge commit shows is asked about with one GraphQL read of GitHub's API on a history refresh, or `gh pr view` for one on another host: merged (a squash merge leaves no merge commit) reads Merged, closed without merging reads Closed, and open reads Finished.
A merged or closed answer is kept and an open one is asked again after 10 minutes.
A pull request GitHub no longer holds, because a repository was recreated under the same owner and name or the pull request is gone, is no answer at all: the task keeps the outcome and link it reported, its card reads Finished, evidence unavailable with why in its pull request's tip, and the address is not asked about again, since no later read can make it evidence.
Nor is a newer pull request a recreated repository gave the same number: one opened after the task finished is never its result, so its merge and its title are not adopted and the card says so the same way.
Neither wakes the CEO, since neither is a failed read it could act on.
An ask that failed keeps what GitHub last answered and is asked again on the next refresh.
The board reports it as an error, which wakes the CEO, only once it failed on three refreshes in a row, and from then on it is asked again after 10 minutes.
The asks of one refresh share a 5-second budget, so a slow GitHub never holds up the supervisor; a pull request not asked before it runs out reads Finished and is asked on the next refresh, and an ask it cuts off counts as failed.
The card wears GitHub's icons and colors: the purple merge icon for Merged, the red closed pull request icon for Closed, and the pull request icon otherwise.
A live task's pull request link wears the merge icon too once its gate shows the merge, in phase merged and after its landed content is verified.
A task no native hook has reported takes its status from the fleet's own records: a question it is still waiting on in the wake queue, then what its gate proved, then what Herdr sees in its pane or, for a native task, what the monitor reads from its terminal, and it is evaluated once a minute like any other.
Each card is one row, like a conversation in a list (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "minimal cards are better like an imessage history with name status of whether working time working or last worked tickets pr icons etc and harness icon"): its goblin's avatar, its name and title, such as Jerry - Code Designer, a queued task's and a finished goblin's included, or the task's short title while it has no goblin, then its status, after its dot, in at most two lines, then its project on a muted line of its own under the status (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "please include project repo below status line in board"), with GitHub's mark beside it opening that repository in a new tab, the repository in its tip, when the project's origin is on GitHub, and at its right only its ticket and pull request as icons (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "for now, we just want to know the pull request and the issue"), each opening what it names with its words in its tip, a pull request linked only when the reported value is an https URL; its checks and deploy, the goblin it helps and its own latest status line are in its panel.
A title shows without the harness a backlog row names after a semicolon, such as "; Claude Code", on every card, panel, node and alert, since the harness mark shows it; the adjust form keeps the row's own title.
A queued card that waits on something adds no line about it, at the Overlord's word (2026-10-05): its status says what it waits for in place of Queued (2026-10-09), at the queued dot, from the snapshot's `waits` for the task, each wait its row's `blocked-by:` has not cleared.
A time reads Starts and when, such as Starts Oct 10, a time read as a card's date is, free memory Waits for 12 GB free, a pull request Waits for PR #7 to merge, and a task Waits for its goblin by name, or its id when the board does not show it.
A wait the scheduler cannot read says why, such as No task is named "quiet-night", and so does one that can never clear, a task that stopped without delivering or a pull request closed without merging, and a second wait adds ", and 1 more".
Nothing folds under a queued task's status in its panel (the Overlord, 2026-10-10: "the [More] dropdown is not really necessary"): the CEO's note on the wait stays on its backlog row, and a task that already finished says what says so in one plain, muted sentence under that status (the CEO's decision 1013298: "No panel tips, those texts move onto the page").
A paused or finished card shows when, kept short (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "why do resume dates need to be so long and typed out, avoid overlap"): its time when that is today, its short weekday, such as Mon, within six days either way, else its short month and day, such as Oct 14, with the whole date and time, such as Mon, Sep 28, 2026, 7:22 PM, in its tip, where a live card shows its clock, and its status says what; the Next up chip sizes to its label and never wraps it.
A goblin's name and title and its project wrap onto further lines rather than ending in an ellipsis (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "please flex wrap goblin name and title"); a task with no goblin yet keeps its title to one line, and a title cut there ends in an ellipsis and shows in full in the board's tip on hover or keyboard focus.
A named goblin's card shows its task in that tip instead, a finished goblin's too (the CEO's decision 1008011), and its panel's header shows its name and title, with its task id beside them in its Task view (#1987).
A goblin held at a pipeline gate keeps the gate as its status, such as Waiting on the CEO: rebase gate while the gate's decision is pending, with the gate's reason in the status's tip, and under it a muted line says what its terminal is doing and its last report, such as Terminal active · Last report: working, since neither is proof the gate moved: a submitted answer clears no gate until the gate itself moves (`frontend/tests/gate-card-status.spec.ts`).
Tips show only in the Board and Orchestration views, where a card's or a node's details come up on hover (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "Only need tool tips in board and orchestration"); the chat, its box, the top bar, the panel, the panes and the Command Center show none, and each control there keeps its accessible name.
Each of those tips is one box that floats over the whole page (`frontend/src/tips.ts`), shown on the frame the pointer or the keyboard's focus comes to a part carrying `data-tip`, with no wait and no fade, so no scrolling box cuts a tip off and a dimmed control does not dim its tip; a part inside a modal dialog shows its tip inside that dialog, which is drawn over the page.
The tip holds its whole text: it grows to its text up to 320 px, or the window's width less 16 px, then wraps, breaking a long word if it must, at 16 px on a solid surface a step lighter than the cards, with an edge.
It is kept inside the window on every side and is gone on the frame the pointer or the focus leaves its part, with no fade, and at any press, even one its part keeps to itself, as a canvas node starting its drag does.
A pointer that moves from one part straight to the next swaps their tips on that frame.
Every tip touches its own part, 8 px from it: above the part, else below it, else to its right, else to its left, on the first of those sides where the whole tip fits inside the window with 8 px to spare.
Along that side it is centered on the part, moved only as far as the window's edge asks, and a small arrow on its edge points at the part's middle, or as near it as the tip's rounded corner lets it stand.
The tip of a control, a chip or a mark on a card touches that part, never the whole card, and no tip opens in a corner of the window, so a tip may lie over what is beside its part, a card's other controls included.
A card's own tip, its title in full or its goblin's task, is the whole card's: by the same rule it stands above the card's middle, wherever on the card the pointer is, the status dot included, which has no tip of its own.
A tip that fits on no side of its part, as the tip of a very long title can on a short window, goes below the part and is kept inside the window.
While it shows it follows its part: it is drawn again when the page scrolls, resizes or changes and when its own box changes size, as it does when its font arrives after its text, and then on every frame for as long as the part moves or a transition or an animation runs on it or on anything around it, so it stays against a part that moves, as a card lifting under the pointer does, and a tip beside a part at rest asks the browser for no frames.
It says what its part's tip says now, and goes when that part leaves the page; a part pressed with a mouse or a pen shows no tip until the press is released, so a card being dragged shows none, while a finger shows a tip for as long as it is down.
Until 2 October 2026 only cards had the floating tip and every other tip was drawn by a stylesheet rule inside its part, where a scrolling box could cut it off, a window edge could run through it and a dimmed button made it see-through.
Until 9 October 2026 a tip waited 1.5 seconds for the pointer to rest, and a card's tips opened over or under the whole card and slid along it, clear of the controls around, which could leave a tip far from the part it named.
Each card in Tasks and In progress also shows a quiet clock at its top right, beside its name, or in a narrow list under its status, in whole minutes, hours and days (just started, 47m, 2h 14m, 1d 3h), counted from the snapshot's `since`: when a live task's worktree folder was created, which `goblins spawn` makes fresh for each goblin and a switch keeps, so the clock counts the whole session across switches, or its spawn generation's time when that folder cannot be read; or when a queued task's `data/<id>/brief.md` was created.
A queued row with no brief, or a live task with neither a readable worktree nor a generation that records a time, gets no clock rather than a guessed one, and a completed card shows none.
Each card carries the mark of the harness its goblin runs at the end of its icons: Codex's mark for Codex (a cloud holding a terminal prompt, drawn in the board's own icon set), Claude's for Claude Code, π for pi, Kimi's for Kimi and a terminal for any other harness; its tip names the harness, then the model by its short name (`frontend/src/model-name.ts`) and the effort it runs, the one place a card names them, since a line for the model or its route on the card is too wordy (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "when you hover the icon, it should tell you the model that it's using. You don't have to have it displayed on it"), and a click on it selects the card.
A card's clock sits at its top right and its icons under it, the harness mark last; in a list narrower than 360 px, such as a column beside the CEO's panel, the clock and the icons share a line under the status, the clock at the left, so the name and status keep the card's whole width: every part of a card has its own place, so none is drawn over another, and the card's text is never smaller than 16 px.
Beside a goblin's pull request, its panel's link row says where its hosted checks stand, as the supervisor's CI poll read them every 2 minutes: Testing, Checks failed, Checks cancelled or Checks passed, in blue, red, grey or mint, with the failed or cancelled checks named beside it in a few muted words.
Testing opens the first running check's page, the run that tests the pull request, and a failure the first failed check's own page, where its log is, when that page is on GitHub, and the pull request's checks otherwise; every other state opens the pull request's checks, and nothing off GitHub is opened.
Approved follows it once a reviewer approved the pull request.
Before them, a goblin in a repository that runs `goblins gate test` shows the newest run of its change, read from the run's report in the store: its gate's run of the commit its pull request is at, which names no task, or a run it made itself.
Local tests passed or Local tests failed, in mint or red, says once it failed what failed, the level, how long the run took and how long it waited for its turn beside it in a few muted words, and opens the end of the run's log, redacted, served by the board from the store and from nowhere else.
Once a task's pull request is merged, its panel, in Completed too, says how the deploy of that merge stands, apart from its checks and in their look: Deploying, Deployed, Deploy failed or Deploy cancelled, with the deploy workflows and the commit beside it in a few muted words once it failed or was cancelled, opening the run that failed, else the newest one.
The same CI poll reads it from the push runs on the repository's default branch: the newest run of each workflow whose name says deploy, for the commit the merge pushed, found by the commit's title, "Merge pull request #N" or a squash's "(#N)".
A merge train lands its pull requests with one merge commit of its own, "Merge train <id>: #1, #2", so that commit's run is the deploy of each pull request it names.
A deploy that no workflow on the default branch runs shows nothing: a site shipped with the Vercel CLI, for one, leaves no run to read, and its panel stops at its pull request.
A [merge train](contract/delivery.md#merge-trains) shows as one card for each batch of pull requests: a running train at the top of In progress, and a finished train that landed at least one pull request at the top of Completed for six hours after it finished.
A column holding one is not empty, and a train still testing stays In progress even when an earlier half landed.
A train that landed nothing is folded into the later train that took its pull requests on, whose card counts its runs on from it, so a train main moved under three times and the train that then landed its pull requests show as one card, Landed on run 4.
A finished train that landed nothing and was not retried shows once in the collapsed Train history below the columns, with its recorded reason, including a stop before any CI run.
Its card names the repository and the branch it lands on and says where the train stands in its tone, opening the train's pull request, with what happened last in its tip: CI tests #1, #2 (with the run, from the second on, and ending in again while a red run's failed checks run a second time) in blue, Landed (with the run, from the second on) in mint, the pull request that breaks CI or Train failed with why in red, and Stopped in grey.
Under that it lists each of its pull requests in train order, opening each on GitHub, with the goblin that made it, by its avatar, name and title, which wraps whole rather than being cut, its task in the goblin's tip, and where it stands, with the train's reason in its tip: Testing or Waits its turn in blue, Landed in mint, Merging main to fix a conflict or Rides the next train in grey, or Breaks CI in red.
A pull request shows its own state only when the train's status does not already say it (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "landed status is already above it"): none reads Testing while the train tests, Landed when it landed, or Breaks CI under the train's own breaks CI, so the goblin's name and title take that space.
A click on a train's card, anywhere but a link, opens its panel, as a goblin's card opens the goblin's, and the card stays outlined while the panel shows.
The panel has the train's mark, the repository and branch and where the train stands, and the train's pull request one tap away.
Each pull request has one primary row with its goblin, a link to the pull request, and its current run state linking to that run, with the goblin's task in a muted line under it.
A waiting, returned or conflicting pull request keeps its own state, without borrowing another pull request's run.
Attempt history is folded below when there are retries, failures or shared runs, keeping each run's actual pull requests together and preserving earlier trains and failed checks.
The panel shows the train's recorded reason as text, and folded earlier attempts retain their own reasons beside their runs, including an attempt with no run.
Each pull request's recorded reason is readable in its primary row, and earlier attempts keep their pull requests' reasons in history even when a later attempt landed them.
A red run opens its first failed check, and any other run opens the pull request it was tested on.
A run that lands merges its train's pull request, so a train that was halved has a pull request for each half, and its card opens the newest.
On GitHub a landed train's pull request reads merged, as each pull request it landed does, and only a train that did not land its last run leaves one closed, saying why ([Merge trains](contract/delivery.md#merge-trains)).
A train's pull request wears the `merge-train` label, which a release's generated notes leave out, so the lines of what is new on the [Update item](#update-code-goblins) name the pull requests a train landed and never the train.
A check can fail by chance, so a red run's failed checks run again once before the train acts on it, and such a run tells the whole of it: Trying again in blue while they run, then Landed on the second try in mint or Failed twice in red, and under its line Failed once with each check that was red on the first try, a chip in red that opens that try.
A clock line says when the batch started and finished.
The panel has no Terminal or Task switch, no explanatory lines and no yellow, and Back returns to the CEO.
A pull request under test reads one state with one link on every surface (`frontend/src/pull-request-test.ts`).
On a train, its goblin's pull request chip in the panel says what the train's card says of it, in the same tone, and opens the train's pull request: the running train that carries it, else the newest finished one while the pull request's head is still the one that rode.
Off a train, the chip is its hosted checks above.
A live goblin idle since it reported the pull request done, which waits only on that test, reads Landing as [above](#board-and-orchestration), in the test's tone, or the test's own words once it failed, was cancelled or landed, as its status on its card, in its panel's Task view with no line under it, and on its node on the Orchestration canvas, whose tip is its card's.
Paused so, its terminal view says the same words in place of Session paused.
A goblin at work, blocked, asking or waiting on something else says that itself, and only its chip reads the test.
The supervisor keeps the trains its CI poll reads, so a card follows its train within one poll.
In a repository other people work in, that row also carries the task's ticket, GitHub's issue mark in the color of where it stands and its number, opening the issue, and the avatar of each teammate whose open work meets a live goblin's branch, ringed in amber, opening that work, with who and what in its tip.
The goblin's panel shows its project's active people beside the project's name: those who committed to its default branch or opened a pull request in it in the last 30 days, so a person who only opened an issue or pushed a branch is left out, and a project with none shows no people.
They are at most five overlapping GitHub avatars, each opening their profile and named to a screen reader, whoever has work in the goblin's area first and ringed, with a muted line under the row saying who and on what, then a +N that opens a list of every one of them by their whole name, which Escape closes, handing focus back to +N, as does a press elsewhere.
The panel puts the ticket beside the pull request.
Avatars come only from GitHub's avatar host, the one image source outside the board its content policy allows; one that does not load shows the person's initial.
Every goblin gets a fun first name and title once its task is queued ([Goblin names](contract/dispatch.md#goblin-names)), which the snapshot carries as `goblin_name` and `goblin_title` on its task and as `goblin` and `goblin_title` on each merge train car, and the Orchestration canvas, its family tree and its phone list name goblins the same way, with the task in a canvas card's tip.
The title is the task's short title: a queued task's is its backlog row's, and `goblins spawn` keeps the one given with `--title`, else the row's cut to 100 characters with no harness named at its end, on the task as `title=` in its metadata so it outlives the row; `goblins title` changes it there, and a running task's recorded title wins over its row's.
A task with neither is named by its id.
The status line and the pull request come from the current generation's lines only, so a respawned task id shows neither its earlier generation's activity nor its pull request until it reports again.
Cards state progress in plain words, never engine words: Queued, Starting, Start failed, Working, In review gate (with its step, such as In review gate: tests), In line for the machine (with its place), Waiting on you, Waiting on the CEO, Waiting on a goblin by its name, Waiting on CI, Waiting on its deploy, Waiting on its gate, Landing on a train, Landing, waits to merge, Checks passed, Delivered and No fresh evidence.
`taskStatus` in `frontend/src/task-status.ts` is the one mapping from a goblin's phase, wait, pause and gate line to these words, for its card, its panel, its terminal pane and its canvas node.
A goblin waiting on another goblin names it once, in its card's status line, and links to it from its panel: a button beside its status there opens the goblin it waits on, and Orchestration draws a dashed line from the waiting card to that goblin's card, apart from the family tree.
A wait a goblin reported says what it does until it takes a prompt after that report: while the turn that prompt started runs it reads Working, its own tool calls in the turn that filed the wait end nothing, and once that turn ends with no newer report the wait stands again.
The prompt is proven by the harness's native hooks; a goblin whose harness reports none keeps its report.
A delivered goblin is at work again only on a prompt taken after its latest done report, and then its newer reports say what it does.
A question or a failure a goblin reported is its state until the goblin takes a prompt after it, as an answer that reached it, a steer or an instruction an Enter submitted, and a failure also while its terminal shows its harness at work since.
An answer on record or an acknowledgement resumes nothing, since an answer can be to stay held, and a hold its own turn is still finishing stays a hold; the line stays in the goblin's history.
A gate run that finished, passed, failed, cancelled or interrupted, holds no step and asks for no decision, so it supplies no current In review gate or Waiting status; the card still shows work or an independent wait reported by the goblin.
A gate run on the task's own branch that waits on a decision holds the card at Blocked, under that run's own pull request or none, only while the goblin has reported nothing since the run parked.
One that parked before the goblin's newest standing report leaves the card to that report: the task's snapshot entry carries it in `parked_runs`, and the CEO is woken once for it with a `parked_run` check wake naming the task, branch, run, step and when it parked, and how to act on it.
A run waiting on a decision on the branch of one of the task's extra worktrees never sets the task's phase either: it is an entry of `parked_runs` too.
Each entry names its `branch`, `run_id`, `step`, `parked_at`, when the step last moved, and its run's own `pr`, never the task's, and the card names each by its branch and step on its activity line.
A native session of an earlier generation, such as the Pi session of a goblin since switched to a harness whose hooks are not installed, is no evidence of the current one, and the card reads the goblin's gate and terminal instead.
Goblins whose standing waits on one another close a circle each read Waiting, with the reason naming the goblin whose wait comes back to it and saying neither can go on as reported, and the CEO is woken once for each circle with a `wait_cycle` notify naming every goblin in it; a newer report of any of them is a new circle.
A goblin that waits on the Overlord shows Waiting on you while its wait, or a question of its, is an item under Waiting on you in his Command Center, as that list's heading reads, and Waiting on the CEO otherwise, since the CEO carries every other question to him.
A goblin that asked a question, or whose session sits idle with nothing in the gate line, shows Waiting on the CEO, or a helper Waiting on its parent by name, since that is who answers it.
A goblin whose last reply asked the CEO something in prose, which the monitor reads and wakes the CEO with as `goblin_asks`, shows Waiting on the CEO with that question, as a goblin that asked with `goblins notify --blocked` does, until it reports again or the CEO acks the wake.
A reported wait on the Overlord ends once the Command Center item it raised closes: his answer reached the goblin, the item was cleared, or the answer on its page went to the CEO to relay; the card then shows what the goblin is doing, and an answer still on its way keeps the wait.
It keeps its phase's colour, without the amber emphasis that belongs to the CEO's bar, so a wait on the Overlord, a goblin, CI or a deploy is shown in the same calmer sand colour.
The CEO is pinned above the Board's columns in a plain bar, a card like the columns under it: the CEO's portrait, a line that says how many goblins the CEO supervises, and one icon button that opens it.
With nothing waiting on the Overlord the line opens with All quiet, and the bar has no Open Command Center.
While something in the Command Center waits on him the bar says none of what it is: Open Command Center appears on it beside the terminal icon, glows in the amber of waiting and carries how many items wait, and it opens the Command Center on the first of them.
The glow breathes, and holds still for a reader who asked for reduced motion.
While no CEO runs the bar says so and offers Start the CEO instead.
That button and its portrait open the CEO in the saved chat style and hand it the keyboard: its chat under the message icon in Modern where the CEO has messages to show, and its terminal under the terminal icon otherwise.
The bar draws no mark of the harness the CEO runs beside its portrait (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "remove the redundant claude or codex or pi icons in cfo banner in top left of board, only need usage").
Each allowance dial on it carries its provider's icon inside its ring and no name beside it, as on the CEO's card on the canvas: the Overlord asked on 2026-11-02 for the icon in the circle in place of the Claude and Codex labels.
A screen reader still hears each dial by its provider's name.
While [AFK mode](#afk-mode) is on nothing glows on the bar whatever waits on the Overlord, and its line says since when it is on and who turned it on, the Overlord from his board or his terminal or the CEO at his ask, and how many decisions the CEO logged.
AFK mode is complete autopilot, so the bar lists nothing held for him: Open Command Center, unlit, with how many wait, is its one way to whatever is there, and it is not there while nothing waits.
A click or key on the board after five minutes with none, counted from when AFK mode turned on, is taken for the Overlord coming back: the click or key does what he meant, and the board then offers to turn AFK mode off or stay.
When the CEO turned it on at his ask, his first click or key since the switch brings a dialog at once, titled The CEO turned AFK on, so a switch made on his words meets him before anything else.
He has just asked for AFK, so one short line says the CEO did it at his ask and when, its main button, Got it, keeps AFK on and closes it, and Turn AFK off is the lesser button beside it.
His next click waits five minutes as usual.
The offer after he was away says who turned it on, with his words when the CEO did at his ask, and how many decisions the CEO made once it made any.
Either one takes the focus itself, so keys he was typing press neither button, and Escape stays.
A switch the supervisor refuses there shows one short sentence that says what to do, never the supervisor's own words for why.
When AFK mode turns off nothing opens over the board: the stretch becomes his page of it, one card in his Command Center ([AFK mode](#afk-mode)).
See [AFK mode in the README](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/README.md#afk-mode) for what the page shows.
Selecting a card or node opens the same goblin panel from either view: a one-row header with the goblin and icon actions, then one view with no tabs, the Overlord's ask of 2026-10-23: the goblin's messages or its live terminal, with its task list beside them on a panel 900 px wide or more, and folded under the header as Task on a narrower one or a small screen ([The Messages view](#the-messages-view)).
The CEO's bar carries the toggle of AFK mode right beside the button that opens the CEO, in either form of the bar, and the CEO's card carries it there too: a small switch labelled AFK, in the board's green while it is on.
A bar that says the CEO is closed or not running keeps Reopen or Start the CEO as its one action, with the switch beside it only while AFK mode is not off, since no panel is left to turn it off from, or while his press still has an answer to show.
The board keeps one switch's state, so the turn-on question, his press and its refusal outlast a change of the bar's form, a change of view and a CEO that stops: the question stays open, the spinner keeps turning, and a refusal shows on whichever switch is drawn next.
The turn-on question focuses Cancel, and a switch that cannot be read shows off, with Unreadable: a press resets it in muted words beside it, which wrap where the row is narrow and take a line of their own above the switch on the canvas's card, so the row keeps the switch and its button inside the bar or card.
The [README](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/README.md#afk-mode) describes the switch's loading and refusal displays; a refusal is drawn under the switch, over the board, so it neither widens the bar nor moves the board down.
The name wraps at the edge of its own part of the panel's row, which never narrows past the session's picture, so it is never drawn under the Chat and Task switch.
While AFK mode is off and his page of the last stretch is in his Command Center, **Open your last AFK report** in the CEO's Task view opens that page again, from the snapshot's `afk.page`.
It has as much room under it, before Workspace's rule, as over it, under the header's, and with no report to open the details over Workspace take no room (#2438).
The status at the top of the panel's Task view is the one place the panel says the task's state (`frontend/src/task-words.ts` translates what the fleet wrote, at display time, and changes no record).
A resume or stop whose lifecycle record failed reads Resume failed or Stop failed, whatever the goblin's own last report says, on its card, node and panel alike.
A failed resume whose goblin then reports from the session the resume started, working, waiting, asking or done, took its instruction after all: the supervisor's next reading finishes its record as running, under the task's lifecycle lock, so the card reads what the goblin reports.
A pause whose lifecycle record failed shows what the goblin is doing, with no state of its own, and one whose native terminal no longer runs is served as paused, in the Paused section, and resumes (the Overlord, 2026-10-08, of a yellow Pause did not finish on Shirley's card).
The details at the top of the Task view show one plain sentence under the status: the goblin's latest report without its leading state word (working:, waiting on ...:, blocked:, failed:, done:), split where a semicolon chained it, in sentence case, with a pull request link as PR #n, other links as their site, a path as the name it ends with and a commit hash left out, kept to whole sentences up to 280 characters.
A paused task has no sentence, since its status already says why it waits and what resumes it (the Overlord, 2026-10-09: "in paused goblin panels the highlight line, it's not needed to be presented").
Its Details is one description written for a person, in the panel's own type and not a terminal's: its goblin's latest own report (`last_report`, never a lifecycle line or its terminal's screen) as that sentence would say it, then what it waits for and that it resumes by itself, or that it stays paused until he resumes it, then any program Windows still closes, by name (the Overlord, 2026-10-09: "every time I look at the details it is the same text ... It should just be a well written, proper case, human readable little description about what that agent did or is doing").
What a pause could not do, such as a handoff it did not save, is never in the panel: the CEO hears it in the pause's `lifecycle-<phase>` report.
A goblin paused for its own pull request, which the board shows under test, says only what it did.
Under Working and any failure the status shows no sentence, at the Overlord's word on 2026-10-05 ("dont need text under working", "dont need text under pause fialed") and 2026-10-08 ("everything error wise goes to cfo"); that sentence is the first line behind Details, the same at every width, and a failure keeps Open the log, which opens and shows Activity.
For any other task, Details under the sentence shows the report or the supervisor's problems exactly as written, each address in them a link as [The Messages view](#the-messages-view) describes; a read that fails shows Retry, and the CEO hears what could not be read.
A panel's header is one short row, so the conversation gets the room (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "Minimize height and maximize the space"): the session's small picture and its name, then the Chat and Task switch, then the panel's controls with Close or Back.
While the Chat view shows a conversation both styles can draw, the picture, the CEO's and every goblin's, is a 44 px button named Chat style whose menu offers Modern chat and Terminal, the style shown checked (the Overlord, 2026-11-03: "when you click on cfo avatar the dropdown with both options"; #1987).
The panel's controls are Open in Windows Terminal and Maximize, each a 44 px icon with its accessible name and no tip, or an item of More where the row has no room, least needed first (#1987); a phone shows neither, since it has no Windows Terminal to open (#2222).
The supervisor's panel and every label the board writes for that role name it CEO (#2222); the `goblins` executable, its commands and its environment names keep theirs, and words the supervisor or the server wrote, such as a push notification or a run's reason, are shown as they came.
The Chat and Task switch's icons are 44 px targets too, and Chat is the message bubble, as Modern chat is in the style menu (the Overlord, 2026-11-04: "I'd rather you have them be the message icon that we had before").
The name wraps whole onto more lines in a taller header when the row is narrow, never cut, at a phone's width and the desktop's (2026-11-04), and the Task view's details open with the goblin's whole name and task id, then its harness with the model and effort it runs, so none of them needs a tip.
The CEO's Task view names no harness there, since its Harness section does (the Overlord, 2026-11-12: "we don't need the Claude code . Fable 5.2", #2438).
Everything else the header used to hold is in those details at the top of the Task view: the goblin's status and the one sentence under it, its project and people, and its buttons, which wrap onto rows of their own when they do not fit.
The task list holds the workspace, harness, changes, activity and commit history, each a section closed until it is opened, and one Connectors line that opens the Connectors page, beside the goblin's messages or its live native terminal.
Its header details keep the link to a goblin this one waits on, both beside the conversation and in the Task fold.
A tall Task fold scrolls within the panel while the messages or terminal below it keep room.
The same task-list instance moves between placements, so unsaved harness choices and nested disclosures survive resizing, maximizing and closing the outer Task fold.
Under those details the task list has one action row, the task's own controls as labelled buttons, the only place they are: Start and Remove for a queued task, and Pause and Stop for a task that has started, or Resume and Stop for a paused one; a waiting goblin offers no Resume, and a completed one none of them.
A queued task is edited at the top of its Task view, where its task line carries a small pencil named Edit task (the Overlord, 2026-10-10: "I get editing pencil a little icon"): the pencil, or a click on the task's text, turns that line into a box in the same type holding the task's title and detail, which grows with its text.
The pencil is the one button on that line, for the keyboard and a screen reader, and closing the box hands the keyboard back to it.
Enter or the check saves it through `POST /api/tasks/adjust` on the revision the box opened on, Shift+Enter adds a line, and Escape or the cross puts the line back unchanged.
While it saves the box shows that it is busy, and a refused save keeps the box and its text and says why under it for a moment, on the board's shared notice.
Nothing follows the action row for it, and the card's Adjust pencil opens the panel.
A pause or stop lists what it kept under What’s preserved, each as a short label without its path or hash, and what it ended under Stopped resources; it repeats neither the status nor its reason.
Whatever opens and closes on the board is the one disclosure (`frontend/src/Disclosure.tsx`), which starts closed and turns the panel's thin chevron, never the browser's own triangle: Stopped resources, Details under a status, a goblin's finished children and the Update card's Output, the same as Workspace (the Overlord, 2026-10-09: "when output > should match task panel arrows in ui meaning the caret").
The Command Center's button is a menu behind its own icon, and `frontend/src/disclosure.test.ts` names any other `details` the source draws.
A caret only ever opens or closes what it stands before: the same test names every caret the source draws, which are the disclosure's, a tree's fold on a button that says whether it is open, and the pager's two arrows.
The panel sends no note to the CEO: the supervisor still accepts the `note` action of `POST /api/tasks/adjust`, but nothing on the board files or shows one.
The CEO's task list holds its workspace and harness and then every queued task, the same list as the Tasks column, in the same order, with the same drag, keyboard moves and Start; an order or a start made in either shows in both.
The machine meters are drawn once on screen (#1598): at the head of the Tasks column while the board shows it, and over the CEO's queued tasks only where no Tasks column shows, as in Orchestration, beside a maximized panel and on a phone the panel fills.
The list carries no heading there, since the column whose heading it would be is not in the panel.
Board and Orchestration both open a goblin, and the CEO, on its one view, a Claude Code session's showing its messages ([The Messages view](#the-messages-view)); Orchestration's view defaults to the registered CEO; once opened, both views stay mounted, so switching keeps scroll position and selection.
A Claude Code session's messages stay mounted too, from the first showing of its conversation or of the box it holds while its panel shows the session; a Classic terminal that shows neither loads no messages.
Typing happens in the terminal itself, or in a Claude Code session's message box, which types into it.
Where a goblin's terminal cannot take typing, for a goblin paused, resuming or starting, a session the board does not read as messages has a message box under its terminal's place instead, the same box the messages have: Send or Enter sends a `message` action through `POST /api/actions` with the task's id, and the supervisor delivers it once whatever the goblin is doing.
While no CEO runs, closed or between the two terminals of a restart, the CEO's slot says why it has no terminal and has that same box under it, since a message starts it (the Overlord, 2026-11-04: "The modern chat should. be its own trigger to start any code session.").
Holding the box's microphone, or Ctrl+Shift+Space in the box, dictates into it with the terminals' own dictation, on the supervisor's speech model, for as long as it is held.
A paused goblin's message joins its resume note and asks the Resume his click asks, so its resume prompt gives the session his words as its first turn, and a goblin pausing, resuming or starting has it wait, then typed into its terminal.
A message to a goblin whose terminal is not running starts it, as the comeback brings a goblin back after a restart, on its own conversation and told that his message follows, and is typed in once its terminal runs (the Overlord, 2026-11-04: "The modern chat should. be its own trigger to start any code session. Especially on a message sent.").
The session is told first, by its resume instruction, and then his words are typed, so the message stays queued until it is really typed and can be neither lost nor falsely read Sent (the CEO, decision 1016436).
No message to a goblin ages while a message's own start of it runs, since that start told the goblin one follows, so one a gate held past ten minutes still goes in, and how the start ends decides where it goes.
A goblin whose failed pause took hold is paused, and a message wakes it by a Resume, told as any started goblin is, so its record reads running and the message reads Starting until it is typed; a restart leaves such a goblin as it was.
While it starts, and while any start of that goblin is under way, the message reads `Starting <task>. The message goes in once it runs.`, and Send now's reason reads `It is starting.` (`message_starts.go` in `internal/supervisor`).
That holds until the start returns, however its terminal reads, since a terminal comes up before its start has told its harness anything, and nothing is typed into a session whose own start is still briefing it.
Each message starts its goblin once, one start at a time as every start goes, and a Resume, a switch or the comeback's start of that goblin already under way is waited on rather than made again.
A goblin whose latest report delivered its pull request comes back for his message too, since the message prompts it, while one he stopped stays stopped and its message goes to the CEO.
One the machine has no room for tries again every 30 seconds until ten minutes have passed, and one that could not start has its message go to the CEO with its words and why.
A goblin in a turn has his words typed into that turn at once, as the CEO's are, for its harness to take at its next tool call; the [message box](#the-message-box) describes the delivery labels.
A message held until the turn ended reached a goblin that worked on only once it stopped, and never while each turn ran into the next (the Overlord, 2026-11-04: "Modern shat goblins that are not the CFO. Never pick up their cube message when I send them.").
A message that waits behind a command held for the turn has Send now, which types it into the turn and has the harness take it at once, as its own send-now key does in its terminal (the Overlord, 2026-10-30: "when sent now it actually sends now same way termianl expereince down with ctrl x crtl s").
That key is Claude Code's Ctrl+X Ctrl+S (`chat:sendNow`), its two keys pressed 50 ms apart as fingers press them, once its prompt line shows the message gone into its queue; Codex's is the Escape its working row names, "esc to interrupt"; and pi's is the Escape it binds to `app.interrupt`, which ends the run, keeps the session, and returns the lines its editor queued to the editor, where `submitHandedBack` submits them as the session's next prompt.
A harness with no such key would take a line typed during a turn only at its next tool call.
Such a message reads `Queued until <task>'s turn ends. Send now has it take the message at once.`, and once the key has gone, `Typed while it was working and sent now.`; one whose key never went, as when a screen of the harness's own stayed up, says the goblin takes it at its next tool call.
A command its harness would queue waits for the turn to end, reading `Queued. A command runs when its turn ends. Send now interrupts the turn to run it.`, and each press of its Send now tries once to run it at once with that same key (#1915).
Claude Code takes the command into its turn's queue, so the board types it there and presses Ctrl+X Ctrl+S once the prompt line shows it gone into that queue with no screen of the harness's own open over it, and the command reads delivered once that line shows it gone with no queue offered on two reads in a row (`spawn.RanNow`).
Seen live on Claude Code 2.1.323 on 2026-11-13, a slash command ran as a turn of its own while the step under way went on in the background, and the session then went on with its turn.
A bang command instead interrupted the turn, cancelling the step under way, and the session then waited to be told to go on, as a Codex or a pi does once the Escape has ended its turn.
That key takes the queue from its head, so it goes only while the queue holds nothing but the command: a queue that already held a line typed in the terminal keeps the command held.
Codex refuses a command while a task runs, and pi expands one into a steer, so there the board first presses that Escape once, over an empty prompt line with no screen of the harness's own open, and types the command at the empty, ready prompt line the turn's end leaves, read so on two reads in a row within five seconds, where it goes as at any idle prompt line (`spawn.InterruptTurn`).
A turn that has not ended by then, as when a Codex's own queue starts a turn of its own or a pi's comes back onto its prompt line, keeps the command held.
A Send now that could not run the command is spent, so no later turn is interrupted for it, and the command reads `Queued. Send now could not run it at once, so it runs when its turn ends.`
Those words stay until the command runs or he presses Send now again, beside the board's Send now, which stays pressed until the command leaves the queue (`IsSendNowSpent` holds them).
A new Send now reads `Queued. A command runs when its turn ends. Send now interrupts the turn to run it.` again until its own try says how it went.
Another reason the command cannot go now, such as words left on the CEO's prompt line, shows in their place while that reason lasts, and they come back once the command waits for its turn with Send now on again.
A command its harness runs at once beside a turn waits for nothing, since he types it into a turn in the terminal and it runs there (the Overlord, 2026-11-11: "cant type / commands into modern chat like terminal", #2372).
Each harness has its own list of those (`harness.RunsBesideTurn`): Claude Code 2.1.322's `/model`, `/status` and `/remote-control`, Codex 0.160's `/status` and `/diff` and pi 1.4's `/compact` and `/model` are among them, and `/compact` in Claude Code and Codex is not.
The board types such a command into the turn and it reads delivered once the prompt line shows it gone on two reads in a row with no queue newly offered there, since it starts no turn and can leave no record (`spawn.RanBesideTurn`).
One its harness queued after all, its prompt line then offering its queue, reads queued in the session, as his words there do.
Claude Code runs `/diff`, `/help` and `/theme`, and `/add-dir`, `/advisor` and `/config` with nothing after them, at once only in its fullscreen interface, which the board cannot tell from the other, so those wait for the turn's end.
A slash command a harness runs itself starts no turn, and Codex and pi record nothing of it, so a goblin with no turn running took one once its screen shows it gone from the input line, the composer empty or the command's own panel open (`Screens.TookCommand`), and the message reads as delivered (#2193).
A command whose screen never shows it gone, such as `/exit`, which ends the harness, is still reported unconfirmed.
A prompt line holding words that were never sent takes nothing, since what was typed would join them, so the message stays queued until the line is empty.
A delivery that typed a message and ended before its harness took the submit key leaves none of it typed for the next message to wait behind (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "queued messages in modern chat get stuck in input box in terminal chat and never send", #2306).
While those words, or a part of them, are all the prompt line holds, it presses the harness's clear-line key, Ctrl+U (`harness.ClearLine`), as often as a harness that gives up one row at a time needs, and once it reads the line empty the message is not sent: `failed`, with a reason that starts `Not sent` (`spawn.TakeBack`).
It presses nothing over a prompt line that holds anything else, his words beside them or his typed as the line cleared, which stay his (`Screens.ComposerHoldsPartOf`).
It presses nothing for words the screen does not show, since a harness that has not read them yet can take a key typed behind them as part of them, as Claude Code 2.1.322 did live on 2026-11-10 with words longer than a row and with a paste, and nothing while a dialog shows, which would take the key as its own.
Such words are remembered with the message (`left_typed`), which reads unconfirmed, and each supervisor cycle looks for them (`takeBackLeftTyped`): once the prompt line holds them and nothing else they are taken back the same way and the message reads `Not sent`, so the messages after it go.
Words of his own beside them leave the prompt line his, and words whose terminal is gone or was started again since, or that never showed within a day, are watched no longer.
A message that was not sent is still the board's: its Send now queues that same message to go at once, and its Cancel takes it off the board.
A message typed into a turn, a goblin's or the CEO's, waits in its harness's own queue until that turn takes it, so from the moment its submit key goes it reads Queued, saying `Queued in the goblin's session. The goblin takes it at its next tool call, or as its current turn ends.` or the same of the CEO, and it reads Sent only once its session's record shows the turn took it (the Overlord, 2026-11-10, of three messages that read Sent for minutes while the CEO's harness held them: such a message "must read Queued, be editable and deletable, and Send now must interrupt and deliver it", #2332).
Its action says so as `awaiting.is_held`, and its three controls act on that queue with the harness's own keys, each only after the screen shows the state its key needs (`internal/supervisor/harness_queue.go`).
Send now presses the harness's send-now key once for each press of his, over a turn under way whose prompt line is empty and, where the harness draws it, still offers its queue (`sendHeldNow`).
It presses nothing once the session's record shows the message taken, once no turn is left to interrupt, or once the queue is given up, since the key would then interrupt the turn that reads the message; the message then reads `Typed while it was working and sent now.` and stays Queued, its Send now ready again, for as long as its harness still holds it.
Edit and Cancel first read the session's record, since a press can land before the supervisor's next look at it: a message its turn already took reads Sent at once, the press says `The CEO already took it.`, the goblin's name in place of the CEO for a goblin's, and no key goes to a session for a queue it no longer holds (#1915).
A harness's queue lasts only as long as its terminal, so a message held in one that has ended or was started again since, the CEO's after a restart of the machine as a goblin's after a switch or a resume, reached nobody: it reads `Not sent. It waited in the queue of the CEO's terminal, which ended or started again before taking it.`, or the same of the goblin's, Cancel takes it off the board, Send now types it afresh, and no key goes to the new terminal for a queue it never held (the Overlord, 2026-11-13, of such a message his Cancel could not clear: "clear out this garbage message", #2541).
Otherwise they take the message back out of that queue where the harness has a key for it, as Claude Code 2.1.322 has: Up at the empty prompt line that reads "Press up to edit queued messages" puts every line it holds on the prompt line, a row each (`Screens.EditQueue` and `Screens.OffersQueue`, live on 2026-11-10).
A harness gives its queue back only whole, so the board presses that key, waits up to five seconds to read on the prompt line exactly the lines it typed into that queue, takes them off unsubmitted with the clear-line key, deletes the message he named, and queues the others to be typed into the turn again in the order he sent them (`takeBackHeld`).
It clears nothing it did not type: a queue that also held a line typed in the terminal comes onto the prompt line with the board's lines and stays there, his to send or clear, and those messages read unconfirmed and are watched there as words left typed are.
It presses nothing while the prompt line does not offer the queue, as while it holds a draft of his, or while the worker runs a delivery to that session, which may be typing onto that line, and says to press again in a moment.
A queue that also holds an answer the board typed, and one whose harness gives it back to no key, as Codex's and pi's do, refuse Edit and Cancel in those words, and Send now still has the message taken at once.
A press on a message whose delivery still waits to see it taken acts once that wait ends (`awaitTry`).
Claude Code records a queue taken back as one `popAll` queue operation, which ends the wait of every line it held, so none reads waiting once it is back on the prompt line (`internal/fleettree/messages.go`).
Messages to one session go one at a time, in the order he sent them, each once the one before it was handed over, unless he sends one now.
A goblin whose resume failed has the message join its resume note the same way while that resume is its session's own or the task's interrupted resume, which a Resume takes up again.
A lifecycle record an earlier session left decides nothing: once a later start, such as a `goblins switch`, replaced a failed resume's session, no Resume of that resume can run, so the message goes to the goblin as to any other, typed into its terminal once that terminal is up.
A `message` action that names no task is for the CEO: while no CEO runs it starts one at once, without its undo window, as the supervisor brings back a CEO whose terminal ended ([A CEO whose terminal ended](#a-ceo-whose-terminal-ended)), told that his message follows, and reads `Starting the CEO. The message goes in once it runs.` while that or any other start of the CEO is under way.
It too is told first and his words are typed after, and a CEO that registered while the supervisor still brings it back reads Starting until that start returns.
A CEO that did not start for it leaves the message waiting, saying why, for a CEO started another way, such as Reopen the CEO.
It goes to whichever CEO runs once its input is ready, exactly as he typed it, a turn under way or not, since the CEO's harness takes a line typed during a turn at its next step, as it takes one typed into its terminal by hand (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "It should just be a direct message that gets queued up and sent with the clawed code session. The terminal already handles this.").
It first waits ten seconds on the board (`ceoMessageUndo` in `internal/supervisor/ceo_follow.go`), where Edit and Cancel still take it back, and is then typed into the CEO's turn; Send now skips that wait, types it into the turn and presses the harness's own send-now key once its own input line shows the message gone into its queue with no screen of its own open over it, which interrupts that turn and has the CEO take the message at once (the CEO, 2026-11-03, withdrawing the 2026-11-02 rule that interrupting stays the box's Stop).
No key is pressed when the CEO's own record already shows it took the message, or when that input line has not shown within five seconds, and the message then reads as an ordinary send does.
A command, a line starting with `/` or `!`, never waits in that window, and his messages behind a held one wait for it, so they still go in the order he sent them.
An idle CEO took a slash command once its screen shows it gone from the input line, as a goblin did.
A command for a CEO in a turn that its harness would queue waits on the board until that turn ends, reading `Queued. A command runs when its turn ends. Send now interrupts the turn to run it.`, and its Send now runs it at once as a goblin's does (#1915).
Claude Code's then reads `Taken by the CEO in its native terminal, which ran your command at once.`, and a Codex's or a pi's goes, once its turn ended, as it does to an idle CEO.
One its harness runs at once beside a turn is typed into the turn and reads `Taken by the CEO in its native terminal, which ran your command beside its turn.`, as a goblin's does (#2372).
Codex and pi record no command and could drop one typed into a turn with nothing to show it (#2193).
Claude Code queues one ahead of what is typed after it, and its send-now key then takes that command alone, as a turn of its own that leaves the message behind it queued: Send now on his message behind three such commands in the CEO's queue left it there on 2026-11-10 (#2332).
A message behind a held command reads `Queued until the CEO's turn ends. Send now has it take the message at once.`, and its Send now types it into the turn ahead of the command and presses the send-now key over it alone.
Claude Code records a command it ran itself as a user message holding its tags, as a system `local_command` record (2.1.309), or, for `/remote-control`, only as a system `bridge_status` record that opens with the command, as "/remote-control is active · Continue here, …" (2.1.304 to 2.1.319).
The session's messages read each as the command's slash row, with what it printed as its output, and the CEO's or goblin's record proves the command taken by the same records (`handedText` in `internal/monitor/took.go`), so the message takes its place in the conversation rather than staying Sent under it (#2193).
A Codex or pi command, which leaves no row, is drawn by the board itself as a slash row where it was taken, once the board saw its harness take it (`withTakenCommands` in `frontend/src/messages.ts`).
One taken before the conversation's first loaded row is not drawn, since it may belong to an older page or an earlier session, so an empty conversation draws none.
A Claude Code turn he stops puts each line typed into it but not yet taken back on its prompt line, so the supervisor submits them there as the session's next prompt, a goblin's as the CEO's, once the screen shows the harness ready with nothing else on its prompt line, whitespace aside: the words of a line it typed, several such lines joined in the order they were typed, or, for a line it pasted, a paste's placeholder alone (`Screens.ComposerMatches` and `Screens.ComposerHoldsOnePaste`).
Words or a paste of his before or after them, even words that end as a lone `?` does, leave the prompt line his to send, and nothing is submitted.
A Claude Code CEO starts with Claude Code's prompt suggestions off, as a goblin does: a suggestion is drawn on the empty input line as each turn ends, which the board reads as words left unsent, so until 2026-10-28 none of his messages went in while the CEO sat idle.
One that waits ten minutes, or whose goblin is stopped first, goes to the CEO with its words, while a goblin that ended is started for it.
Each message shows under the box with where it is: Queued, Starting, Kept for resume, Sent or With the CEO.
A message a paused goblin's resume will carry shows Kept for resume, or Starting while that resume waits its turn or runs, read from its resume note, which the task's lifecycle names in `kept_messages` and which outlasts the message's own action, and it leaves the list once a resume carried it.
A failed resume that the supervisor finishes as running once its goblin reports carried only the messages kept before it began, so each message kept for it goes to the CEO with its words and the resume's start.
So does each message kept for a failed resume whose relaunched terminal a Resume finds already up: that Resume relaunches nothing, so it hands them to the CEO before it writes its own record over the failed one.
A message kept for a resume whose session a later start, such as a `goblins switch`, then replaced, whether kept before that start or while it ran, goes to the CEO with its words within a minute, since no resume of that record runs again, and a failed resume's also names that resume's start.
A pause or stop of the later session that comes first hands such messages to the CEO before it writes its own record over the one that kept them.
A stop of a paused goblin, or of one whose resume failed, ends it before that resume runs, so it hands each message kept for the resume to the CEO with its words before it writes its own record, which keeps no note, and a failed resume's also names that resume's start.
So does a `goblins cleanup` that retires such a goblin, once every check it makes has passed and before it changes anything, since no reading looks at a retired task's record again: it takes the messages out of the note, so none is handed on twice, and a message it cannot put on the CEO's notify wake refuses the cleanup with the task as it was.
A record that cannot be read refuses the cleanup, `--force-archive` too, before anything changes, since the gate run a pause saves in it cannot be read either ([goblins cleanup](commands/cleanup.md)); what a hand-off met after the messages went to the CEO is only a warning in its output.
A pause of a goblin whose resume failed hands each message kept for that resume to the CEO the same way, with that resume's start, before it writes its own record, since that record replaces the failed resume's, which then never runs again; a paused goblin paused again keeps its note for its resume.
Such a message has a delete button, which sends a `message_withdraw` action naming the goblin and the message's words through `POST /api/actions`: the supervisor takes it out of the resume note under the goblin's lifecycle lock, so the resume never carries it, and the message leaves the list.
A message typed into its terminal, carried by a resume or unknown is refused.
The board's own clicks show at once: a Start, Pause, Resume or Stop draws its task as Starting, Pausing, Resuming or Stopping in the frame it is clicked, until a snapshot from the revision the supervisor answered with shows the task, and a double click sends one request.
Open in VS Code and Open folder require a deliberate click and resolve the selected goblin's fresh, isolated Git worktree; they show for a live goblin only, and not on a small screen (40rem or less), since both open on the PC, while its ticket, pull request, checks and deploy beside them show in a finished task's panel too.
The API accepts task identity and an editor enum, never a browser-provided path or command; it starts Code.exe directly with literal arguments and removes Electron Node/development flags from its inherited environment.
Successful launch means the application was requested, not that a window was observed.
A task reads one status, its words and the phase its dot is drawn in, on its card, its panel, its terminal pane and its canvas node, all from the same snapshot (`taskStatus` in `frontend/src/task-status.ts`), so they change together and never disagree (the Overlord, 2026-10-08: "status dots need to be better synched across pane and panels and boards"); a paused goblin reads why it waits and what resumes it on its card, its panel and its terminal pane, and its panel adds no sentence under that.
Queued tasks show their known project, without querying nonexistent task metadata, and their panel is the task list alone, and a terminal takes its place beside it once the task has one or its Start is on its way.
Operational wake records remain intact; only a deliberate CEO question is offered to the Overlord, and the Command Center opens when he chooses it.
Task-semantic goblin avatars are presentation choices, not inferred native role evidence; a task no keyword classifies gets a stable artwork of its own instead of the shared app icon.

Orchestration nodes use explicit native parent/root IDs and the launch environment's reported relationships.
The canvas and the lineage list hold only the CEO and the goblins in progress, working or waiting as the board's In progress column holds them, each read from its live task record and the session that record names as its own, with their helpers (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "it should just be for in-progress goblins to begin with").
A goblin appears there once its start is under way and leaves the moment it finishes, pauses or retires, and a paused goblin waits on the board's Paused bench.
No other session record is a card: an earlier generation's session is over, and a sub-agent's or worker's record has no terminal of its own, while its goblin's family tree draws the children still running.
The supervisor keeps a session's record after its task retires, as it last reported, since a goblin that `goblins cleanup` ends never reports its end, and until 2026-10-17 the canvas kept every session no archived record proved retired, while the board's history holds only its newest 20 finished tasks and merged pull requests, so goblins retired the day before came back under the CEO reading Turn finished or Working (`frontend/src/behavior.test.ts`, `frontend/tests/canvas-live-only.spec.ts`).
A live task record no native hook reported hangs under the CEO, because goblins spawn is how every task record was dispatched: under the reported CEO session, or under a supervisor root drawn when none reported, which opens the CEO terminal and names a stale registration.
A goblin's session hangs there too when the parent it reported is not in the tree, whoever started or resumed it, and the canvas and the lineage list draw the same family.
A goblin records the session its start explicitly names (`GOBLINS_SESSION_ID` plus `GOBLINS_SESSION_HARNESS`) as its parent, else the registered live CEO's session, else the session its start runs in (`CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` or `PI_SESSION_ID`), so a goblin the supervisor starts, resumes or switches by itself records the CEO too; a helper never takes the CEO, since its record names its goblin.
The CEO's status on the canvas and in the lineage list comes from the registered CEO's own session, which the snapshot names as `ceo_session`: an older CEO's session, ended or still marked running, never stands in for it (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "it says session ended when it's truly working") and leaves the canvas, while a goblin in progress it started hangs under the CEO; with no registration naming a session, the newest CEO session still running is read, and while the registered CEO has reported no session, or its session has ended, the canvas draws the supervisor root.
Its Task view omits the runtime status strip and, when there are no header details to show, leaves no space or divider before Workspace.
Completed history and unstarted briefs stay on the board, not in the tree.
Directory names and temporal proximity never establish parentage.
Replayed, stale, reparenting, or cyclic input cannot silently change the tree.
Task dependencies are rendered separately from spawned/delegated links.
Unreported child models remain unreported even when their owning task declares a model and effort.
A goblin's card on the canvas and in the lineage list is its board card, drawn by the same `TaskCard` from the same status, without the baby goblins the canvas draws under it: its name and title, its status, its project, its clock, its ticket, its pull request and its harness mark (`frontend/src/TaskCard.tsx`).
On the canvas, a goblin whose task names no session of its own says so beside its card: Native terminal running, session details unavailable while its native terminal reports busy, active, idle or parked, else Session details unavailable, so a terminal the board can see is never mistaken for a missing one and an unknown identity is never drawn healthy.
The CEO's card has its weekly allowance dials and the OpenRouter key's spend and the button that opens it, as the board's CEO bar has, with the brand inside each dial, and keeps its harness mark, which the bar does not wear.
On the canvas its dials wrap with gaps, Browser active takes a row of its own above them while the CEO presents a page, and below 70% zoom a card with multiple dials hides the subscription percentages and key-spend budget labels, keeping the rings and dollar amount (`frontend/tests/orchestration-card-text.spec.ts`).
Every card on the canvas, a baby goblin's included, holds all it says inside its border at any length, since each part keeps to its own lines and ends in an ellipsis past them (`frontend/tests/orchestration-card-text.spec.ts`).
