Every relaunch plans the task's MCP configuration again from the project's `.mcp.json`, for the servers the task's record names, and writes it once the old harness has stopped, so the new harness is never handed the file the last one was started with.
`goblins switch` changes a goblin's harness, model or effort in place: the harness exits on its own command, its terminal is closed if it will not, which ends it and everything it started, and the new harness starts in a new terminal under the same id with the task's credentials in its environment.
A reboot or sign-out ends every native terminal; the monitor's wake for a native task whose terminal has ended says so, and `goblins resume` brings every such task back, as `goblins switch <id>` to what it already ran (`--harness` naming its own harness) does for one, starting it again under the same id on the task's own recorded conversation (`claude --resume <session>`, `codex resume <session>` or `pi --session <session>`, never another task's latest) and tells it to continue where it left off.
A resumed conversation that comes back in a turn, its status row showing it working, has started: nothing is typed into that turn.
`goblins resume` reads every task record: it leaves a task whose lifecycle record of its generation says paused or stopped, whose failed pause took hold, or whose operation still holds its lock, and a task whose terminal still runs; it finishes a resume that was cut short; it reports a task that ran in Herdr, one whose pause or stop was cut short, and one whose resume or stop failed, for a hand; and it admits each launch as a spawn does (`supervisor.CheckLaunch` on the memory left after the launch before), so a goblin there is no room for waits with the reason and none after it starts either.
Every restarted goblin, resumed or handed off, is also told that a question it asked the CEO before the restart was cancelled with it, and to ask it again with `goblins notify --blocked` if it was waiting on an answer.
That resume in place skips the dirty-worktree refusal, since uncommitted edits are the goblin's own work in progress and nothing is stopped or handed off; a switch that changes the harness, model or effort is still refused on a dirty worktree unless `--force-dirty`.
A task an older build recorded in Herdr is refused by `goblins switch` by name, before anything is stopped or written.
A `/` or `$` command gets the completion popup's longer wait before Enter, and is delivered once a harness that ran no turn shows it working or shows it gone from its input line, its own panel, such as `/model`'s picker, open over it or not (`Screens.TookCommand`).
Otherwise it is reported submitted once but unconfirmed, as `/exit` is, since it ends the harness, and as one typed into a turn is, other than one its harness runs at once beside a turn, which reads as a board message's does (`spawn.RanBesideTurn`); check it with `goblins peek` rather than sending it again.
The monitor supervises a native goblin as it does a Herdr one: its host's record says whether its terminal runs, and the harness's own screen, read the way the spawn reads it, says whether a turn is in progress, a dialog waits on a person or the composer waits for input.
Its progress evidence is the processes its terminal's program started and the writes of its own conversation, the one its family tree reads: Claude Code's transcript, the Codex rollout or pi's session.
The board's runtime line for a native task names its terminal instead of Herdr.
`goblins cleanup` returns a native task whose terminal has ended, or whose harness waits at its ready composer with no working marker, in which case it closes the terminal, which ends the harness (decision 2339), and then ends what the goblin left running outside the terminal, by the same proof a pause uses ([Whose a process is](#whose-a-process-is)), before any worktree is returned; any other screen, a screen or host record that cannot be read, and a harness whose screens goblins cannot read are refused with or without `--force-archive`.
A terminal has ended once Windows shows its host's pid ended or free, or once a process that started after the host recorded itself holds that pid, as Windows gives an ended host's pid to a later process; a process this user may not open, such as a system service's that took the pid after a restart, is judged by the start the machine's process list gives, so a cleanup retires its task and a switch or resume brings it back (issue #1206).
It asks nothing of Herdr for a native task, so a machine without Herdr retires its native goblins; only a Herdr task needs Herdr.
The orphan sweep (`goblins reap` and the watcher's) refuses when a running Herdr server for its session cannot be read, since every goblin in it would read as an orphan, and sweeps on the native hosts and the process table alone when no Herdr server runs for its session, so no pane can exist; a test fixture's server for another session or a Herdr CLI call does not stop it.
Whose a process is, it asks where the janitor's process sweep asks it, by the terminal's mark, its task's folders and its own processes' children, so a process that sweep keeps for a terminal that runs is never an orphan here, and `goblins process-plan` shows the owner both go by.
It reads the machine twice, its list of processes and then which of them still run and whose each is, and a listed process that ended between the two is named in a note and is no finding.
On 2026-10-10 it woke the CEO twice in half an hour for a working goblin's spawn test: the three processes it recorded were 9 to 11 seconds old when that sweep began, a spawn test lasts about ten, and nothing had been read about any of them.
A missing or stale registration shows on the board as the CEO's bar saying the board cannot reach the CEO, with what went stale and the fix, `goblins register` in the CEO session, in its tip, and in the CEO terminal as its own state. The board shows no banner for it.
Each snapshot takes the registration problem from the same read of `state/primary.json` as `ceo_runs`, `ceo_starting` and `ceo_terminal`, so a CEO that was closed and opened again never shows running beside the problem of the registration it replaced; what the once-a-minute recovery cycle finds is shown only for the registration it examined.
On Windows normal message delivery holds that registration against replacement and validates the live process and start time and that the registered terminal's host still names that process as its program before it types anything.
Missing or changed identity is refused with nothing typed.
The CEO's own prompt hook establishes accepted delivery to an idle CEO, and its own record of its conversation to a CEO in a turn; the current native contract cannot prove a model response or provide an atomic process-identity compare-and-send operation.
Terminal input pins the exact terminal ID and task generation, checks process ownership and pipeline custody on the schedule above, and consumes ordered input identities once.
Writing native stdin does not acknowledge application acceptance.
Herdr cannot atomically compare the foreground process while writing: if an agent exits after the check, bytes may reach the same PowerShell terminal.
Known exited/replaced sessions are refused, but the board does not claim to eliminate that native check-then-write race.

Workspace details show the working folder and Harness follows them; both are sections closed until opened, with the same header as Changes.
Harness holds every harness control: a task's Harness, Model and Effort selectors, with the real harness mark and efforts beside the model, the CEO's Restart with its harness and its own engine selector, and a child session's reported read-only engine rows.
Under Harness a running goblin's panel has one line, Connectors, which opens the Connectors page; a panel with nothing to check, such as the CEO's, a queued task's or a baby goblin's, has none, and no panel holds a connectors section of its own (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "I also don't like the connectors panel inside the task panels for every goblin"; #2303).
`GET /api/engines` reads installed, signed-in harnesses and their local model catalogs: Codex's `models_cache.json`, Claude Code's own catalog for the organization `~/.claude.json` says it is signed in to (the newest `~/.claude/cache/model-catalog/<organization>-*-cc.json`, with each model's effort options) and its `additionalModelOptionsCache`, pi's `models-store.json` and configured model in `settings.json`, plus fleet routing and the adapter's default model.
A model a harness catalog lists keeps the catalog's name and efforts; a routing lane or the default model only adds its effort as the model's default when the catalog names none, so Opus 5.5 still opens at the fleet's xhigh.
The model catalog is not a model-name list embedded in the board; each model's reasoning levels are limited to those the installed adapter accepts, and an unavailable current value remains visible with its reason.
`POST /api/tasks/engine` binds live changes to the task's spawn generation and queued changes to the backlog revision.
A queued choice changes the settings Start reads under the backlog lock; a paused choice is saved in `state/engine/<id>.json` and consumed only after Resume succeeds.
A running task's **Restart** acts at once, as the CEO's does, with no confirmation: with its selectors unchanged it restarts the goblin on its own harness, model and effort, on its conversation, through `goblins switch --restart`, and with any changed it switches onto them.
A choice sent to `POST /api/tasks/engine` with `when: turn-end` instead waits for the end of the goblin's turn: the pending choice survives a supervisor restart, is shown under its Harness, and applies after two idle empty-composer readings at least one second apart, with no running, fixing or fix-review gate step and no sub-agent, background shell or monitor of the goblin's still at work in its [family tree](#the-family-tree), since the switch ends them.
A task paused while that choice is still pending uses it on its next Resume, which removes it only after the launch succeeds.
The catalog is read again as the choice applies: a selection that is no longer available is removed, with its reason on the task's card.
An idle or gate reading that fails shows on that card until a later reading succeeds or the choice is cancelled; neither kind of failure becomes the board-wide error.
A switch from Restart interrupts the turn and a running gate step, using the existing `goblins switch` path with `--force-dirty` and `--generation`, which refuses the switch when the task's session has changed; uncommitted work stays.
The old native terminal closes, ending the processes it started, and there is no additional leftover-process refusal.
The target launch is built before the old harness stops, so an invalid launch leaves the session untouched; a failure after the stop shows the CLI's recovery reason and preserves the worktree.
A switch that keeps a running goblin's harness resumes the conversation the board recorded for its generation, or, where it recorded none, as a home without the [native hooks](#native-hook-setup) records none, the one the running harness records for itself: Claude Code's `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`, proved by its process's creation time, the Codex rollout of the goblin's worktree written last, never a child agent's, or pi's session of the goblin's worktree written last since pi started.
pi writes a session's file only when its first reply ends and exits on a `--session` it finds no file for, so a pi goblin whose session has no file yet starts cold with the handoff.
The panel keeps its live values while the switch runs, then shows the resulting session and any failure reason.
The CEO receives one digest line in the notify wake queue, never a prompt, for each saved queued or Resume choice, each switch that completed or failed, and each pending choice removed because its session changed or its selection became unavailable.
Completed outcomes record the last harness, model and effort, while older outcomes without those fields say Engine not recorded.
Connectors, the header's third view, is one page of everything the running goblins can reach, laid out as Claude's own connectors page is: a list, and a page for each connector (`frontend/src/connectors-page.tsx`, `frontend/tests/connectors-page.spec.ts`, #2303).
The list holds MCP servers, the default connectors every goblin carries, such as the [crawler](crawler.md), repository services and the CLI variables present in a goblin's launch environment, one row for each however many goblins have it, with 16px or larger text.
A row has the service's logo on a tile, a file of its own bundled with the board from `frontend/src/connector-logos` (Simple Icons marks, and Context7's own MIT icon) that loads only once its row is near the screen, or its initial where the board has none, then its name without a harness's prefix, its type and its status.
Its type says how it reaches a goblin: `MCP server`, `Claude MCP connector` or `Plugin MCP server` for an MCP server, `CLI variable` for a variable in the goblin's terminal, `Repository service` for a service the auth manifest declares, or `Built in`.
The status is one word, the one of the goblin whose check says least, so a row never reads better than any goblin's check: Verified with a check mark only when every goblin's check confirmed it, Set for a variable or a service whose credentials resolve and that no check confirms, Installed for the crawler on a goblin's PATH, a green Sign in button when a goblin must sign in and the board can start it, the trouble's own word when its check failed, such as Expired or Unverified, Checking while a check runs, Not checked where no check could run, and a Connect button when no goblin carries it.
Sign in and Connect each open the connector's page, where the goblin that signs in or takes it is chosen; where he must act it is a button in the board's look, never coloured words (the Overlord, 2026-11-11).
Only Verified is green and only a failed check is amber, with no warning mark (the Overlord, 2026-11-11: "clean up the sign in and amber alerts to make it much more simple and user friendly especially with verification and difference between mcp and env cli variables", #2358).
A Connect or Disconnect that waits for a goblin's next terminal says Applies at its next terminal in one quiet line under the row, and the connector's page says why.
The page says how old its oldest result is, Checked 12 minutes ago, with rechecking... while a recheck runs behind it, Checking connectors... only while a goblin has no result yet, and Check failed in its place for a check that failed, Check failed, rechecking... while a recheck runs behind it (#2394).
On a phone, what the page is doing takes a line of its own under the heading, clear of the heading and the recheck button.
Yours lists the connectors at least one goblin carries and Discover those on offer that none does, a search box narrows either by name or type, and Browser logins is a third list where a goblin's project uses one.
A row opens the connector's own page and Back returns to the list as it was left: the logo, name, type and one line on what a known service does, each goblin that can use it with its state in a word and the reason its check gave, and then what reaches a goblin through it in labelled parts.
An MCP server's part says the goblin's harness starts it and calls its tools, and holds the tools its harness reports, grouped as read-only and write, each group closed until opened.
A repository service's CLI variables part says command line tools in the goblin's terminal read them and names each variable the auth manifest declares for it, one another service shares included, with what each goblin's terminal holds of it, Set, Not set, Not given or Not checked, and a variable's own part names every service that declares it; neither shows a value.
No harness reports a permission for a tool, so the page shows none.
With no goblin running the page says so and lists nothing, since a connector is read from a goblin.
The page has the canvas to itself while no panel has anything to show, with no placeholder asking for a task beside it; a goblin's or the CEO's panel that is open stays beside it, and on a phone, or maximized, it closes when the page is chosen.
A goblin's row on that page offers Connect for a repository service the task does not carry or a project MCP server its harness was not given, and Disconnect for one the task's record names; a credential variable and an MCP server from outside the project's `.mcp.json` offer neither, and a credential variable no goblin carries has no row in the list, since its service's row offers it.
Every name wraps whole, so a long name reads in full.
A goblin whose connector is failing shows its trouble's word in amber with its reason under its name, and its repair buttons beside it, and one that must sign in shows its reason beside its green Sign in button, as Connect is green, with no word; a verified one says Verified alone, a change that waits for its next terminal says why in a quiet line, and no state shows a warning mark.
Check details, closed until opened, holds for each goblin what its check proved and did not, when it ran and what it read of its session, so Verified carries no words on how it was checked (the Overlord, 2026-11-11).
Claude checks use its MCP health report and the goblin's strict/config-file arguments; Codex inventory preserves the goblin's disabled-server overrides, and enabled servers earn Connected only from a fresh app-server runtime report, never from stored auth or cached tools.
Repository services reuse the auth manifest's probes and status words: one whose credentials resolve and that declares no probe is Provided, which reads Set, and says it declares no connection check, one whose probe or identity check needs a program this machine does not have is Provided and says it was not checked here and why, and a token present in the goblin's environment is Provided rather than Connected.
A check that could not run, because the credential store could not be opened or the goblin's terminal could not be read, is Not checked and says why; a Claude MCP server still starting when its check's time ran out is Unverified alone, and a failed, expired, missing or unreachable connector keeps reading so.
A service the task does not carry is not probed, and its goblin's row offers Connect with nothing to store, since the value is stored and only kept from this task ([Credentials by need](contract/authentication.md#credentials-by-need)).
Workspace says in one line which services' credentials the goblin's terminal carries, by name.
Checks are cached for ten minutes and limited to two concurrent workers and 45 seconds per check, counted from when a check takes its worker, and return Checking immediately instead of blocking the board; a result older than ten minutes keeps its verdicts, each with the time it was checked, while its recheck runs.
Each check starts the goblin's harness, so with a one-minute cache every open of the page after the first minute started one harness for each goblin and said Checking connectors... until the last had ended, minutes with fourteen goblins; an open within ten minutes of the checks now starts none (#2394).
A grant or revoke made with `goblins auth` changes the task's record, which rechecks that goblin at the page's next read.
The page reads `GET /api/connections` for every running goblin at once, so each one's last result shows at once while the checks queue behind it, and nothing reads it while the page is closed.
Opening the page starts a goblin's check only when its cached result is older than ten minutes or its record changed; Recheck and the first return to the board after each sign-in click always start one for every goblin, and a finished repair card, a Connect or a Disconnect has its own goblin read again.
The page polls a goblin only while its check runs, so an open page never rechecks on its own.
A recheck or finished repair that arrives while a goblin is being read has it read once more after that reading, and repair buttons act on the shown result without starting a check.
Native environment reads verify the host, process ancestry, working folder and spawn generation; a terminal that cannot be read leaves its variables and the crawler Not checked.
Herdr environment reads first verify the pane's registered harness, working folder, foreground process and shell ancestry, then the spawn generation in the process itself.
Project MCP servers omitted from the goblin's configuration show with Connect on that goblin's row, and a token action when the project names a token variable and they are failing.
CLI sign-in is offered only when the service's declared credentials resolve, and launch-disabled MCP servers offer no sign-in.
Sign in opens the manifest's HTTPS login page and CLI sign-in a server-generated repair card using the existing Command Center run machinery; Store from clipboard creates a store-from-clipboard card without reading the clipboard in the browser.
A variable a goblin's terminal holds, that its repository's auth manifest declares for a service the goblin carries and that is no harness's billing key, offers a button that says Replace, named Replace from clipboard with the variable and the goblin for a screen reader, beside that goblin on the variable's own page (#2389; the Overlord, 2026-11-12: "i hate this much text on a button"): it creates the same card, titled Replace, which stores what he copied for that goblin's repository through `goblins auth store`, so every running goblin of it is told to load it again, and the page says so and that Set does not say which value a goblin holds; nothing shows either value.
Fix requests accept connection and action identities only, require the board token and origin, and reject replaced tasks; repair cards verify the task again before running.
`POST /api/connections/grant` takes a task, its spawn generation, a `service:`, `mcp:` or `withheld:` connection and `grant` or `revoke`, and does what `goblins auth grant` and `goblins auth revoke` (with `--mcp` for a server) do. Past the token and origin checks it takes only a board proven to be the Overlord's own, as AFK mode's switch does, since any program on the PC can read the token. The task's status credits his board, the CEO gets a notify wake, a live native goblin is told to load a granted service's credentials, and the response is the check that follows. The goblin's row shows his press at once and keeps it until a finished check shows it, or puts it back with the supervisor's reason.
Finishing a repair refreshes the cached status, and returning from a browser sign-in rechecks it.
A stored token does not change an already running native process's environment, so its goblin's row keeps its Missing state until the goblin receives it.
Environment values, full process environments, dotenv, auth scripts, MCP commands and headers are never exposed.

### A session read as messages

The supervisor reads a Claude Code, Codex or pi session as one list of messages, which the board's [Messages view](#the-messages-view) shows.
`GET /api/messages?task=ID&generation=GEN&token=TOKEN` reads a task's goblin of that generation, from the conversation the board recorded for the generation, or else the one the board's read of the goblin's tree proved its running harness in and kept, as `fleettree.OwnedSession` reads them, and `?ceo=TERMINAL&token=TOKEN` reads the CEO registered in that native terminal, from the conversation its harness proves it is in, which the board records when the record named another ([The session event stream](#the-session-event-stream)).
Finding a goblin's reads no process and parses no store file, so its conversation shows once a read of its tree, every fifteen seconds, has kept it; the CEO's read proves its live conversation from the harness every time, and a record that names a conversation another folder keeps, or one never written, is reported as stale rather than rendered as current.
It reads the harness's conversation records without requiring hook events or changing hook trust.
Codex contributes recorded replies, public reasoning summaries, tool output, diffs, embedded pictures and usage; encrypted reasoning and arbitrary image paths are not read.
The board's token comes in the query, as a terminal view's does, since a browser's event stream cannot send a header, and a request without it is refused with 403.
An unknown task is refused with 404, a task that restarted or was replaced since the board named its generation with 409, and the CEO in another terminal than the one named with 409.
Like a terminal view, and unlike a baby goblin's terminal, it is served over Remote access too, since it shows what the session's terminal already shows.
Without `stream` it answers a page, the newest 50 messages or with `before=SEQ` the 50 before that message, as `{"conversation":"...","messages":[...],"more":true,"usage":{...}}`, `more` saying older messages come before the page's first; a session whose harness has written nothing yet has none.
`conversation` names the conversation the page was read from: it changes when the session moves to another conversation or its file is replaced, and stays the same when the supervisor reads again a conversation it let go while idle.
`before=SEQ` must come with the `conversation` of the page it reads back from, and is refused with 400 without one and with 409, Conversation changed, once the session's conversation is another.
A Claude Code conversation longer than 8 MiB is read from the first prompt, interrupt or compaction in its last 8 MiB, so its newest page waits for no more than that, and the whole conversation is read beside it: a page that reaches the first message of that tail waits for the whole, and `usage` is left out until the whole is in.
The whole then takes the tail's place without a `page` event, a message it reads otherwise coming as a change, unless the tail held no message or one the whole does not hold at its `seq`.
`usage`, present once the conversation records any, is what the session spent as far as its own file says: `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `cost_usd`, `context_tokens` for the context its last request carried, and `context_window` from the harness's own model catalog where that names it; it names neither the session's model nor its effort, which the session's one record says ([The message box](#the-message-box)).
A notice Claude Code writes itself, such as a usage limit's, names the model `<synthetic>` and carried no request, so it changes neither `context_window` nor `context_tokens`.
A stat the conversation does not record is left out, and the board leaves that stat out rather than inventing one.
With `stream=1` it answers server-sent events: a `page` event with the newest page, then a `messages` event, `{"conversation":"...","messages":[...]}`, with each message that began or changed since, read every half second, each in full, which a view puts in place of the one with the same `id` or adds by its `seq`, and after a page or a change a `usage` event, `{"conversation":"...","usage":{...}}`, with what the session has spent, from the read already made.
A view takes a `messages` or `usage` event that names a conversation other than its page's as a stream it cannot parse.
`waiting` lists, oldest first, prompts recorded by the harness whose delivered rows have not appeared, each as `{"id":"...","at":"...","source":"...","text":"..."}`, on a page while it holds any and on every `messages` event.
A prompt of his that a board message typed also names that message as `"action":"<action id>"`: the supervisor reads its own committed actions with the waiting list and names, for each prompt in order, the oldest message to that session it has started typing (`running`), of the same words, or for a `!` command the words after its `!`, a shell prompt naming only a `!` command, taken before the harness recorded the prompt and named by no earlier one (`namedWaiting` in `internal/supervisor/waiting_prompts.go`).
The board commits a message as running before it types it, so the name is there however the board's snapshot and the session's stream are ordered, and a prompt typed in the terminal, or recorded before the board took the message, names none.
A prompt has no place among the messages until its delivered row appears, since they sort by where the record holds them, so it is listed apart.
The list goes out whenever it changes, even with no message changed; changed messages and waiting prompts come from one locked read, so a delivered row and the removal of its waiting entry reach each stream together.
Claude Code's `enqueue` starts a wait; a `dequeue` marks the oldest prompt not already sending, or a `remove` marks one with matching source and text, with `"sending":true` while its delivered row is still absent.
Delivery removes one entry with the same source and text, a shell command's row one of his words of the same text too, preferring one already sending, and can also clear a wait whose dequeue or remove was never recorded.
An answer written after the last dequeue or remove ends every wait still sending, since the session has gone on from that handoff and no delivered row is coming for it.
Claude Code 2.1.322 queues and dequeues its own prompt after a usage limit resets and records it for its model alone, which shows nothing, so that wait ended with no row and read Sending for good (#2327); an entry the harness lost, marked sending in the place of the one after it, ends the same way.
An answer stamped before the handoff and recorded after it, as a notice Claude Code writes itself can be, ends none.
The fleet's notices never wait here, and the Codex and pi readers read no queue, so their prompts show once taken.
A later `page` event of another `conversation` replaces everything a view holds: the session moved to another conversation, as `/clear` moves it, or the conversation was replaced; one of the same `conversation` keeps the older messages the view already read in before it, while the view still holds the page's first message.
The session's conversation is found again as often as a terminal view's binding is checked, and once the session no longer runs an `unavailable` event says why and the stream ends.
The board names a session for a task's generation from its own ledger, and where that names none, from the conversation its read of the running harness proved and kept (`namedSession` in `internal/supervisor/fleet_tree.go`), so a harness whose start the ledger never took, as pi's is on a home whose native hook it never ran, still reaches Modern Chat, the family tree and the goblin's panel, and a relaunch finds it again.
A stream that has sent nothing for five seconds sends an `alive` event, so the view hears from its stream at least every five seconds.
A `session` event says what the fleet's stream says of the session: its newest turn, whether that turn runs, ended or was never reported by its harness, why not when it was never reported, the stream's head, and why the conversation the board reads is not the one the session writes, or why the board cannot write or read the stream.
The view draws a turn's working indicator from that event alone, never from a snapshot of the session's terminal, so a finished turn never draws one at any snapshot timing.
It reads that word against the conversation it draws, never against a copy kept from when he left the end, so a turn that ends while he reads up loses its indicator there too.
A view that draws less than the session holds is behind: one that holds back what its stream sent while he reads up, and one whose stream's head names a newer message or a newer conversation, as `/clear` begins.
Behind is a state of its own that is neither disconnected nor ended, it draws no indicator and no turn's end for what it has not drawn, Jump to latest catches up to every message it held back without a reload, and no timer infers it from elapsed time.
Nothing says it over the conversation, since Jump to latest shows wherever he reads up (the Overlord, 2026-11-12: "I hate all these alerts remove them!!", #2438).
A view that follows the end draws what its stream sends as it arrives, so it is behind only while its stream's head is past what the stream sent it.
While its stream reconnects, the view keeps the turn's last word, so it claims no turn end and no Send the stream never said, and it draws the working indicator only while its stream is live and connected, as the turn may end meanwhile.
A closed stream draws nothing it last said of the turn until a new stream speaks.
The box under the conversation reads its Stop from the same event, so a turn that ended leaves a Send, never a Stop, whatever a snapshot still says of the session (#2292).
On 2026-10-29 the Overlord's view of the CEO showed a call Running at least 76 seconds after it ended, with no word that it was stale.
A stream that drops or ends is opened again, which begins with the newest page, and says Reconnecting until it does.
Nothing is drawn over the top of the conversation, at a phone's width or the desktop's, in the CEO's panel or a goblin's (#2438).
While it reconnects, a quiet amber dot on the picture in the panel's header says so, where a messenger marks presence, and takes no room, so nothing moves (2026-10-28).
Why the conversation it draws is stale, or why its stream cannot be kept, is a quiet line of its own under the header's row, and stays while it reconnects.
A stream also ends after ten minutes, as a baby goblin's does, and at most 32 stream at once, a limit of their own.
With `picture=ID` it answers a picture a message holds, read back from its entry by the id a read of the messages gave it, never by a path or an offset the browser names; a Claude Code picture's id names where it is in its conversation, so a read of the tail and a read of the whole name it alike, and it is answered only when a read found a picture there.
It must come with the page's `conversation`, and is refused with 400 without one; a picture of a conversation the session no longer has, or whose file changed while it was read, is answered with 404 as one no read found.
With `document=TARGET` and a task's `task` and `generation` it answers a PDF that goblin's conversation names, for a [document card](#the-messages-view); unlike a page or a stream, it takes the board's token in an `X-CEO-Token` header and never from the query, so no PDF address the board makes carries it.
`TARGET` must be, letter for letter, the address in a Markdown link's parentheses in a message's words or a reply's text, never its thinking, found back through the whole conversation within 8 seconds before any file is opened (`internal/supervisor/session_documents.go`).
The file must be a `.pdf` named by a drive letter or a root path, inside the task's worktree, extra worktrees, `data\<task>\` folder, `state\tasktmp\<task>\` folder or scratch folder, opened as a regular file without a link or junction out of them, sniffed as `application/pdf` and holding 1 byte to 64 MiB; it is answered as an attachment, `private, no-store`.
The CEO's conversation, a stale generation, another task, an unnamed, missing or out-of-scope file, traversal, a UNC, device or alternate-stream path, content other than a PDF and a file over 64 MiB are each refused with the same 403, That PDF is unavailable for this session., which names no path.
`GET /api/commands?task=ID&generation=GEN&token=TOKEN` answers `{"commands":[{"name":"model","description":"...","source":"builtin","sigil":"/"},...]}` for the task's current harness and worktree; `ceo=TERMINAL` reads the CEO's harness and home.
Each entry's `source` is `command`, `skill` or `builtin`, its `description` is the frontmatter description of a command or skill, empty when it gives none, and its `sigil` is what the harness takes it behind in a message: `/` for a command, and `$` for a Codex skill, which Codex takes as a mention.
Claude Code contributes command files and user-invocable skills, and the commands and skills of each plugin `installed_plugins.json` records for the user or for the session's folder alone, in its plugins folder (`CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR` or `plugins` under its configuration folder), that the user's settings, then the folder's `.claude/settings.json` and then its `.claude/settings.local.json` leave enabled, or its manifest's `defaultEnabled` when none names it (`internal/supervisor/commands_claude.go`).
A plugin's entries are named behind its manifest's name, or the name it was installed under, and a colon: its `commands` folder, or the commands its manifest names in its place, its `skills` folder and the skill folders its manifest adds, or the plugin itself when it is one skill.
Plugins synced from a claude.ai account are not read.
pi contributes the prompt templates and skills its settings and persisted project trust permit.
Codex 0.160.0 has no prompts of its own; it contributes the skills it loads, as its own skills loader reads them (`internal/supervisor/commands_codex.go`): `.codex/skills` and `.agents/skills` in each folder from the project's root down to the session's, `skills` in its configuration folder, the shared `.agents/skills`, its bundled `skills/.system` unless `config.toml` turns them off, and the skills of each plugin `config.toml` turns on, from the plugin's active version in `plugins/cache`, behind the plugin's name and a colon.
A skill needs frontmatter with a description and a name a `$` mention carries, at most six levels below its folder, and none in a hidden folder; one a `[[skills.config]]` rule turns off is left out, and so is a name two loaded skills hold, which Codex takes as no skill.
After them come the harness's built-in commands, from a table kept in Go of the commands Claude Code's command reference, Codex 0.160.0's slash command source and pi 1.4.0's slash command source list, leaving out each one the harness hides from its own list.
Each group is sorted by name, and a session's own `/` entry hides a built-in of the same name, while a Codex `$` skill is listed beside it.
The composer renews command discovery when a task's generation or the CEO's terminal start time changes.
`GET /api/files?task=ID&generation=GEN&token=TOKEN&q=QUERY` answers `{"files":["relative/path",...]}` for the composer's `@` list: the files `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` names in the session's own folder, as `/api/commands` selects it, with forward slashes, kept for 10 seconds so a burst of keystrokes runs git once.
It answers at most 50, ignoring case: a path whose base name starts with the query first, then one whose base name holds it, then one whose path holds it, and the first 50 by path for an empty query; a folder outside any git repository answers an empty list, and it is refused as `/api/commands` is.

Each message has an `id` that stays the same for its life, its `seq`, its place in the session, its `kind` and when it began.
A Claude Code message's `seq` is the offset in its conversation of the entry it began at, so every read of the conversation gives it the same; a Codex or pi message's is its place counted from 0.
A `prompt` carries its `text` and its `source`.
The source is `overlord` for a prompt typed into the session that names no other source, `goblins` for the CEO's brief or a line the CEO sent, with its `CEO: ` stamp taken off, and `answer` for the Overlord's answer from the Command Center.
It is `notice` for Claude Code's notice that a background task or sub-agent ended or a monitor reported, which reads as its summary, `slash` for a slash command as it was typed, with its `output`, and `shell` for a `!` command, with its `output` and `errors`.
A `shell` prompt, and a waiting one, that is the line a message in [shell mode](#the-message-box) typed, naming a script in the home's own `state/shell-commands`, reads back as the command he wrote, from that script, with neither `powershell.exe` nor the script's path; a waiting one also reads as `shell` (`shownShellCommands` in `internal/supervisor/shell_messages.go`).
`queued` marks a prompt Claude Code took while it worked, and `pictures` are the pictures pasted into a prompt.
A `reply` is one answer, its `blocks` grouped by the id Claude Code gives the answer, since it writes each block as an entry of its own: `text`, `thinking` where Claude Code recorded its words, since most is recorded empty and shows nothing, and `call`.
A call names its `tool`, what it was called on and its `state`: `running` until its result is written, then `done`, `failed`, `denied` when a permission rule or auto mode refused it, or `interrupted`.
Its `result` is the first 4,000 characters of what came back, an edit's `edit` its added and removed line counts with its diff, a sub-agent's `agent` its node in the goblin's tree, whose own output `GET /api/tasks/<id>/agent` serves, and a picture it returned is among its `pictures`.
A result too large for the conversation, which Claude Code saves to a file in the conversation's `tool-results` folder, reads from that file, with `spilled` its size; a file named anywhere else, or reached through a link or junction, is not read, and the preview the conversation holds stands.
A `compaction` carries its `trigger`, auto or manual, and the summary Claude Code goes on from, and an `interrupt` the notice Claude Code records where the Overlord stopped it.
A sub-agent's own entries, and what Claude Code records for its model alone, its requests, hook notes, caveats and a skill's expanded prompt, show nothing.
The reader reads a conversation on from where it last stopped, as the tree's reader does, and passes over an entry still being written until its line ends.
It keeps each conversation for five minutes after a view last asked for it, which an open view does twice a second, so it keeps every conversation a view shows and lets the rest go at the next ask or the next read of the fleet's trees.
Each conversation is read under a lock of its own, apart from the trees, so a view's read never waits on a tree's, nor on a first read of another view's long conversation.
Each harness writes its record a message at a time as its turn runs: Claude Code an entry per content block of an answer, and Codex and pi one entry per assistant message, so a reply shows as the harness produces it, one completed block or message at a time, and a view changes only that reply (`frontend/src/message-window.ts`).
No harness writes a text block before it ends, so the words of one long answer arrive when that answer's block is complete, whichever harness produced them; pi's session is read the same way as Claude Code's transcript and Codex's rollout, and needs no other record to keep up with them.

### The working indicator

Modern Chat's working spot, the end of the reply in progress, draws a session's working state as one indicator, drawn in the board's own CSS in the harness's colour: a helix of seven rows of two dots that fades into sixteen cells lighting in Bayer order and back every 8 seconds (the Overlord, 2026-10-30: "i prefered the helix").
Every part of it moves by transform and opacity alone, which the compositor draws without the page's main thread, and it runs only while a turn runs and its conversation shows, never behind the terminal or the Task view.
For a while it was a still halftone that breathed as one, after a panel at rest kept the main thread 44 percent busy (2026-10-28: "32 animations??? remvoe like all but the streaming one").
Measured on that build with a snapshot each second, about 30 of the 36 ms of main-thread work each second came from its 32 CSS animations, 50 style recalculations a second, and about 5 from the one-second clock that drew the working spot again and the redraws of the queued messages on every snapshot, which stay gone.
The animations reached the main thread because Chrome wakes it at every turn of a CSS animation while anything in the page listens for animationiteration, as React's root always does, so the indicator animates by element.animate, which raises no such event.
render-budget.spec.ts holds a working panel at rest under 40 style recalculations in 3 seconds; the CSS helix took 138.
The colour is the ink of the harness's own mark: Claude Code's orange, Codex's light ink and pi's moss (2026-10-30: "make color of the helix match teh harnes icon coloring").
Beside it, the words the harness says it is doing, or Working when it says none, type out a letter at a time, a shimmer crosses them, and whimsical verbs follow in turn, one every 3 seconds, typed the same way (2026-10-30: "make twitsting and teh verbs rotate in a type writere hroiztonal shimmer"), each ending in one ellipsis (2026-11-04).
While the session's own hooks say it compacts, from Claude Code's or Codex's PreCompact until its PostCompact, pi's `session_before_compact` until its `session_compact`, or until whatever the session reports next, the words say Compacting… alone, continuously visible in purple with the indicator in the same colour; a message he sends meanwhile is held as his Queued entry, never Sent before the board hands it over, and his draft and its files stay in the box (2026-11-04 and 2026-11-05).
A goblin's compaction reads so only while its native terminal is up, since a harness that died compacting never reported its end.
While the terminal's turn row says the harness waits to send a request that failed again, as Claude Code 2.1.322's "✻ 529 Overloaded · Retrying in 3s · attempt 3/10", the session still works, and the words say Retrying… alone in amber, with the attempt and the failure after them, as "attempt 3 of 10 · 529 Overloaded", until the terminal shows the turn go on (#2236).
Under the box, Modern Chat shows what the terminal draws by its prompt line for as long as it draws it, as the terminal draws it under its prompt (#2294): the context gauge in its own words, as "83% context used" or "0% until auto-compact", and the notices after it, as "Auto-update failed" (#2236).
A notice of a failed update says why and offers Retry (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "the Auto-update failed notice needs a reason and a Retry", #2333).
The terminal's own advice beside it, "Run claude doctor", is part of that one notice and is not shown: `/doctor` is a prompt that starts a turn of its own, and the button that typed it put it into the CEO's running turn three times on 2026-11-10, ahead of his message.
No notice types anything into a session, and Retry never queues a message: it posts `POST /api/harness/update-check` with `{"harness":"<kind>"}`, and the supervisor runs that harness's own update check, `claude update` for Claude Code, as a hidden program outside every session, one at a time and for at most three minutes (`internal/supervisor/update_check.go`).
The snapshot's `update_checks` says, by harness, that the check is `running`, which reads Checking on a disabled button; that it `failed`, with the last line the check said as its `result`, which the notice reads after a colon; or how it ended otherwise, which reads `Checked again: <result>` beside the notice.
A check that succeeds is followed by a read of the installed version against its publisher's newest, and one found `current` takes the notice away at once, with no restart, though the terminal draws its own until its session ends.
A session's screen that shows the notice has that same read made, at most once in fifteen minutes for a harness, so a notice left over from before an update goes away by itself; a publisher that cannot be read leaves the notice as it is.
A harness with no update check the board can run refuses Retry with those words, shown over the box.
The typing slides a window over the word to each letter's edge, and the shimmer is a light band crossing it, both by transform alone.
Every word is in the page from the start, laid unseen in the one place, and shows in its turn by its own animations, which come round again while the turn runs, so nothing in the page changes as the words rotate (the CEO, 2026-10-30: "nothing moves when nothing runs"): a working panel at rest recalculates the page's style 2 or 3 times in 3 seconds, and React draws nothing again.
It carries an accessible name, the harness's own words or Working, and reduced motion keeps the helix still and shows those words whole.
Working cards, tree rows and a goblin's Task view show the board's still blue status dot instead; the indicator is only in Modern Chat's working spot.
Running tool calls keep a static state mark.
The CEO's own panel shows the indicator at its reply's end while it works, as a goblin's does.

### The Messages view

A Claude Code, Codex or pi session's panel offers Modern messages and the Classic native terminal in the same conversation area.
Classic is what a browser that saved no choice draws, for the CEO and every goblin, until Modern Chat is ready to be the default (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "the classic terminal worked well ... It should be the default for now"); a saved choice of either is kept in this browser as `ceo-chat-style` (`frontend/src/chat-style.ts`).
Classic draws the session's terminal alone, as the board drew it before Modern Chat: no message box under it, while it is held, busy or reconnecting included, and no Copy terminal text beside it, so a held terminal says Typing is paused and the terminal's own microphone shows.
A box with work of its own stays under Classic's terminal as [The message box](#the-message-box) describes.
The session's picture in the panel's header opens the choice of style, Modern chat or Terminal, with the style shown checked (#1987).
Modern chat saves Modern and shows the conversation with the keyboard in its box; Terminal saves Classic and hands the keyboard to the terminal, or to the box while work of its own keeps it under the terminal; the style already shown changes nothing.
The box itself keeps no choice of style.
A choice of style, in the header's menu or the Workspace setting, redraws the open panel at once, the CEO's included: it ends a read back out of Classic.
That menu is the one switch of style between the conversation and the terminal (2026-11-04).
A command's row in the conversation, opened, has no button into the terminal (the Overlord, 2026-11-13: "we dont need the open in terminal button"; #2524), which that menu reaches as it does everywhere else on the board.
A row whose command drives the goblin's own browser through chrome-devtools-axi offers **Open screen** while that browser runs, which picks the panel's Screen view with the keyboard on it (#2362, approved 2026-11-11).
A view the panel cannot show has no button, and nothing switches the panel back by itself: Open screen holds as the selector's Screen does ([A goblin's own browser](#a-goblins-own-browser)).
A read back out of Classic belongs to the session it was taken on while the panel shows it: showing another session, or closing the panel, ends it, so opening, switching to or reopening any session lands in the chosen style, while each session keeps its own draft, attachments, recording, queued messages, history and reading place (#1987).
The CEO and goblins have Modern while their harness runs in a native terminal, from the start of its generation to its end, paused included (`messagesQuery` in `frontend/src/session-messages.ts`).
A session in Herdr and a stopped or retired goblin keep their existing terminal or ended-session view, and a baby goblin its terminal alone.
A CEO starting before its harness registers shows its terminal, which may ask for a sign-in.
An open panel follows the task's current root generation or the CEO's terminal start time without a reload, preserving the draft and durable queue.
The messages fill the panel, so they fill the window while it is maximized.
A switch by Ctrl+Alt with a digit or an arrow, the first open and the end of the tour hand the keyboard to the message box, or to the terminal where the session has no messages or Classic draws it.
The view shows the session's conversation as `GET /api/messages` serves it ([A session read as messages](#a-session-read-as-messages)), read only.
The panel's selector chooses between this conversation and its task view, for the CEO and a goblin alike, and while a goblin's own browser runs its Screen view too ([A goblin's own browser](#a-goblins-own-browser)): a segmented control of Chat and Task, never a fold or a dropdown (the Overlord, 2026-10-26: "i dont like how you remove the selector at the top between caht and task panel bring that back i dont like the drop down also").
Its buttons are icons alone, Chat as Modern chat's message bubble, Task and Screen, each named for a screen reader, with no words and no tip, in the middle of the panel header's one row, between the name and the panel's controls, at desktop and phone widths alike (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "chat and task doesn't need to be full width, it can fit inline with cfo and afk and just be the icons").
It is only as wide as its icons, and the room either side of it is shared equally, so it sits in the middle while both sides fit (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "also put the switch in the middle less wide").
On a narrower panel the controls keep what they show and the name gives way, wrapping onto more lines, and a control goes into More only once the name is down to its first letters.
A maximized panel has room for both at once, draws its task list beside the conversation and hides the selector, except while the goblin's own browser runs, when the selector switches between the conversation and the Screen view.
The conversation carries no speaker line: no sender name and no harness icon above a message, for any speaker (the Overlord, 2026-10-26: "you dont need to ahve cfo and You above each of our messages either").
What says who spoke is the side and the bubble it already has.
The conversation is one centred column of a readable width, about 48 times its text size, however wide the panel is, with the message box centred under it.
A prompt sits on the right in a quiet bubble, and a notice is a quiet line with no bubble.
An answer he gave on the Command Center or on a session's Scrawl page reads as his own message with one small line under it, Answered on the board or Answered on the page, so it is told apart from one he typed in the chat.
Each message of his that the session's record holds, a slash or `!` command he typed as much as his words, has no label under its bubble, and its date, time and Copy are in the line that shows on a hover or a tap, as any of his messages has (the Overlord, 2026-10-30: "we dont need read 8:26 only and th copy tomestamp hover line").
A slash or `!` command shows as typed with what it printed under it, a long line of the print scrolling sideways inside its own box.
Harness queue entries appear at the conversation's end with the delivery labels described under [The message box](#the-message-box) (#2103).
The pictures he sent show above his bubble, on his side, at a size he can read, as the Claude desktop app shows them (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "see how claude includes pictures when pasted"): those the transcript holds, read back by id, and those the message box kept, whose path on a line of its own in his words shows as its picture instead and never as the path, read back through `GET /api/composer/file` ([The message box](#the-message-box)).
Each picture loads only as its row nears the screen and decodes off the main thread, and once loaded changes nothing but itself; a click opens it in the conversation's picture viewer, described with a call's pictures below.
A long prompt, more than six lines or 600 characters, and a long print show their first lines fading out over Show more, which opens them whole, and Show less folds them again; a short one shows as it is (`frontend/src/long-text.tsx`, the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "pull with a lot of text need to be cleaned").
A reply sits on the left as plain prose with no bubble.
Its text has its bold, inline code, lists, tables and links as a question card draws them (`frontend/src/messageText.ts`), its headings in bold, and its code blocks as written in rounded cards under a quiet header with the language the fence names, Copy and Full screen (`frontend/src/code-block.tsx`).
Copy is its icon alone, named Copy for a screen reader, and shows a check once the whole block is on the clipboard (the Overlord, 2026-11-13: "You don't need the word copy, we just have the icon"; #2524; `frontend/src/code-copy.tsx`).
Every http or https address the board shows in a message, a report, a card or a panel is a link that opens it in a new tab, which the desktop window hands to the default browser, so the board is never navigated away (the Overlord, 2026-11-11: "Should be clickable links or buttons"; #2360).
A pull request, issue or CI run on github.com, and a review page's session on this PC, reads as a chip with a short name, such as `code-goblins PR #2358` or Review page, with the whole address in its tip; each is known by its exact host and path, so a look-alike host keeps its own words.
A Markdown link to an address reads as its words, an address in a code block, in a command or in what a command printed stays as written and is still a link, and any other scheme stays text; message text is always escaped.
The sentence a panel says under a goblin's status links the words it says each of the report's addresses by, such as PR #12.
A goblin's terminal, the CEO's and a baby goblin's draw each http or https address as a link that a click or a tap opens in a new tab, one that wraps onto the next row included.
A link a program prints with words of its own, an OSC 8 hyperlink, opens its http or https address the same way, as a Markdown link's words do in chat; nothing else in a terminal opens.
One of the board's own [addresses](#board-addresses), in a message or a terminal, opens its place in the board itself instead of a new tab, with no reload (#2363).
A card shows its first sixteen lines and scrolls a long line sideways inside itself; Full screen opens it whole over the window, the board dimmed under it and every line wrapped ("The full screen should open up like a full window or full width"; #2524; `frontend/src/full-screen.tsx`).
Escape or the card's own button leaves the full screen and nothing else, and the keyboard returns to the button that opened it.
A PowerShell block in a finished reply's own words also has Run beside Copy, at its size (#2051): one fenced `powershell`, `pwsh` or `ps1`, or an untagged one whose first word, after an optional `! ` that Run drops, is `powershell` or `pwsh`.
Run never shows on another language, on a tool's output, on his pasted or quoted words, on a hook's note or on the reply still being written, and nothing runs on render, hover or Copy.
Run sends `POST /api/code-run` with only where the block sits: the session (task and generation, or the CEO's terminal), the message's id, the block's index and which code block of its text it is, behind the board's token and its exact Host and Origin checks, with no command in any address (`internal/supervisor/code_run.go`).
The supervisor reads that message back from the session's own transcript and runs only a closed PowerShell block of that reply's words, so text the browser names, a block the reply never held, a fence left open, a prompt or a tool's output is refused and nothing runs.
It runs once, as a [run item](#run-items) of the board's own in a new, visible terminal in the Code Goblins home, with Windows PowerShell 5.1, and the Command Center opens on its card; while that block's run is ready or running a second Run is refused, and Run says Starting until the board answers.
`state/runs.audit` records each, with the reply and block it came from and when.
A refusal shows in the card, under its code.
A long path or address in prose or in a prompt breaks where it meets the column's edge, so nothing but a code card's own line is ever wider than the conversation, on a phone included.
A Markdown link to a local PDF in a prompt's, reply's or compaction's words, such as one whose words are Retail report and whose address is `C:/task/report.pdf`, shows as a document card instead of its Markdown and address: a PDF mark, the link's words as its label, wrapped however long, and Open PDF, at most 32 rem wide and never wider than a phone's column (`frontend/src/message-document.tsx`).
A link inside a code value or a web address stays as written, a code value inside a link's words stays part of its label, and a link in thinking stays plain text.
Nothing is read until Open PDF is pressed: it says Loading PDF..., reads the file through `GET /api/messages` with `document` ([A session read as messages](#a-session-read-as-messages)), and opens it from a browser-local address in a new tab, leaving Open PDF and Download PDF on the card, the keyboard on Open PDF where the keyboard pressed it.
A refusal says That PDF is unavailable for this session., and an answer not begun within 30 seconds says Opening that PDF timed out. Try again.; a download already under way is never cut off.
The desktop window hands only HTTP(S) addresses to the default browser, so a browser-local preview does not open there, and the card's Download PDF, which needs no new tab, is what it offers instead.
Its thinking folds to one quiet line, Thinking and its first words, that opens to the whole of it (the Overlord's review of 27 October 2026, which took the agentic chat's look over the 26 October reading in place).
A call that writes a plan, Claude Code's TodoWrite or Codex's update_plan, shows as a checklist card instead of a row: each step with its state's mark, the step in progress in full ink, those done struck through, and how many are done at its head (`frontend/src/plan-card.tsx`); the supervisor reads the steps from the call (`internal/fleettree/messages.go`, `Block.Plan`).
A plan call that failed, was denied or was interrupted stays its row with its outcome, and so does an update_plan Codex refused, one with a step missing its words or its state or with a state other than pending, in_progress or completed.
Newer Claude Code builds keep their checklist with TaskCreate, which adds one task, and TaskUpdate, which changes or deletes one by its ID, so the supervisor builds the list as those calls run and shows it as the same card at the latest task call that ran; each earlier task call is a row, Added a task or Updated a task, with its task's subject, and one that failed, was denied or was interrupted, or a TaskUpdate Claude Code refused with success false, changes nothing and reads its outcome, Failed, Denied or Interrupted.
A list the conversation cannot know shows no card: the page served from a long conversation's tail, until the whole conversation is read, and a conversation whose task call names a task no call in it made, or whose TaskCreate result names no task.
Each run of calls made one after another is one quiet stack of rows, and calls that finished as asked, two or more in a row, fold into one row, as Claude's own app shows them (#2191).
That row has a list's icon and counts what the calls did by kind with their verbs, such as Ran 5 commands or Read 2 files, ran 3 commands, and a tap or Enter opens it to each call's own row (`frontend/src/message-calls.tsx`, `callRuns` in `frontend/src/session-messages.ts`, `callTally` in `frontend/src/call-words.ts`).
A call still running stays one live row with its running verb beside the fold, and a failed, denied or interrupted call, a question, a plan and a baby goblin are never folded, so they split the run they stand in.
Reply text breaks a run.
What is open is kept by call, by its call_id or else its place in the reply, never by fold, and a fold is open while any call in it was open, so a row he opened stays open in the fold it joins and a fold he opened stays open as a call on either side finishes and joins it (`frontend/tests/folded-tool-calls.spec.ts`).
A call's row is one line: its tool's icon, the verb it is told by, as Read, Edited, Ran, Searched or Updated the plan for Claude Code's, Codex's and pi's tools (`frontend/src/call-words.ts`), what it was called on cut with an ellipsis, and at its end an edit's added and removed lines and the mark of its outcome, with Running, Failed, Denied or Interrupted for a screen reader.
A connector's call, an MCP tool's, wears that connector's logo in place of an icon, or its initial on a tile where the board bundles none, and reads as the connector's name and its tool in words with no harness's prefix: `mcp__claude_ai_Supabase__execute_sql` is Supabase · Execute SQL (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "the UI for connector should actually use the logo"; #2302, `frontend/src/connector-names.ts`).
A tool search wears a wrench and says how many tools it loaded ("for tool search the icon should be the wrench tool"), and a fold of one connector's calls counts them by its name, as Used Supabase 3 times.
What the call was called on is cut before its tool's words are, and the opened row keeps the call as its harness named it and a tool search's whole query (`frontend/tests/connector-call-rows.spec.ts`).
A running call has a quiet static mark so the end of the reply in progress remains the conversation's one animation.
A long command never wraps into a column (the Overlord's phone, 2026-10-28, showed one PowerShell row over 14 lines); a tap, or Enter, opens the row to all it was called on, a command and what came back in one terminal block, what any other call brought back in one of its own, an edit's diff and its pictures, and under a command that drives the goblin's browser Open screen, as above (`frontend/tests/phone-text.spec.ts`).
What opens is as wide as its row and starts under the row's arrow, at every width (the Overlord, 2026-11-13: "can we make it the full width of the dropdown"; #2524).
Terminal text reads one way wherever the board shows any: a row's command and what it printed, what any other call brought back, a [run item](#run-items)'s command and what its terminal showed, what the [Update item](#update-code-goblins) printed, and a block a question fences ("Just have a better way to format terminal text ... consistent across terminals"; `frontend/src/terminal-text.ts`, `frontend/src/terminal-block.tsx`).
One card holds the command under its shell's name, each of its lines as written after the prompt's mark, `$` or PowerShell's `>`, then what it printed under output, then how it ended on a line of its own under the row's own mark: Running, Completed, Failed, Denied or Interrupted.
A program's colour codes colour its text in the board's own shades of them, every other escape is dropped, and a line its program redrew, as a progress bar does, shows as its terminal left it.
Where a program coloured nothing, a line that starts as an error, a warning or a success reads as one, a path wears a quiet colour and a web address is a link.
A path from a drive reads whole, as a card names one for its link ([Deliberate CEO questions](#deliberate-ceo-questions)), with the line and column a tool may add after it.
A prompt's question and its numbered choices read as written, the mark its terminal points with included, and none is drawn as picked, so a card's own choices are the one place to choose (the Overlord, 2026-11-13, reviewing a question that quoted one: "fix two option confirm on radio button or panel to just make it simple and intuitive").
A long line wraps at its spaces and after the separators of a long word, such as a path's folders, its later rows set in, and never inside a word; a word with no separator that is wider than the card scrolls sideways inside it.
The command and what it printed each have Copy, which takes that part whole with no colour code among it, and Full screen opens the card over the window as a code card's does (`frontend/tests/terminal-text.spec.ts`).
There the command takes up to two fifths of the card above what it printed, which takes the rest, and all of the card when nothing printed follows it.
A run item's kept output is its terminal's rows, already cut at that terminal's width, so a row its terminal wrapped is drawn as the rows it left.
A row whose call read, took or made pictures ends in their thumbnails, as Claude's app shows them, and a fold in those of all its calls: at most three, then how many more, each loaded only as its row nears the screen (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "in goblins modern chat there's no image viewer or previews like claude"; #2305; `frontend/src/call-pictures.tsx`).
A pointer over a thumbnail shows the picture larger in a card over the board, with the verb of the call that holds it and its place among the row's, such as Read and 1 of 3 (`frontend/src/picture-hover-card.tsx`).
The card takes no pointer and no keyboard, opens under its thumbnail where the window has no room above it, and goes when the pointer leaves, the list scrolls or he presses Escape, which then closes nothing else.
A click, a tap or Enter on a thumbnail, or on a picture he sent, opens the conversation's one picture viewer over the whole window with the board dimmed behind it, and never opens the row (`frontend/src/picture-viewer.tsx`).
Its counter, such as 38 of 40, runs across every picture in the messages the view holds, his own and his goblin's in the order their rows show them, so the pictures of an older page join as he reads up to it.
Its arrows, the Left and Right keys and a swipe step one picture and rest at the first and the last, a click on the picture shows it at its own size and a second fits it to the window again, and Escape, Close or a click beside the picture puts the viewer away with the keyboard back on what opened it.
The viewer reads a picture at full size only once it shows it, through the same two routes its row reads it by, so the page names no path the board would not already serve.
A picture that is gone or cannot be read is a quiet mark in its row, its card and the viewer, never the browser's broken image (`frontend/src/picture-image.tsx`).
An open row shows its pictures under what came back, at a size he can read, in place of its thumbnails.
A message still waiting in the queue opens its pictures among its own.
`frontend/tests/chat-pictures.spec.ts` checks these at a desktop's and a phone's width, and `frontend/tests/iphone-chat-pictures.spec.ts` by touch in WebKit at an iPhone 15's size.
A call that started a sub-agent is its baby goblin's row, named as the canvas names it, with Open, which shows that baby goblin's terminal while its goblin's tree holds it.
A compaction is folded to its summary, an interrupt is a plain Interrupted row, and a background task's notice is a quiet line.
Each prompt he sent keeps a small row under it with Copy, which copies its words as written, and when it was sent; the row stays hidden until he taps the message or points at it where there is a pointer, and closes when he taps away.
Nothing else shows under his message: no receipt of when it was sent or replied to (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "We don't need the sent just now if we already have the hover where it shows a timestamp and copy").
A selection he settles in the conversation, released with a mouse or a touch or ended from the keyboard, copies itself and says Copied quietly right under where the selection ends, as a button says it on itself (#2438), and a selection he clears copies nothing.
Each text of a reply has a Copy of its own, shown with no pointer over it and on a phone, which copies that text's whole Markdown source as written, while the turn runs too (#2048).
Only the reply that ends a turn, the last before his next message or an interrupt, shows when it arrived, once the turn has ended, with its last text's Copy between that time and the harness's mark, so a turn of many replies and tool rows prints its time once (`turnEnds` in `frontend/src/session-messages.ts`); a message the harness queued into its turn, or one of his whose delivery ended with no take seen ([The message box](#the-message-box)), goes on with that turn.
A notice and a compaction keep Copy too, which copies the notice's whole text, but for the Stop hook feedback note, whose own words are only its label, and every message's text can be selected.
Exact fleet wake markers, session-lock and wake-queue digests, and stamped background-run and helper notices draw as service lines.
A genuine message that merely begins with `goblins`, such as `goblins drain please`, remains the user's message.
The list scrolls as a page does, with the wheel, the page keys, and Shift with Page Up and Page Down as in a terminal.
The page holds only the messages within 1,200 px of what he reads, each measured as it lays out and the rest kept as room above and below (`frontend/src/message-window.ts`), so a session of thousands of messages costs what a screenful does, a scroll draws again only when it changes which messages the page holds, and a reply that grows as it streams changes its own message and at most the rows its growth pushes off the window's far edge, with the room kept above them, never every line (`frontend/tests/conversation-window.spec.ts`).
What a call folds under its row, and a thought under its line, is in the page only while it is open.
At its end it follows what arrives, and while he reads further up nothing moves or draws again the history in view (#1771): messages that arrive or change meanwhile are held back, only the latest state of each kept, and drawn below him once his scroll down brings the end he left within a screen, or at once when Jump to latest is pressed, and an older page read in above leaves the message he reads where it was (`frontend/tests/conversation-window.spec.ts`, `frontend/tests/conversation-scroll-race.spec.ts`).
Any scroll of his up from the end leaves it at once, a trackpad's slow drag or a flick's first frames included, so a streamed line never pulls him back down, and a scroll back down to within 24 px of the end follows it again once what was held back lies within a screen of it; from further off he reads on down to the newest line, and follows from there (#2007).
Rows leaving the window go from the page before those taking their place arrive, and WebKit can lay the list out between the two, so `.conversation-floor` holds the list's height through the swap: near the end the list would otherwise pull his scroll up to what is left, and keep it there (#2001, #2007).
While his scroll runs the list writes no scroll position, which Chromium drops while it carries a smooth key, wheel or scrollbar scroll on: what moves above him, a row drawn in above at a height other than its estimate included, is taken up by the room above the rows drawn, as far as that room reaches (#2031), and an older page waits until his scroll rests, 250 ms with no scroll and nothing held down on the list, when the scroll position takes what moved in one frame (`frontend/tests/conversation-scroll-race.spec.ts`, `frontend/tests/iphone-conversation-scroll-race.spec.ts`).
A finger counts as held down until the last one lifts, though the browser cancels its pointer once it takes the pan, and a mouse or pen only by its main button, and only until a context menu opens or the window loses its focus, either of which takes its release (`frontend/tests/conversation-scroll-race.spec.ts`).
The message box keeps to the session as it is meanwhile: its model and effort follow the session's record, and its usage and what Up brings back follow the stream, while the history stays still (`frontend/tests/conversation-publication.spec.ts`).
Jump to latest, at the list's foot while he reads up, returns to the latest message and follows the end again; pressed during a momentum or smooth scroll, on a desktop or in an iPhone's WebKit, it settles there, and no scroll or resize queued before it pulls it back (`frontend/tests/conversation-window.spec.ts`, `frontend/tests/iphone-conversation.spec.ts`).
A scroll that finds the list where it put itself to show its end keeps it following: a message that arrived while he read up comes in at an estimated height until it is drawn, and Jump to latest's own scroll lands before the rows it draws are measured, so a reply taller than its estimate once left the list short of the end, with Jump to latest back (#1753).
Classic normal-buffer history accepts wheel, trackpad, Shift+PageUp/Down and touch while held or disconnected, and keeps its reading anchor on reconnect.
A full-screen program owns its native history, so Shift+PageUp/Down follows its wheel path.
While such a terminal cannot accept input, scrolling back opens its recorded conversation without typing or changing the saved Classic preference (#1584), and Terminal in the header's chat style menu returns the terminal, as showing another session and coming back does (#1987).
The terminal decides that before xterm sees the wheel, since xterm's own scroll bar takes a wheel first whenever it has room to scroll, as for a moment after the terminal's box changes size.
While the session works, the end of the reply in progress, where a finished reply shows its time, carries the harness's working indicator in the harness's colour (`frontend/src/reply-working.tsx`); with no reply yet it sits at the reply's place under his message.
It reads no clock, so React does not draw it again while the session works: a hint that counted how long it had worked drew it every second.
The row holds no Stop, on a desktop or a touch screen (the Overlord, 2026-11-03: "The stop button next to the When I'm the verb icon bothers me. Remove it."; #1987): the box stops a turn, with the Stop in Send's place while it is empty and with Escape in it.
While the conversation is read up, the row keeps the room it had when the list left its end, or when what was held back was last drawn below him, so the rows he reads stay put, but its indicator is the session's word on the conversation drawn: it goes when the turn ends, and while the view is behind it shows nothing.
The indicator is drawn only while the conversation reaches its end.
The session's usage is small pies in colours of their own, each with its percentage (`frontend/src/usage-bar.tsx`; the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "Claude uses a bunch of different colors to show smaller little circle pie graphs, pie slices, percentages, relevant to the context of the actual conversation").
They end the message box's row of controls, after the microphone, where the send arrow sat before it moved onto the line he writes on (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "The usage circle goes in place of the arrow in the box"), and in a narrow box, a phone's or a narrow side panel's, they drop their words and keep their percentages.
Its pies are the context the session's last request filled of its model's window, the week's allowance used for a Claude Code or Codex session, and a Pi session's OpenRouter key spend against its limit, each only when the session's own reading backs it (`usageReadings` in `frontend/src/session-usage.ts`).
Each pie is a meter a screen reader reads with its numbers, and nothing opens on hover.
A reading the session does not back draws no pie, and with none the bar is not drawn: the box never says usage is unavailable or could not be read.
While the conversation still connects the bar is not drawn, so usage shows only once the conversation is connected and its reading has come.
