The supervisor reads the context window from the session's own record: Codex's rollout names it, a Pi session's model has it in Pi's `models.json` or `models-store.json`, and a Claude Code transcript names its model, whose window the supervisor knows (`claudeContextWindow` in `internal/fleettree/model.go`).
The page of 50 before the first message held is read once he reads within 600 px of the top, until none is older.
A reconnect in the same conversation whose newest page has no overlap leaves the history he is reading in place; after Jump to latest, scrolling up can still reach all the missing history.
An older read belongs to its conversation and first-message cursor: if the latest stream state replaces either, its late response or deferred insertion cannot block or release a newer read, change its retry allowance or report a stale error (`frontend/tests/conversation-publication.spec.ts`).
A read that fails is asked again on his next approach to the top, no sooner than five seconds after it, until three in a row have failed, and then not until the stream reconnects.
Ctrl+Plus, Ctrl+Minus and Ctrl+0, the right button held with the wheel, or the wheel with Ctrl and Shift held, size the message text a pixel at a time between 12 and 28 px from 16 px.
The size is kept in this browser as `ceo-message-font-size`, apart from the terminals' size, since a size the terminals take resizes their programs.
Shift+Escape leaves the messages for the panel's first control, as it leaves a terminal, and Escape does what the corner button does.
The messages lie where the terminal deck lies, under the panel's one-row header, beside its task list, in the same flex and under the same rule (`.panel-stage` in `frontend/src/styles.css`), so their box is the terminal's.
Terminal loading and sizing follow [the message box's presentation lifecycle](#the-message-box).
Switching between Modern and Terminal for the selected session keeps its conversation mounted with its stream open and preserves its reading place.
Selecting another session opens at the latest response; the board keeps only the last 50 messages of eight recent sessions, scoped to the board instance and session generation, to paint that session's last known page while its new stream connects, with no inactive view or connection kept alive.
A plain line above the conversation says Connecting until its first server page arrives, including while cached content is shown, and never covers the message box; a session without a cached page waits for that page.
A stream that drops reconnects by itself and says Reconnecting meanwhile.
A stream the supervisor ends says why, and one that drops five times, or that the supervisor refuses, says the messages cannot be read, each with Reconnect.
What the board could not parse goes to the CEO.

#### The message box

Under the conversation is the session's message box, in place of its terminal's input, which shows only in Classic.
The message box types through the native terminal's input link (`frontend/src/terminal-link.ts`), keeping its parser and connection active without opening its visual surface.
Chat parses and acknowledges the replay without claiming the native terminal's size; its input link becomes ready after replay and the initial screen reading, or a bounded fallback if that reading does not arrive.
The first explicit terminal request opens xterm and sizes it to the panel, staging the surface until its repaint completes or the bounded readiness fallback expires.
Returning to Chat detaches the terminal's visual DOM and hides its connecting indicator, removing that surface's `.xterm` element from the page and stopping its `terminal-spin` animation.
Later switches retain the parser, connection and reading position, and an unchanged grid sends no further resize.
Presentation readiness can finish while hidden, so reopening at the same size does not depend on another repaint; a replay already accepted in Chat can also be shown after its connection closes, with the closed notice and no live resize request.
`frontend/tests/orchestration-terminal.spec.ts` covers this lifecycle, and `frontend/tests/messages-view.spec.ts` and `frontend/tests/terminal-steps-in.spec.ts` count first-visible sizing and stable subsequent switches at the relay.
Send, or Enter, types what he wrote as one paste, bracketed as the harness asked, then presses Enter, and Shift+Enter starts a new line.
Clear message, the trash button beside Speech history at the start of the box's row, on a phone (40rem or less) only (the Overlord, 2026-11-03: "trash button only needed on mobile"; #1987), clears all unsent typed or dictated text, every line and the saved draft, in the CEO's and goblins' Modern Chat boxes and any local message box shown in Classic.
It cancels that box's active or pending app dictation so late words cannot restore the cleared text, and returns the focus to the empty field for replacement typing.
In Classic the replacement field stays visible until a Send succeeds or he presses or moves the focus outside the box.
Attachments, Speech history and messages already sent or queued are kept; clearing sends no message and no terminal keys, interrupts no turn and leaves terminal history as it is.
It clears only this box: text already in the native terminal's input is edited there.
Like every control in the box it shows no tip; its accessible description says it clears only this message field.
It is disabled while sending, or when the field is empty with no app dictation pending.
While a turn runs and the box is empty, the send button is Stop, which presses Escape in its terminal, as Esc in the box does; a conversation's box reads the turn from the fleet's stream, as the working indicator does, and a box with no conversation from the board's snapshot and its terminal's screen; with the box's own pane, Speech history or its add menu open, Escape closes that instead and reaches neither the harness nor its screen.
Busy and held sends, and those to a terminal that is not live, connecting, reconnecting or closed, go through the durable board queue for CEO and goblins, so no draft is stranded in the box (2026-11-04); a message refused before typing stays there until the native composer returns.
A message that starts with `!` is shell mode, and while it does the box's outer edge is the board's danger red (#2051).
Send hands it to the board as a `message` action, never typed into the terminal, and the supervisor keeps the rest of the message as `state/shell-commands/<digest>.ps1`, written as a run item's script is, records it in `state/runs.audit` once, and types `! <Windows PowerShell 5.1> -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File <that script>` in its place (`internal/supervisor/shell_messages.go`).
It does so only where it types the message into a terminal: a message handed to the CEO for a goblin that cannot take it, or kept for a paused goblin's resume, goes as he wrote it, and keeps no script.
The harness's own `!` runs that line in its session's working folder, so the command runs natively in Windows PowerShell 5.1 whatever shell the harness's `!` uses, Git Bash for Claude Code included, and the command and what it printed land in the session's conversation as any `!` command does; no shell parses the command, so its quotes, `$` and backticks reach PowerShell as written.
Claude Code, Codex and pi all take the line; Claude Code's `defaultShell` setting and pi's `shellPath` are not used, since Claude Code's prefers PowerShell 7 where it is installed and pi's also changes the shell its agent's own tool uses.
Where the path of Windows PowerShell or of the home's state folder holds a space or a character a shell splits on, no such line can name it, and the message is refused with that reason and nothing runs.
While the harness's own screen says it is in a turn, or a screen of its own is open that is no list to choose from, such as its words for a usage limit, Send types nothing into the terminal and sends a `message` action, which the board holds as his queued entry until the harness's input is empty; it holds on what the screen says, not on the task being under way, which a goblin's is while its harness waits at an empty prompt.
Typed into such a screen, a message stayed on the prompt line unsent and the next one joined it, so his messages reached the harness as one (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "it's sending and appending messages together that were sent previously").
A box with no conversation, beside a terminal or the deck's, holds at such a screen too, and in a turn types into the harness's own queue.
Routine queueing has one neutral note, naming a review hold or why a detached worktree's messages wait, in the words the gate held its typing with, and never a message queued before it is accepted, and a failed Send takes priority, keeps the draft and retries with the same submission identity.
Every notice of the box, that note, a failed Send, Nothing was heard, or what a press on a queued message could not do, shows as a small banner just above the box, laid over the conversation so that neither it nor the box moves, and fades a few seconds after it shows, and a second refusal in the same words shows it again (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "a little banner right above the chat input that is only temporary, goes away slightly and doesn't move the screen up").
The latest notice shows, so a note that starts or changes after a refusal takes its place, and a note that ends, such as Adding the file once the file is in, takes only its own banner with it, never a refusal's, and brings back no note that showed before it.
Each hold of the microphone that hears nothing says Nothing was heard anew.
A note that starts while the box is off screen, such as behind the Task view, shows once he comes back to the box, if it still says the same.
What a press on a queued message could not do is the banner's alone and keeps no box, and so is what the box could not do that was no Send: a key the session could not take, a model, effort or mode it could not set, a file or picture it could not keep, or a queued message it could not take back (#2327).
A screen reader hears each note from a status line in the box that stays as its words change, and each refusal at once.
The same composer stays mounted across Classic and Modern surfaces, keeping pending sends, attachment uploads and recordings through release or reconnect.
The box a terminal's hold, busy program or reconnect shows beside a terminal the board reads no messages for, or that a switch to Classic leaves under the terminal, stays once the terminal takes typing again only for the box's own work, a file adding, a Send landing, a failed Send whose words wait in the box to be sent again, its own recording or the replacement field after Clear message; a recording started in the terminal does not bring it in, and a box that stays sends directly again.
While a screen of the harness's own that lists choices is open in its terminal, as the relay reads it, a question, a permission prompt or a picker, or one the relay could not read, Modern Chat draws it as the conversation's last message, right above the box and in the conversation's one scroll, and never as a popup over the box (#2223), and a box with no conversation draws it above itself; either is a list he picks from (`frontend/src/harness-panel.tsx`): what it asks or shows, each choice as a button with the harness's focus marked, one plain Cancel, which presses Escape, where the footer names Esc or cancel or where nothing is listed, with no row of key chips (the CEO's review, 2026-10-27) and nothing that shows the terminal.
Only a menu his own input asked for is drawn or given his keys: a terminal's host keeps each viewer's submission beside his own terminal-wide mark, which the board's view and a delivery of a message or answer he sent set before they type, and any other writer, a watcher's wake line, a spawn's briefing or a notice the CEO or the supervisor writes, clears both (`history.typedFrom` in `internal/host`), so the screen's `submission` is the human's own while nothing else has typed since, and the box draws no list and routes no key from a panel that mark does not stand for (cg-human-triggered-menus).
What it asks reads as a reply does, with none of the terminal's gutter bars or pane edges: its first row alone in bold as its title, a row joined to the row above it only where the terminal wrapped it, its first word too long for the room the row above left at the width its words wrap at, a pane's own inside its edges or else the terminal's columns as the screen was read less the indent the relay took off the rows, which it sends with the panel (`cols`), each counted in the terminal's cells, a wide character two and a mark such as a variation selector none, every other row its own paragraph, and every row its own paragraph where neither width is known, and a command or preview it quotes, a row indented under its words, one after `$`, rows between two rules of dashes or the indented rows above a lone closing rule, a preview the relay cut at its top, as a code card kept line for line (`frontend/src/panel-words.ts`).
A prompt that opens or changes while he reads up takes the conversation to its end and follows it again, so a key the empty box hands the screen never answers a prompt he cannot see.
One press sends its keys once: the list's buttons wait until the harness has taken it, or for 3 seconds where its screen stays as it was, so a double click or tap reaches the session once.
A choice with a number sends its digit alone, which Claude Code's and Codex's numbered lists take as that choice at once wherever their focus is, and one without a number, as in pi's lists, sends the arrows from the harness's focus to it and then Enter (`frontend/src/panel-keys.ts`); a press on a choice or on Cancel leaves the keyboard in the box.
A screen of words with nothing to choose is not drawn, except a dialog whose footer explicitly asks for Enter: its instructions and result, such as Login successful, show with Continue sending that session's Enter while preserving the draft (the Overlord, 2026-10-28 and #2106).
An empty box hands the screen the arrows, Enter, Escape, Tab, Space and a choice's number, and Send pastes what he wrote into a list of choices as one paste on one line with no Enter, even while the session works, so no key the screen binds acts on it, and the list's Enter confirms.
There is no amber card and nothing steps the terminal in by itself (the Overlord, 2026-10-27: "why am I getting an answer in terminal? Waiting on you in the terminal? Again, all functionality should be available in the modern chat"); for a screen the list does not cover, Terminal in the style menu of the session's picture shows the terminal.
The list is not drawn while Classic shows that terminal, which shows the screen itself, or while typing is held, when Send queues what he wrote as it does under any hold.
A goblin paused, resuming or starting has no terminal that takes typing, and its box sends a `message` action as the box under a terminal does, listing his latest messages to it, each one still queued with Cancel, and those its resume will carry, each with a delete.
While no CEO runs the CEO has that same box, since a message starts it.
Its microphone, held or tapped on a touch screen, and Ctrl+Shift+Space in the box dictate into it, and a click on the microphone, a press shorter than 300 ms whose hold is dropped unheard, or Speech history beside the box's + opens the board's recent dictations, each with Copy and Paste into the message, in Modern and Classic alike (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "what happened to being able to click the microphone and see recent messages").
The active composer owns the microphone and queue note, while a phone terminal keeps Copy terminal text independently available.
An already-running terminal recording keeps one Stop control, on the active composer when a box is shown and on the terminal's own bubble when none is, and a live terminal takes the words its own microphone heard, which Speech history still lists, and holds for pasting back, only where held input refused them.
The box is one bordered field with what he writes on top and its controls in one row along its bottom edge, inside the border, as the Claude desktop app draws its box (the CEO's review of step 3, 2026-10-27): Add files, Speech history and, on a phone, Clear message at its start, then the model by its short, proper name (Opus 5.5, GLM 5.3 Flash), never its raw id, with the effort the session runs beside it in its level's colour, and the microphone.
Send, which is Stop while the session works and the box is empty, sits at the end of the line he writes on, as tall as that line, beside his last line as the box grows (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "place send button in line with message cfo height in chat input").
The box's edge is the edge every pane of the chat wears, whether he writes in it or not.
The box keeps no choice of style and no Open in Windows Terminal, which the panel's header holds (#1987), and the whole row fits a 360 px phone: the model's name shows whole from a 390 px phone up, and on a 360 px phone, whose row also holds Speech history and Clear message, it gives way with an ellipsis.
While he dictates, the microphone is its red dot and level bars, wider than the microphone, and the row still lies inside the border on a phone and on a narrow or wide desktop, with the harness's mark, the usage pies, the recording control and every other button whole: only the model's button gives way where the row has no room, its name first and then its effort, each ending in an ellipsis (the Overlord, 2026-11-03: "Whenever I start the dictation, it gets clipped"; #1987).
Shift+Tab in the box sends the terminal Shift+Tab, which cycles the session's permission mode, and the model's pane shows the mode and sets it.
A `/` at the start of the box lists the session's commands and skills from `/api/commands` as he types the command word, a `$` lists a Codex session's skills alone, the one named exactly that first, behind the sigil he typed before one behind the other, so `/model` picks Codex's `/model` and `$model` a skill of that name, then by name and then by description, and an `@` lists the session's files from `/api/files`, read 120 ms after he stops typing, with the last list for that same `@` kept meanwhile; the arrows move through the one list, Enter sends a picked `/` command whole at once, as a harness's own list runs it in its terminal (#2372), only one whose name starts with what he typed or one he moved to with the arrows, and otherwise sends the box as he typed it, Tab takes the picked entry into the box, as Enter does a picked Codex skill or file, and Escape closes it until the draft changes.
The list is a card above the box, over the conversation, each entry its name and below it, in up to two lines, what it does; it is as tall as the room between the box and the top of what the screen shows, or of the nearest panel that would cut it off, up to 22rem, and scrolls inside itself, so a phone's keyboard never pushes its first entries off the screen (#2013).
A pick goes into the box behind its own sigil, `/kit-one:tidy` or `$alpha-notes`, with a space for its arguments, and a click or a tap takes it without taking the box's focus, while a finger that scrolls the list takes none.
A message to Codex that ends in a `$` word is sent with a space after it, by the board's terminal and by every delivery the supervisor types into a Codex terminal (`Screens.Submittable`), since Codex's own list stays open while its text ends in one and its Enter would complete the word instead of sending the message.
Wherever Modern Chat names a session's harness, the row under a turn's last reply and the model's button, it shows that harness's own mark, Claude Code's, Codex's or Pi's, the one the board draws on its cards (`frontend/src/harness-mark.tsx`), and a harness with no mark of its own its name; the model's button names the session's own model.
Send lights up when there is something to send.
The panel's header draws the session's small picture beside its name (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "I like the avatar and the icon"; #1987 moved the harness's mark into the Task view, as the harness, model and effort in words), and no status line, since the working line in the conversation says what the session does.
A session has one record of its model and effort, a goblin's task record and the CEO's `state/ceo-engine.json`, and every view of the session shows that record and nothing else: the tip of its card's harness mark, the model's button, the Task view's line and its engine selector (the Overlord, 2026-11-10: "It all should be one standard setting, centralized control, just different views"; #2326).
A spawn, `goblins switch`, a task's engine selector and the CEO's engine selector write the record, and the next start or resume of the session reads it.
What the harness itself is told, a `/model` or `/effort` typed in its terminal or sent by the model's pane, reaches the record from the harness's own session record (`internal/fleettree/engine.go`): Claude Code's `/model` and `/effort` results and the model and effort Claude Code 2.1.316 names on each answer, Codex's turn context, and pi's model change, thinking level change and answers.
The supervisor reads it on its read of every live session's tree, and as a session whose messages a board streams says something new, writes what changed into the record (`internal/supervisor/session_engine.go`) and sends the snapshot, so a change made in Classic through the harness's own menu shows on the card and in Modern with no reload, and the board polls for nothing.
Only what the conversation named after the session started counts, since what an earlier run named is what a switch or a resume replaced in the record.
A model Claude Code's picker set with nothing typed after `/model` is printed by its name alone, so it is recorded as the catalog's id for that name, or once an answer names it.
What is typed after `/model`, or sent by the model's pane, is what a start takes, so it is recorded as typed, whatever the record named before: `/model claude-sonnet-5-5[1m]` over a record of `sonnet` records the id and its 1M window, and `/model opus` over `opus[1m]` records `opus`.
A record that names a Claude Code model by an alias, `sonnet` for `claude-sonnet-5-5`, or asks for the 1M context window, `opus[1m]`, whether a spawn wrote it or it was typed after `/model`, stays as written while the session's answers name that model, which they name by its id alone, and becomes another model's id once an answer names one; an id is no alias of another, so `claude-opus-5` does not name `claude-opus-5-5`.
`/model default` names whichever model Claude Code picks, which no start can be handed, so the record keeps what it named until an answer names the model that runs.
An effort no start of the harness takes, such as Claude Code's `auto`, is recorded as `default`, for which a start passes no effort flag.
A message stream's usage names neither the model nor the effort.
A Claude Code CEO started with no `--effort` and never given `/effort` writes its effort nowhere else, so until 2026-11-02 its button showed none (the Overlord: "I can't see the effort level next to the model and the chat input").
A Claude Code or pi model picked in the pane by itself goes in at the default effort `/api/engines` gives it, Opus 5.5 at the fleet's xhigh, whatever effort the session ran before; Codex's own picker keeps the running effort where the model offers it.
The CEO's views show its engine record's model and effort only while the record is what the CEO runs, applied to the harness it runs with no change pending and no problem on it; a selection that waits, could not be applied or names another harness shows under the engine selector alone, and the CEO's card, box and Task view then name its harness alone.
The model's button, and `/model`, Claude Code's `/effort` and pi's `/thinking` typed in the box, open the board's one pane for the session's settings (`frontend/src/engine-pane.tsx`): the models the session's harness offers, the one it runs at the top and checked, each model's efforts in a flyout off the pane's side, beside the model he points at, focuses or taps, without moving the list, and, while the terminal names one, the permission mode, each mode in a colour of its own and with no icon.
The board reads the harnesses' catalog from `GET /api/engines` once, as a box with a model button shows, and keeps it for the page's life, so the pane opens at once; a catalog that could not be read goes to the CEO and is read again by the next box shown or pane opened, or by Retry.
The model it runs takes the keyboard as the pane opens, its efforts out beside it, and an effort command opens it with the running effort taking the keyboard; the arrows move through the choices, right and left move into a model's efforts and back, and a value typed after the command sets it at once.
A press on a model whose efforts are out takes that model, so on a touch screen, which cannot point, the first tap on another model brings out its efforts and a second takes it.
A pick goes in as the harness's own command with the value after it for Claude Code and pi, and an effort picked on another model sends that model's command first and the effort's once the terminal shows the harness took the model, giving up after 8 s and saying so; Codex's `/model` takes no value, so the box sends `/model` and answers Codex's model list and then its reasoning list by their digits, or with Enter for its focused level when no effort was chosen, the harness's list hidden and the terminal never shown, and gives up after 8 s with Escape and says so.
A mode picked in the pane is set as in the terminal (`frontend/src/mode-cycle.ts`): the box presses Shift+Tab once per mode the footer names until it names that one, and stops where it started, saying so, when a whole cycle never reaches it, or after 8 s.
These actions preserve the draft and use the existing native input guards: a missing, reconnecting, closed, unread or held terminal, or a native prompt already open, takes no command, and a `/model`, `/effort` or `/thinking` typed meanwhile stays in the box, never queued, with a note that the model and effort controls need a connected, unheld session.
The board does not invent a model catalog; a pick from the pane shows on the model's button until the session's record says something new, a Codex pick its picker did not take leaves the button, and a value typed after the command shows only once the session's record says the session runs it.
The terminal icon opens the current session in its own Windows Terminal window through the same action as the former panel-header opener, which is absent while this composer is visible.
A Classic view that shows only the native terminal keeps its header opener.
An authenticated phone can request that window on the board's Windows PC; it does not open a terminal on the phone.
The existing session and custody guards remain, and a refused window request says why over the unchanged draft.
A picture pasted into the box, a file dropped on it or one added with Add files goes to `POST /api/composer/file?task=ID&name=NAME`, which keeps it at most 10 MiB in the task's `composer-files` folder, or in the state folder's `composer-files` for the CEO, and answers `{"path":"..."}`.
A paste takes its picture from the clipboard's files, or from its items when the files list is empty, as some Chromium builds hand an image copied from a browser.
An iPhone offers its own Paste in a plain text box only for text, so a copied photo or screenshot never reaches the box there (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "picture pastong dowsnt work on mobike"); on a touch screen Add files opens a menu of Paste a picture, which reads the clipboard once he taps Safari's own Paste, and Photos or files, the phone's own picker with its photo library and camera, and a press outside the menu or on Add files again closes it with nothing added (2026-11-04).
`frontend/tests/iphone-picture.spec.ts` checks both, and the picture above his sent bubble, in WebKit at an iPhone 15's size.
His row in the session's record shows each kept picture as the picture and never its path, whether the record keeps each path on a line of its own or, as a CEO's or goblin's record does with a line the board typed on one line, joined to the end of his words by spaces (the Overlord, 2026-11-02: "when images were pasted, they weren't file paths").
Each kept file shows inside the box's border above his words, a picture as a thumbnail and any other file by its name, each with a Remove button that takes it out of what Send sends; the thumbnails are kept with the draft (`ceo-draft-files:`) and read back through `GET /api/composer/file?token=TOKEN&task=ID&path=PATH`, which serves only a picture inside that task's, or the CEO's, `composer-files` folder and refuses any other path.
Send types his words, then each kept file's path on a line of its own, and the session reads the file from there.
Each harness reads a picture by its path as an image: Claude Code with its Read tool, or as an attached image when the message is pictures alone; Codex with its view_image tool; and pi with its read tool, which is how pi's own paste of a picture names it too.
It takes that road rather than the terminal views' clipboard-and-image-key road because this box also writes to a goblin with no terminal up yet, and every harness reads a path.
A picture the board cannot keep says so over the box rather than disappearing, and Alt+V and Ctrl+V both paste what the clipboard holds there and in a terminal, while Ctrl+C stays the harness's interrupt (the Overlord, 2026-10-26, "alt + v or ctrl + c both should work for paste").
A message of his on its way that the session's record does not hold yet shows at the end of the conversation, in its one scroll, as his own bubble, with no outline and no clock (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: "the double scrolls and having its own little scroll for queued messages doesn't really work").
While the board holds it, it is dimmed, whole, with Queued under it and its three actions (the Overlord, 2026-10-30: "id ratehr it just say qeued"); once its session has it for a turn it is solid with Sent under it and no actions, unless the harness queue records a more specific state, and one typed into a turn that has not taken it stays dimmed with Queued and the same three actions, which act on its harness's own queue (#2332).
Claude Code prompts queued from Terminal or Modern appear as soon as the harness records them: dimmed with Queued while waiting, then solid with Sending after dequeue or removal until the delivered row takes their place, solid with no label.
Sending never outlasts the session's next answer: a prompt the record delivers in no row the board reads as it leaves with that answer.
A ! command typed during a turn is queued by Claude Code as the words after its ! and run at the turn's end as a shell command, so the row that runs it delivers it: it ends that queue entry, names the board message that typed it, and is the take the supervisor waits for (#2242).
A harness queue entry typed in the terminal has no board actions; the board message an entry names, whether the board's snapshot shows it queued, running or not yet, and a send still reaching the board too, since a send keeps its message's id, is that entry's one row with the entry's state, and with the board message's three actions while the entry reads Queued, while a message it does not name keeps its own row and controls.
Codex and pi expose no such harness queue state.
AFK mode changes none of these delivery states or controls.
Nothing about his messages is drawn as a warning (the Overlord, 2026-10-30: "i ahte the yeyllow warnings").
A delivery typed into the terminal with neither a recorded harness queue entry nor evidence of its being taken is not shown as waiting however long the board still watches for a late take, and its outcome stays in the task's Action delivery list (the Overlord, 2026-10-29, of a message from the day before still shown as queued: "that queued message no longer waiting command doesn't make sense").
A message whose delivery ended with no take seen, other than one its goblin could not take that the CEO has, stays with what happened under it until something he sent after it lands, so no message reads as on its way for ever (#2242).
It is drawn among the conversation's rows where he sent it, before the first row written after it or after the last, never under the turns that came after it nor among what still waits at the end, a compaction's line included, and one sent before the first row loaded shows once the page it belongs to is read (the Overlord, 2026-11-13: "Stale old messages that appear randomly when memory gets low", #2541).
One typed and never seen taken may have arrived, so it reads Not confirmed with Send again (an up arrow, which sends its words as a new message) and Dismiss (an X, which takes it off the board through `message_withdraw`, at his word "There's no way to clear em out.", #2541).
One that was not sent reached nobody and is still the board's, so it reads Not sent, why as its description, with Send now (an up arrow, which sends that same message through `message_now`) and Cancel (an X, which takes it off the board through `message_withdraw`) (#2306).
One the board still holds has three icon actions: Send now (an up arrow, which sends `message_now` through `POST /api/actions`, on every message that waits, at his word "send now should be an option with all queued messages": where it cannot go now, such as a gate holding the branch, custody that cannot be read or a prompt line holding words never sent, a press says why over the box and asks nothing), Edit (a pencil, taking it back off the queue into the box, as Up does, before any words he wrote there since, with no edit window of its own), and Cancel (an X, taking it back unsent by the row he picked, a message held for the CEO too).
A press that reaches the supervisor after the board let the message go says at once where it went, in plain words over the box: `It was already typed into the CEO's turn.`, `The CEO already took it.` or `That message is no longer on the board.`, the goblin's name in place of the CEO for a goblin's.
One that lands while the board is trying the message, typing it or finding a moment later that its session cannot take it yet, as a command held for a goblin's turn has it every second, waits for that try to end, for at most ten seconds, and acts on how it ended: an Edit or a Cancel takes back a message the try left queued, and a Send now is kept for its next try (#1915).
Only a try still under way after that says `The board is trying it with the CEO right now. Press again in a moment.`
A goblin's message has Send now from the moment the board takes it, before its first try, so a press in a running turn is kept rather than refused with `It cannot be typed into its session now.`
A press the supervisor refuses is said over the box and goes no further; only one that never had its answer also goes to the CEO through `POST /api/ceo/report`.
A goblin's message goes into its turn at once; one waiting behind a command held for the turn reaches it sooner only by Send now, which presses the harness's send-now key itself once the message is in the harness's queue, and reads sent now only once that key has gone; one whose key never went, as when a screen of the harness's own stayed up, says the goblin takes it at its next tool call.
A command, a line starting with `/` or `!`, that its harness would not run at once beside a turn (`harness.RunsBesideTurn`) waits for its turn to end.
It has its Send now once a try finds it held for that turn, and each press of it tries once to run the command at once with the harness's send-now key (#1915).
A line starting with `$` is words for the session, like any other.
The reason is the bubble's description for a screen reader, not a line of words or a tip, and the box's queue control is a quiet count that reveals the entry rather than a second copy.
Each act goes once however often he presses it (the Overlord, 2026-10-28: "lack of indmepotent click on teh queue"): Send now on one the board holds stays pressed until the message leaves the queue, and a second Send now of a message already sent now is no error, even once it has gone on its way; Edit or Up takes a message back once; and a Cancel taken takes the actions away, its request ID the message's own, so a Cancel sent again from another screen withdraws nothing twice.
The entry shows the moment he presses Enter, before the board answers, at the bottom of the conversation (`frontend/src/pending-sends.ts`), and a send the board refuses takes it back and returns his words to the box as he wrote them.
It becomes an ordinary message in its place once the session's record shows his words as he typed them, a command with its `!`, in a row of his own, never one the CEO or the fleet wrote, whichever of the record and the board's own answer arrives first, and the queue control in the message box offers the same entry and the same three actions.
Each row is one message, the oldest of the same words the board took before the row was written, whether that one is already read or still waits, so a second message of the same words waits for its own row.
A message the board has settled as taken only ever had a row written before it settled, since the board settles it only once it has seen the harness take it, so a later row of the same words is never given to an older message whose own row the conversation has not loaded.
The conversation follows its end, so his waiting messages stay right above the box while the session prints before them.
A message he sends from the box returns the list to its end from wherever he read, so what he sent always shows, and the box keeps the keyboard; a message sent from another screen moves nothing (`frontend/tests/busy-send.spec.ts`).
A waiting message shown while the conversation is read up keeps its actions, and a press it no longer allows, as on one already on its way or gone from the queue, looks disabled and says why over the box, asking nothing (`frontend/tests/conversation-publication.spec.ts`).
A terminal's Alt+V starts no browser paste event, so the board reads the clipboard itself and puts a picture on this PC's clipboard before pressing the harness's own image key.
His draft is kept per session in this tab (`ceo-draft:` and the task's id, or `goblins`, in its session storage), so a switch of view or session, a reconnect and a reload, as an update makes, all find it.
Shift+Escape leaves the box for the panel's first control, as it leaves the messages.
Escape closes Speech history while it is open, from the box or from inside it, and puts the keyboard back on the Speech history button beside the +, which opens it; a press on that button while it shows leaves it shown, and Escape with the keyboard anywhere else, or a press anywhere off it but on a microphone, which opens and closes it itself, closes it too (the Overlord, 2026-11-02: "When you click off the modal for speech dictation it should close").
Its title and its close share one row, the close with room on every side, and its title, each record's words and its foot start at one inset from its edge, with a gap between records (the Overlord, 2026-11-02: "I hate the container padding, the X is squished and each record has no margin in the internal container").

#### The harness's screen

Nothing in a Modern pane brings the session's live terminal into the messages' place: Terminal in the style menu of the session's picture is the one switch of style to it, in the same box with the keyboard in it, and Modern chat the one switch back, which hands the keyboard to the box with his draft as he left it (2026-11-04).
The relay reads the screen and tells the view whether something of the harness's own is open in its input line's place (`Screens.IsOpen` in `internal/harness/screens.go`) and, while it is, the panel it reads there (`harness.ReadPanel` in `internal/harness/panel.go`): its rows, each choice with its number and whether the harness's focus is on it, and the keys its footer names, taken from the screen's last 30 rows below the conversation's last message or rule, or below the row of `▔` Claude Code 2.1.316 draws over the pane its question opens in, so a turn's spinner and the reply streaming above it are never the panel's (the Overlord, 2026-11-02: "Just take the question and make it static so I can choose the selector here, just like I would in Command Center. Remove that terminal garbage."), as in `{"type":"screen",...,"panel":{"rows":[...],"choices":[{"text":"Yes","number":1,"focused":true}],"hints":[...]}}`.
pi draws the turn's status inside its editor's top border while a turn runs, as in `── ⠸ Working ──`, and the count of lines it scrolled past above it, as in `─── ↑ 3 more ───`, so its editor is still the rows between that border and the rule under it: the board reads a draft a person left there and types into an editor it proves empty, in a turn or not, and reads typed text only from those rows, never from the conversation above them (`Screens.RuledBorder`).
The relay carries `working`, read by `Screens.IsWorking`, beside `asking` and `open`, and `mode`, the permission mode Claude Code's composer footer names, as `"mode":"auto mode"` for "auto mode on (shift+tab to cycle)", read by `Screens.Mode` from the screen's last 3 rows that are not blank, its footer, only, so a reply that quotes a mode is never read as one; other harnesses, and a screen whose footer names none, carry no `mode`.
It carries `status`, the gauge and notices the harness draws by its prompt with each notice's `action`, read by `Screens.Status`, and `retry`, the failure and attempt of a turn row waiting to retry, read by `Screens.Retry`, each only while the terminal shows it (#2236).
The relay tells the view again whenever the report's bytes change, so a changed panel or mode is reported.
Each Send marks its submitted input with an ID before typing it into the host, which keeps turn-start evidence for it as it processes output, and the relay reports them as `submission` and `started`.
The board stepped the terminal in by them until 27 October 2026 and, since a screen opens as a list, reads `submission` again as the list's provenance, which decides whether the panel is drawn and given his keys ([the message box](#the-message-box)).
An older terminal host needs a restart to provide this output-screen protocol, and the board refuses the connection before it can accept input so the draft stays intact.
Every key is the terminal's while it shows, Escape included, and Shift+Escape leaves it for the panel's first control.
Sizing on a switch of style follows [the message box's presentation lifecycle](#the-message-box).
Every terminal spec whose terminal a fixture page draws, including `terminal-held`, runs a second time in a Messages view's place, in the board's own stage (the stepped-in project in `frontend/playwright.config.ts`), and the terminal specs that load the whole board run under Terminal on a Codex goblin and on a Claude Code one.
A goblin whose terminal is gone, as one that did not come back after a restart, shows its messages, its box still taking a message, which starts it, and its terminal's own notice is behind them, while its task list names what brings it back.


### The session event stream

One append-only event stream per session is the fleet's one conversation record, so Modern Chat, the family tree and a goblin's panel render one projection of one thing, per harness, instead of three guesses at it.
A session is one harness conversation of one goblin generation, or of the CEO, and its stream lives beside the identity record the fleet already kept for it: `state/conversations/<task>/<generation>/<session>.jsonl`, or `state/conversations/ceo/<session>.jsonl` for the CEO, since the CEO's conversation is its own generation.
No daemon, database or dependency is added; the stream lives where the fleet's other records live.

An event is one thing that happened, in the order it happened, with a stable id: a prompt, the assistant's text as the harness writes it, thinking, a tool call, the result that answers it, a notice, a compaction, an interrupt, the session's own report that a turn runs or ended, the end of a session, and the board's word where a harness cannot provide part of it.
The newest report of a turn is the one the board draws, so a word that a session the board had not yet seen report anything is running supersedes the earlier one that said it could not tell.
A report is written only where it changes the newest word the stream holds of its turn, so a turn a notice set running again after it ended is told again, and a report read again writes nothing.
A harness that rewrites its own record behind the reader, as pi's branch reset drops an abandoned answer, has its record restated in the stream, so a stream that only appends never shows a message its harness has dropped.
A read of the newest page is weighed against everything the stream holds, an earlier supervisor's writing included, from the message where one begins inside the other: a page that slid past messages the stream holds only carries it on, and one that disagrees with it, or shares no message with it, is restated.
Its id is derived from the session, the message and block it belongs to and its own content, so a reader that derives the same events from the same harness record again writes nothing, and content that changed derives another event a reader folds over the one before.
Every event of a turn names the prompt that began it.
`internal/sessionstream` owns the shape and the file; `internal/fleettree` maps the messages a harness's own reader already normalized into those events, and back, so a new harness is one adapter and no reader of the stream changes with it, and Claude Code, Codex and pi produce the same events for a turn.
The harnesses' own transcript readers are the adapters: a harness's record stays the evidence, and the stream is what the fleet agrees on.

The supervisor is the only writer: it appends what its own read of the session produced, the turn's end from the session's own report of it (Claude Code's Stop, Codex's turn completion, pi's settle, which it already keeps as the session's phase), and the fact that a harness reports no turn where its hooks said nothing.
A turn ends too where no hook says so and the session's record does: at an interrupt, or at a reply Claude Code wrote itself in the model's place, as for a usage limit or a failed request, once the session's last report is older than it, and at a session that started again after the turn's prompt, other than by a compaction its harness made, which leaves its turn running; a compaction the Overlord asked for comes between turns, so nothing runs after it (#2292, #2293).
`/api/messages` serves the page and the changes it sends from the stream, so the board renders the stream; a session with no stream yet, or an older page a stream began after, is answered from the harness's own record as it was before streams existed.
A stream that cannot be read is reported in the session event rather than shown as an empty conversation, and the session's own record stands meanwhile.
A stream reaches a bound of 20,000 events, and is then compacted to the newest event of each thing the session did, keeping whole messages; a tool call and the result that answered it are two things, so the call keeps what it was called on and its plan.

The CEO's conversation record, `state/ceo-conversation.json`, is written when its terminal registers and is refreshed whenever the session the harness proves it is in differs from the one recorded, at every read of its messages and once a recovery cycle (`internal/supervisor/ceo_session.go`); a record that names a conversation the session never wrote is reported as stale rather than rendered as current, and the board needs no reload (issue #2172).
What proves the live conversation is the one the harness itself keeps for the fleet's home: Claude Code's record of its own process, Codex's rollout of that home written last, or the pi session of that folder written last, once written since pi started.
With nothing to prove it, the record stands only while the home's own folder holds its conversation, as `fleettree.HoldsTranscript` reads it, and Codex's rollout exists: a record naming a conversation another folder keeps, or one never written, is reported as stale, and the board reads no other folder's conversation as the CEO's.

### Harness updates

A harness keeps running the install it started from, so an update of Claude Code, Codex or pi installed while the CEO or a goblin runs takes effect only when that harness restarts.
Every ten seconds the supervisor reads each running native terminal for one, the CEO's and every live goblin's that is not paused or changing.
An update waits when the harness's own footer under its composer says so, which Claude Code 2.1.292 draws as "✓ Update installed · Restart to update" with a second half its servers may word as "/restart to apply" or "Restart to apply", or when its install was written after the terminal's program started.
For Claude Code that install is `claude.exe`, which its installer writes again on every update.
For Codex and pi it is the package npm installed under the shim `codex.cmd` or `pi.cmd` on PATH, read from its `package.json`, since npm writes a shim only when it links the bin and their own updaters, `codex update` and `pi update`, rewrite the package instead, so a shim npm linked again is no update and a package either route installed is one.
Codex and pi announce only an update that is available, which a restart does not install, so for them the install is the signal; the same words above the composer are the conversation and never count.
While one waits, the snapshot's `ceo_update` puts **Update** on a line of its own under the CEO panel's header row, at its end, and a task's `harness_update` puts it among the goblin's controls, on its card and in its panel.
Nothing restarts until the Overlord presses it: an update waiting only shows the button, AFK mode never presses it, and the CEO never does.
The CEO's press, `POST /api/ceo/update`, is kept in `state/ceo-update.json` for the CEO whose terminal's host started then, and `{"cancel":true}` takes it back.
The supervisor restarts the CEO onto the update through the Restart CEO path, on its own conversation (`claude --resume <id>`, `codex resume <id>`, `pi --session <id>`), once its turn has ended on two readings a second apart: no dialog shows, its composer is empty, no background shell or monitor waits, and either its Stop hook waits on the wake queue, proved by the hook holding `state/.claude-autoarm.lock` under the registered CEO's own harness, or no turn or tool runs.
Until then the button reads Cancel update beside a clock and the CEO's Task view says the CEO restarts when its turn ends; a press for a CEO whose terminal was replaced since is dropped, and a restart that fails says why in a few words under Update for a moment, over the conversation, and leaves the CEO running.
A goblin's press is a pending engine choice with `when` `update` and its own harness, model and effort, shown on its card, which applies at the end of its turn as a deferred switch does and runs `goblins switch <id> --generation <generation> --restart`.
`--restart` starts the goblin's own harness, model and effort again in place while it runs, which a same-values switch otherwise refuses, on its own conversation, `pi --session <id>` for a pi goblin as `claude --resume <id>` and `codex resume <id>` for the others.

### The tab and the installed app

The board's tab shows the goblin mark, from `/favicon.svg` with 16 and 32 px PNGs beside it, and its title counts what waits on the Overlord, such as (2) Code Goblins, dropping the count once nothing does.
Its theme color is the #03050a base, so a browser that tints its bar, such as Chrome on Android, blends into the board.
The supervisor serves a web app manifest at `/manifest.webmanifest` as `application/manifest+json`: Code Goblins, standalone, in the base color, with 192 and 512 px icons and a maskable 512 px icon, so Chrome and Edge offer Install Code Goblins and open the board in a window of its own with no tabs or address bar.
Added to an iPhone's or iPad's Home Screen from Safari, including over the private Tailscale link, the board is an app named Code Goblins that opens without Safari's bars, with its icon at 120, 152, 167 and 180 px, the sizes iOS and iPadOS ask for.
While an installed board is open, its icon carries the same count as the title where the browser badges installed apps, as Chrome and Edge do, and an iPhone does once the app's notifications are allowed.
In his phone's Home Screen app, opened from the private board, the Remote access section has an **App badge** switch where the browser pushes (CEO decision 1008918); the board on the PC has none, since the desktop window and the page tell what waits there.
Turning it on asks for notifications in the app's own words, the system's prompt naming Code Goblins, and only then: the phone app never shows the board's ask for Windows notifications, and claims no alert from the board on the PC.
It subscribes the app to the supervisor's pushes, and the app tells the supervisor of its subscription again each time it opens, subscribing again if the board's key changed, and ending the badge if he turned its notifications off in Settings.
The supervisor then pushes each item newly waiting on the Overlord, in the words the desktop window notifies it, with how many wait, so the icon's count rises and the item shows as a notification while the app is closed; tapping it brings the app up at that item: an app already open is told the item's [board address](#board-addresses) and opens it in place, so what he was typing stays, and otherwise the app opens at that address (#2363).
A count that falls is corrected when the app opens, never by a push, since iOS changes a badge only with a notification (CEO decision 1008953).
It pushes nothing else, nothing under AFK mode, nothing for what already waited when the badge turned on, and nothing for a goblin's question the CEO left, which counts but, as on the page, never alerts.
The pushes go through the browser's own push service (Apple's for an iPhone), encrypted so only the app reads them (RFC 8291), padded to one size, and signed with the board's VAPID key (RFC 8292), which `push.json` in the state folder keeps, readable by its owner alone, with each subscribed app; the supervisor sends only to Apple's, Google's, Mozilla's or Microsoft's push service, and forgets an app its service dropped.
Turning the switch off unsubscribes the app, and turning remote access off forgets every app at once, as for a phone he lost.
The PNG icons under `/assets/icons/` are rendered from `/favicon.svg`, the smaller Home Screen icons from the 180 px one; render them again whenever the mark changes.

### Board addresses

A board address opens one place on the board (#2363, approved 2026-11-11 from the study closed as #2359): `/?task=<task id>` opens that goblin's panel, and `/?item=<key>` opens one Command Center item by the key the board and its pushes give it, such as `question:<id>` or `review:<id>` (`frontend/src/board-address.ts`).
It is the board's own origin and root with that one parameter and no other, so any other address, another parameter beside it included, is not a board address.
The board opens only a goblin its snapshot holds, at work or finished, or an item that waits on him, and only opens it: nothing it names is answered, started or stopped.
Any other address opens nothing and says nothing, as the board keeps its words about trouble to the few the Overlord approved.
The page reads its own address as it loads, once its first snapshot comes, and then puts its address back to plain `/`, so a reload opens nothing again (`frontend/src/use-board-address.ts`).
A board address in a message, in a goblin's or the CEO's terminal, or in a tapped push opens its place in the page already open, with no reload and no new tab, so what he was typing stays: in a browser, in the desktop window, which hands such a link to no browser, and in his phone's app.
A link in a Scrawl page or anywhere off the board opens a board at the address, which opens the place as it loads.
No protocol handler is registered.

### The desktop window

A desktop window for the board, `goblins-window.exe`, is the program in `cmd/goblins-window`: a Wails v3 window on Microsoft's WebView2 around the board root the supervisor serves, never linked into `goblins.exe`, which every hook runs.
`go build -trimpath -tags production -ldflags "-H windowsgui" -o goblins-window.exe ./cmd/goblins-window` builds it as a release and `-Dev` do, and it accepts `--board <the board's address> --state <the home's state folder>`.
Without `-tags production` the window has WebView2's developer tools and the browser's menu on a right click, which is how to look into the board's page while working on the window.
`goblins --window` starts it that way where it sits beside `goblins.exe`, after finding or starting the supervisor as `goblins --board` does, with its output appended to `state\window.log`; a window of the same home already running takes the start as its second instance and comes to the front.
Each home has a window of its own: the home the user's own environment names, by `GOBLINS_HOME` or else the per-user home, keeps the one instance and Windows' one notification registration for Code Goblins, and a window on any other home, such as a scratch home or a second install, is an instance of its own, keyed by its state folder, so opening it brings its own board to the front and never the first home's window.
`goblins --window --background` starts it in its tray, and where no window sits beside `goblins.exe` either exits 1 and says so.
Started with neither `--board` nor `--state`, as the Start menu, desktop shortcut and **Start at login** start it, the program runs the `goblins.exe` beside it with `--window --locate` in a hidden console.
`goblins --window --locate` finds or starts the supervisor and prints only the board's address followed by the state folder, one per line; it opens no window, browser or CEO.
The original window uses those two lines to show the board itself, or stays in its tray if it was started with `--background`.
A window started that way has no standard error of its own, so once it knows the state folder it appends its output to `state\window.log` too: each window's first line there names its process id, and what it says after, Wails' fatal messages and the words of a Go crash follow it, so a window that ends unexpectedly leaves its reason beside the supervisor's `serve.log`.
Before it knows the state folder there is no log to keep, and a window that cannot find its board says why in a message box instead.
The supervisor takes the home's own window for the Overlord's by the program it runs, whoever opened it ([ownership proof](#afk-mode)), so its AFK switch and **Update** work however it was started.
That covers a window the desktop shell started, one `goblins --window` started from a terminal of his own after that launcher has exited, one the installer started, and one the supervisor moved onto an update.
A window started from an agent's terminal or under an agent harness carries that mark and is refused.
So a supervisor is started in one way only, by `goblins`, whoever opens the app.
A click on a notification while no window runs opens the app the same way: Windows then starts the program alone, with `-Embedding`.
COM starts that window with no parent at the desktop, which the proof by its program does not need.
Quit it from the tray and open Code Goblins from the Start menu or desktop shortcut to use those controls.
When `goblins` fails, the launcher shows the last sixteen lines it wrote to stderr in a message box titled Code Goblins and exits 1; with no `goblins.exe` beside it, the box says that Code Goblins is not installed there.
If `goblins` exits 0 without naming both the board and state folder, as an older build may, the window shows a message box saying the board did not open and exits 1 without showing a board.
Sixteen lines hold all that `goblins` says about a supervisor that did not start: what to do, where `serve.log` is, and the end of that log.
For installation from source with `.\install.cmd -Dev`, see [To work on it](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/docs/install.md#to-work-on-it).
The one-line install puts it in the home from a release whose `SHA256SUMS` lists it, as releases from v0.4.0 on do, and `goblins update` carries one that sits beside the candidate into the home once the candidate serves.
It needs only what the supervisor already provides: the board's address in `state\board.json`, the board's page, `/api/waiting`, which it reads every 3 seconds for what newly waits on the Overlord, and `/api/announce`, where it claims each of those before it notifies it.
It holds no fleet state, and closing it leaves the supervisor, the CEO and every goblin running: it runs as a single instance, closing hides it to the tray, and only **Quit the window** in the tray menu ends it.
It follows the supervisor to a new address, loads the board again when a supervisor that was down answers, and opens in the default browser every way off the board the page takes: a link that opens a new tab, a link that opens in the same tab and a script that sets the page's location alike, so the window always shows the board.
A link it cannot open, such as an address of another scheme or one the window could not hand to the default browser, is said in the board with its address to copy, until it is closed.
A PDF preview's browser-local `blob:` address opens only inside the board's page, so the window refuses it without a word, and the card's Download PDF stays the way to the file.
While it is shown, it asks its page at every look whether it is the board's and has drawn a frame, since a page can stop drawing with nothing else changing: on 2026-10-14 WebView2's GPU process was slow to start, and the window showed only its dark ground while its page stayed loaded.
A page silent for three looks in a row is loaded again, then, still silent, the window ends its own WebView2 GPU process, which WebView2 starts again, and it tries both again a minute later, never in a loop.
Meanwhile Windows itself draws "The board stopped drawing. Loading it again." in place of the page, and `window.log` records each step.
It raises the board's alerts as Windows notifications only while it is minimized or hidden to the tray; on the screen the board's Open Command Center is the signal, and nothing is claimed.
Its notifications are the board's: only a new Command Center item that asks the Overlord something, never a goblin blocked, failed or done.
Its own look at the board, every 3 seconds, finds those items in `/api/waiting`, so a notification does not wait on the hidden page, which Windows runs at its lowest priority.
That route answers the items alone, read from the supervisor's memory with each goblin named as the newest snapshot names it, and builds no snapshot: on 2026-10-28 the look read the whole snapshot, 984 KB, every 3 seconds (#1716).
Each is titled with who asks, a goblin by its title without its harness or the CEO, and says the item on one plain line as the page does: a question's lead, a wait's reason without its queue prefix or page link, without Markdown marks or a table of values.
It claims each under the key the board's page uses, `alert:` and the item's key with its `created_at`, after the 1.5 seconds a hidden tab waits, and notifies only what the supervisor hands it, so nothing in AFK mode; a supervisor that cannot be asked lets it notify, as it lets the page.
A click on any of its notifications brings the window to the front and opens that item in the Command Center, one it raised from its own look as well as one the page raised: the page hears of the first as the `code-goblins-notification-click` event.
Each notification carries the goblin from `goblins-window.png`, which every start writes beside the program and names as `IconUri` under `HKCU\Software\Classes\AppUserModelId\Code Goblins`.
A picture that went missing while the window ran is put back before the next notification, and a notification goes out without it when it cannot be kept.
A window started with `--profile`, as its tests start it, or on a home other than the user's own registers for no notification, raises none and claims nothing from its board, since Windows hands a click on any Code Goblins notification to the program that registered last.
**Start at login** in the tray menu writes the `CodeGoblins` value under `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run`, and clearing it removes the value.
It is one setting with the board's switch, `POST /api/start-at-login` with `{"on": true}` or `false`, the setup's box and `goblins install --start-at-login on|off`: each keeps the choice in `state\start-at-login`, and the tray reads the value again every 3 seconds, so it shows a change made on the board.
An install sets the value unless that choice is off, and removes this home's value when it is; a value that starts another home is left as it is, while one whose program is gone, such as a window of a home that moved or was removed, is set to this home's.
For a window that `goblins` started or that located its board when started alone, the value runs the window alone with `--background`, which locates the board as above, so the supervisor starts before the window and no terminal shows at sign-in; `goblins uninstall` removes the value where it starts a program in that home, and an install rewrites it to this home's where it started a copy of the window installed on its own.
The install writes the window alone only where it put the window in the home itself, from a release that ships it or, with `-Dev`, from the build it just made; where the home only kept a window it already held, the install writes that home's `goblins.exe --window --background`, which opens any window.
An install that retained the home's window keeps the earlier standalone window, its picture, folder and Start-menu entry unchanged.
A value an earlier window wrote, which ran the `goblins.exe` beside it with `--window --background` in a terminal at every sign-in, is rewritten to this one the next time `goblins` starts the window, so the tray shows **Start at login** as on and clearing it once removes the value.
The board's dictation works in it as in a browser tab, because the page records through the browser engine's own audio worklet and the supervisor recognises the sound; WebView2's speech recognition, which has no service behind it, is not used.

### Restarts

A restart or a sign-out ends the supervisor and every `goblins host`; [Start at login](#the-desktop-window) brings back the board with its supervisor detached from any terminal.
The supervisor then brings back what the sign-in before this one left running, the comeback, recorded in `state\comeback.json`.
It reads when this sign-in began from Windows (`LsaGetLogonSessionData` for the process's logon session), since a restart and a sign-out both end every process of the sign-in before.
The first supervisor of a sign-in plans the comeback once, at its first memory check and before memory is read, so a sign-in whose memory never reaches the floor still records itself.
It plans the registered CEO when it no longer runs and ran in the sign-in before (at or after the last comeback's sign-in and before this one), by its terminal's host record or by its registration, or when an earlier comeback had not reached it.
It plans every live native goblin left meant to run whose terminal no longer answers, whatever its host record says, except one whose record names a terminal started in this sign-in, which the restart did not end.
A host removes its record when its terminal's program ends before it does, and a restart ends both in no fixed order: on 2026-10-14 Ursula's host removed hers, and a comeback that planned only goblins with a record in the sign-in before left her behind while Wally and Ned came back.
A goblin is left meant to run when its lifecycle record for its session says running, or says a resume was cut short with no controller left to finish it, before or after it published its new session.
A goblin whose task the board shows done or merged for its session, whose latest report of its session is `done` with a pull request, or with the issue it closed or the report it kept in place of one, and that took no prompt since, which waits on its merge or on nothing, that was paused or stopped, whose pause or stop was cut short, whose failed pause took hold once its terminal ended, whose resume failed and waits for a Resume, or that is retired, is not planned.
A failed resume whose goblin reported from the session the resume started is finished as running before the plan reads it, so that goblin is planned.
A home with no comeback record has no sign-in before to compare with, so its first supervisor plans nothing and only records this sign-in; a supervisor that starts again in the same sign-in, as an update restarts it, carries on with what still waits.
Each memory reading, once a minute, takes the comeback's next step: the CEO at the first reading at or above the 4 GB floor, then the next goblin once memory and commit both read at the 5 GB start mark twice in a row, memory and commit less the about 1 GB its session takes still meet the floor the machine's heavy line keeps for its next test run, as for a start the scheduler makes, and a launch has room as a spawn's does (`CheckLaunch`: memory and disk; no count of goblins), one at a time.
While anything waits to come back nothing else starts by itself: the scheduler's queued starts, paused goblins' automatic resumes and `memory_ready` wait.
A Start or Resume the Overlord presses on the board, or a `goblins spawn` the CEO chooses, is still allowed.
The CEO comes back as `goblins` brings back a closed one, under `.ceo-launch.lock`, by the rule Restart the CEO keeps: on its registered conversation, and on a new one where it has none to resume, its context is past 400K tokens or its resumed terminal does not hold, with its startup dialogs answered and a first instruction that says the machine restarted, so nobody types into it.
One that does not come back is left closed with the reason, which the board's line names, and Reopen on its bar tries again.
The CEO that comes back holds the home whichever session starts first after the restart: another Claude Code session, such as one the desktop app reopens, takes no lock and gets no wake, and one that holds the lock from an older build loses it to the CEO at the CEO's next hook ([The CEO's own session](contract/supervision.md#the-ceos-own-session)).
Each goblin comes back as `goblins resume` brings one back, through the in-place switch on its own conversation where the board's record or the conversation its running harness proved (kept in `state\conversations\<id>.json` for its generation) proves it its own and from a handoff where neither does, with one more line in its instruction: that the machine restarted, which ended its terminal, and to continue where it left off.
One whose resume the restart cut short comes back through that resume, finished as its card's Resume would, so its record reads running again, with the same line.
One that cannot come back is recorded stopped with the reason on its card, as its action error, and raises a `check` wake keyed by its id that names `goblins switch <id> --harness <its harness>`; the next goblin's turn comes at the next reading either way.
The snapshot's `comeback` carries the record for the board's line, and each waiting or stopped goblin's card carries its own entry; the monitor's wake for a terminal with no host says the supervisor brings it back rather than naming `goblins resume`, whatever the goblin last reported, and for one the comeback does not hold whose latest report is `done` with a pull request, or with the issue it closed or the report it kept in place of one, it says that a restart leaves it down unless a prompt reached it since, and that `goblins switch <id> --harness <its harness>` brings it back if it has more work.
An example board brings nothing back.

<a id="a-ceo-whose-terminal-ended"></a>

### A CEO whose terminal ended

Between restarts, the supervisor brings back a CEO whose terminal ended while nobody asked it to stop, as when its harness crashes or ends itself after an update, by itself and within about ten seconds.
On 2026-10-16 and again on 2026-10-17 the CEO's Claude Code ended with exit code 1 right after a turn ended, in conversations of 634 MB and 404 MB, and the CEO stayed closed, the first time for 16 hours, until the Overlord pressed Reopen and typed its first instruction into it.
Every host records how its terminal's program ended in `state\hosts\<id>.end`, beside its record and outlasting it until the terminal's next program ends: the host by pid, the program by pid and creation time, when, its exit code, and whether a viewer closed the terminal.
A launch waits for its host's record, and a host whose program ends at once can serve and remove its record between two of the launch's looks, as the CI stand-in harness of `TestANativeCEOStartsTheHarnessTheLauncherFindsNotTheOneOnTheUsersPath` did by chance (#1243): an end that names the host the launch started says it served, so the launch succeeds as it would have a moment sooner, and a host that ended without one fails the launch with its exit status and the end of `state\hosts\<id>.log`.
Every ten seconds the supervisor looks at a closed CEO (registered, its process ended, no terminal up) whose program started in this sign-in, since one of the sign-in before is the comeback's.
An end somebody asked for stays: a terminal closed through its host, as Restart the CEO, its Update, a harness switch and the uninstall close it (the uninstall stops the supervisor before the terminals in any case), and a harness that exited with code 0, as the Overlord's own exit does.
Any other end brings the CEO back: a non-zero exit code, and a host that ended with its program without recording how, which a killed host does.
A CEO whose terminal a host from before this build ran has no end record, so its own exit right after the update that installs this build brings it back once, since its host recorded nothing of how it ended.
The end record names the program that ended, so a CEO that ended before it registered is read too.
The supervisor brings it back through `Comeback.CEOEnded`, which `goblins serve` answers as the comeback after a restart does: under `.ceo-launch.lock`, as the remembered agent, on its conversation where that is small enough and on a new one where not, with its startup dialogs answered.
While a live process holds `.ceo-launch.lock`, as Restart, Reopen and `goblins` do while they start the CEO, the supervisor neither reads, counts nor spends an end, so a resume that start tries and that ends is that start's, and the next look sees the CEO it brought up or its own failure as a newer end.
A Claude Code conversation is resumed only once Claude Code has written its transcript, which it writes as the conversation's first turn begins: a home's first CEO, which starts with no prompt, has none until someone types into it, and Claude Code 2.1.302 ends with code 1 ("No conversation found") on a `--resume` of it, 3.6 seconds after it starts, longer than the three seconds a resume is given before it is called held.
So a resumed CEO whose terminal ends while its startup dialogs are answered did not hold either, and starts on a new conversation, as `goblins` already did; a live proof on a scratch home on 2026-10-17 met both before they were fixed.
Its first instruction names why it is back, such as `its terminal ended with exit code 1, after an update of Claude Code was installed while it ran`, where the harness's program was installed after that program started.
Each end is acted on once: `state\ceo-back.json` records the program whose end was acted on, before the CEO starts, so a supervisor that dies meanwhile never starts it twice, with why the CEO did not come back when it did not.
A CEO that keeps ending soon after it started is not restarted in a loop.
An end of a CEO that ran under ten minutes is one more in a row when that CEO started within ten minutes of the row's last end, and starts the row again when it started later; an end of one that ran longer empties the row.
So a long run before the ends never counts, and a short end long ago never joins a later one; at the third in a row the supervisor brings nothing back.
It raises one `check` wake keyed `goblins` that names the ends and the last reason, for the next CEO, and makes one Command Center item for the Overlord in plain words, such as "The CEO's terminal ended 3 times in a row, each within 10 minutes of its start, with no more than 10 minutes between one end and the next start, the last time because its terminal ended with exit code 1, so the supervisor stopped bringing it back. Reopen the CEO once you have looked?", whose one action is the board's own Reopen the CEO.
A comeback that fails without a newer end spends its end too, so it leaves the CEO stopped the same way, with the same wake and item, such as "The CEO did not come back after its terminal ended with exit code 1: claude is not on PATH. Reopen the CEO once you have looked?".
A CEO it started that ended during startup left a newer end instead, which is one more end in the row: the next look brings the CEO back or stops at the cap, so one incident raises one wake.
The item runs no command, and the supervisor refuses to run or skip it as one; it is made while none waits, and taken back once a CEO runs again.
While an update of the home installs, nothing is brought back, since the update stops the supervisor and swaps the programs a start runs from.
An example board brings nothing back.

### Whose a process is

Every process a terminal starts stays tied to that terminal, and three kinds of evidence say so, because no one kind survives everything a process can do.

| Evidence | What carries it | What loses it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The terminal's job | Everything the harness starts itself, a process whose parent has exited included | A process Git Bash starts, since the MSYS runtime leaves the job, and one that asks to leave |
| The terminal's mark | Every Windows program, wherever it works and whatever became of its parent | A program one of Git Bash's own tools starts when it is such a tool itself, which has no Windows environment |
| Its place | Any process at work in the task's folders or running a program from them | A process that works anywhere else |

The mark is the proof value the terminal's host puts in the terminal's environment as `GOBLINS_HOST_PROOF`, beside `GOBLINS_HOST_ID`.
A terminal is given a new value each time it is started, resumed or switched, and what it started under an earlier one is still its own, so the host keeps the SHA-256 of each value given since the machine started in `state\hosts\<id>.proofs`, which outlasts the host's record.
A pause and a stop end every process that is the task's own by any of the three, and every process such a one started.
