The stop holds each terminal's job itself before it ends the host, so the job is still read, and what it holds still ended, once the host is gone.
Measured on 2026-10-09 with 11 goblins and the CEO running: of 77 marked processes, 19 were outside their terminal's job, 10 of those reached no host through their parents, and one had lost its parent 2 hours and 39 minutes before.
On the same day two browser bridges like those held 3.7 GB while five goblins were paused for memory.

Three kinds of process are never a task's by its mark or its parent, whatever started them.
A machine service, with what runs under it: Docker Desktop, the no-mistakes daemon, and the Scrawl server that keeps every goblin's review page.
A gate's agent, which carries the mark of whichever goblin started the daemon while it works for any gate. It is a task's only while it works in that task's own gate run.
And a desktop program, with what it started: a program that shows a window, a browser no tool drives (one started with none of `--headless`, `--remote-debugging-pipe`, `--remote-debugging-port`, `--enable-automation` or Firefox's `-headless` and `--marionette`), a packaged desktop app and Explorer.
A goblin can start one for the Overlord, as when a sign-in opens his browser, and it is still his to close.
When the desktop's windows cannot be read, or the desktop shows none, as a hidden desktop a process started on shows none, nothing is a task's by its mark or its parent, only by its job and its place.

The janitor's hourly sweep applies the same rule to what no pause or stop ended.
For each terminal the home gave a proof since the machine started, it reads which processes are that terminal's own.
A terminal with no host left has its processes ended: a goblin that failed, and a pause, a stop or a cleanup whose own sweep ran out of time.
A teardown's own sweep runs one priority class above normal while it reads and ends, and gives the class back after, so it runs out of time far less: at the priority of the work it ends, each of its readers waits its turn behind that work.
A cleanup and a relaunch (a switch, a resume, a comeback after a restart of the machine) end what their goblin's last terminal left themselves, once that terminal has ended and, for a relaunch, before the next harness starts: afterwards the next harness would be the task's own by the same folders and the same terminal's proofs.
The next harness cannot reach what the last one left in the background, so a dev server left running would hold its port against the one the goblin starts again.
These two leave one thing a pause ends: a process at work in the task's folders that carries no mark of its terminal and has a parent that still runs and is not the task's.
Somebody else runs it there, as when the CEO tests a goblin's worktree or the Overlord has a shell open in it. A pause stops a task where it stands, and a cleanup or a relaunch ends only what nothing else accounts for.
A relaunch whose sweep fails starts the harness all the same, and what it missed shows under the goblin on the board until its next pause, stop or cleanup, or until it has sat idle for an hour.
A terminal that runs keeps what its host still reaches through its parents.
A tree that does not reach it is detached, as every background command a harness starts through Git Bash is, and the tail of every pipeline.
Nothing the sweep can read says which of those a goblin still waits on, so nothing of a goblin that works is idle, however still it sits: a gate waiter that blocks for hours, a watch on a quiet file, a browser bridge it will drive again.
A goblin works unless both hold: the last outcome it reported is `done` or `failed`, and the monitor reads it at its prompt with no turn in progress and nothing asked (`state\monitor\tasks`). The CEO's terminal and a run item's always work, and so does a goblin whose state cannot be read.
A detached tree of a goblin that rests is watched by its own processes, each with the processor time it has used, kept in `state\janitor.json`. It did something when a process joined it or left it, or when the processes it still holds used a tenth of a second between them. It is ended once the goblin's rest and the tree's stillness have both lasted an hour.
The sweep never adds a tree's processor time up: the sum falls when a process that used the processor exits, and on 2026-10-10 a dry run against this machine showed a long test run, between two of its test programs, reading as idle by it.
A loop that looks at something once a minute starts a process each time and is left alone.
The sweep reads every process on the machine and judges only those of the terminals in its own home's `state\hosts`, so another home on the machine, a scratch home of a test among them, is never touched.
`goblins process-plan` prints what the sweep would do now and ends nothing: what it would end, what it would end an hour on, what it would name and what it would leave, each with its rule, and what a cleanup or a relaunch of each task would end.
It is the sweep's own plan, taken with a reader that forgets no proof, so it writes nothing into the home, and it reads the command that asked and that command's ancestors like any other process, which a teardown never does.
What nothing proves is named for the CEO and never ended: a browser bridge with no mark whose parent is gone, and a gate agent's process whose gate is gone.

A browser bridge belongs to one owner.
chrome-devtools-axi keeps one bridge and one browser for each session name, and a terminal that names none shares its unnamed session with every other program of the user, so every goblin's terminal starts with `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_AXI_SESSION` set to its task id and the CEO's with `goblins`, whatever the user's environment named.
A goblin started by a supervisor with a board also gets `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_AXI_BROWSER_URL`, so its bridge drives the goblin's own browser, which the supervisor runs and the board shows (see [A goblin's own browser](#a-goblins-own-browser)), and starts no browser of its own.
The bridge is started with its terminal's environment, so it carries the terminal's mark and ends with it.
The tool hashes a session name to one of 1,000 ports, so two tasks can be given the same port. The second bridge then fails to start, and its goblin sets `CHROME_DEVTOOLS_AXI_PORT` for itself.
The tool removes no session's folder. The janitor removes one under `.chrome-devtools-axi\sessions` once the bridge its `bridge.pid` names no longer runs and nothing wrote to it for a day, and only when it holds nothing but the tool's own files.
A bridge keeps its page drawing whether or not anything drives it: three left on 2026-10-09 used most of a processor between them. So the sweep judges a running terminal's bridge by when its session was last used, the newest write among the tool's files in the session's folder, and ends it with its browser after an hour unused.

### Interface rules

These rules hold for every board surface, and new work follows them.
Recurring tool actions (open in VS Code, open folder, open pull request, refresh, zoom, fit, arrange, close, reconnect) are icon buttons, each naming itself with an accessible label and a tooltip on hover and keyboard focus.
Decisions and one-off commands keep a short word, for example Send decision, Retry or Show the next 300 lines.
Every connector, harness and model provider shows a mark beside its name: a connector its service's logo, or its initial on a tile where the board bundles none, and a harness or a model provider its brand's mark from Simple Icons where one exists and a plain glyph where the owner withholds its mark.
Delivery reads as a mark: one check once the supervisor accepted it or while it is sent and waits for its reader to take it, two checks once delivered; only a delivery that failed or never arrived is spelled out, with what to do.
An answer to a question or on a review item, to a goblin or to a CEO, and a `goblins answer` to a goblin, that arrives while it is working, such as inside a long tool call, is held by its harness and handed over at its next tool call, never interrupting the call that runs, and nothing but the harness's own record of the conversation shows it taken (a prompt hook runs as the harness queues it), so it is sent until that record shows it; a `goblins send` steer to a working goblin prints `queued for <id>`, and its board receipt shows only once the goblin's record shows it, while a run result or a review request sent to a working CEO reads sent until the CEO's record shows it.
A review answer's own action keeps one check, because it succeeds whether the answer reached the goblin or went to the CEO; only its review item says which.
Status words say what is happening in plain words, such as Working, In review gate, Waiting on you, Waiting on the CEO or Merged, verifying, never the evidence the supervisor holds.
Text is never smaller than 15 px.
Every surface wears SIQstack's glass from SIQshift's brand stylesheet: a 135-degree green-to-blue tint over the #03050a base, a green hairline border and a deep shadow with a green top highlight; a hovered card brightens its border and glows faintly, and what floats, such as a dialog or a menu, is the same glass laid on the solid #04060a surface so nothing behind it shows through; a tip has its own solid surface, described with the cards' tips.
The tokens live once, in `frontend/src/styles.css` `:root`, under SIQshift's and SIQstack's own names.
A selected card has a solid green edge and glow, and keyboard focus is SIQstack's 3 px blue ring, so a focused control never reads as selected.
The active tab of a pill switch carries SIQstack's green, blue and purple ring; primary buttons are SIQshift's solid green pill.
The goblins' dialogue boxes, the lantern box, keep their stepped pixel frame, hard shadow and pixel type in SIQstack's colors from its Code Goblins page, and add a green glass hairline that follows their stepped frame, with a faint green glow around it: the lantern palette in `frontend/src/styles.css` is SIQstack's card `#0c1219` (`--hide`), its mint `#65efcd` (`--lantern`), its text `#edf4f3` (`--bone`) and the board's mint edge `#2f7d66` (`--edge`), and a lantern button is SIQstack's green gradient from `#00e59b` to `#63f3d0` (the Overlord, 2026-10-11: "keep lantern styling but be siqstack styled").
Lavish's copy of the board's tokens (`board-tokens.css`) follows them at its next sync.
The board has one dark theme, and status colors keep their meaning in it: working blue, waiting and next amber, failed red, merged purple, done mint.

<a id="deliberate-ceo-questions"></a>

## Deliberate CEO questions

Worker alerts and natural-language questions stay with the CEO, including a goblin's blocked notify that offers choices.
The registered CEO must deliberately publish a decision from its own process ancestry:

```powershell
goblins question --id layout-choice-001 --text "Which layout should I use?" --option "Compact" --option "Spacious" --option "Keep current" --recommend "Spacious"
```

Omit `--option` when the question needs a written answer.
`--recommend` must exactly match a supplied option, which the modal shows first with its real recommendation; omit the flag when no option is recommended.
`--for <task>` names the goblin whose decision the question puts to him, which the fleet must know: while the question waits, that goblin's waits on him must declare whether they are this decision, and one it declares with `goblins notify --follows <this question's ID>` is hidden while the question waits and closed by its answer, never a card of its own.
The Supreme Overlord Command Center labels supplied choices A/B/C and always offers Other for a written answer.
The box for a written answer has a microphone at its bottom right corner, and Ctrl+Shift+Space held anywhere in its card, as on the Other just chosen, dictates into the box and puts the caret there: until 15 October 2026 only the box itself heard the keys, and choosing Other leaves the keyboard on the choice, so a dictation held there was never typed.
On a pending question the box also takes a picture pasted into it, a file dropped on it and one picked with Add files at its bottom left corner, which on a touch screen opens Paste a picture and Photos or files, as the message box does (#2206).
Each is kept through `POST /api/composer/file` in the asking goblin's `composer-files` folder, or the state folder's for the CEO, and shows above his words with a Remove button.
Send waits while a file is still being kept, and the answer is his words, then each kept file's path on a line of its own, which the asker reads the file from.
A question, the CEO's or a goblin's, is shown as body text at a readable line length rather than as a heading: a blank line starts a paragraph, a line that starts with `- ` is a bullet, text between `**two asterisks**` is bold, an http or https address, or a Markdown link to one, is a link as [The Messages view](#the-messages-view) describes (punctuation ending its sentence is not part of it), and everything else but a block it fences, markup included, is shown exactly as written; no HTML is ever interpreted.
So a question leads with one short sentence that is the actual question, puts its details on `- ` lines and bolds only the verdict or the blocking item.
What follows a line that reads only `Details:` is its evidence, such as run IDs, finding codes and receipts: the card shows the decision above it and folds the evidence under a Technical details disclosure, which opens with a click, a tap, Enter or Space, tells a screen reader whether it is open, mounts its content only while open, and stays open or closed across board refreshes, while the stored question, the CEO's wake and History keep the whole text.
A question without that line, or with nothing on one side of it, shows whole as its asker wrote it; nothing guesses where its evidence starts.
A block a question fences between lines of three backticks reads as terminal text does in [The Messages view](#the-messages-view), on either side of that line (#2524).
One whose fence names a shell, `powershell`, `pwsh`, `cmd`, `bash`, `sh` or `zsh` in any case, is a command under that shell's name, and any other, such as what a command printed or the prompt it stopped at, is output.
A local path in either is a link to the whole path, as one in the question's words is, drawn in its line's own type with its underline and folder and wrapping after its separators as the block's words do.
`--message-file <file>`, on `goblins question` or a goblin's `--blocked` question with choices, gives the question a message he sends himself, such as an email, which the question holds as `mail` and its card shows under its text as a [step card](#step-cards) does.
The inbox and history list each question on at most two lines, with the marks dropped.
No choice is preselected and written text is sent only when Other is selected.
Use a new stable ID for a new question, and keep the same ID/content for an uncertain publication retry.
The publisher walks up to 32 process ancestors and verifies the registered CEO PID, creation time and live native identity; a worker cannot escalate on the CEO's behalf.
For a CEO in a native terminal, a publisher whose chain of parents stops short of the CEO is proven instead by the terminal's proof value, as for a goblin's question.
The board's stream, `GET /api/events`, opens with a `snapshot`, the whole board, and after it sends only `change` events, each what changed since the event before.
A snapshot is read from the fleet's files, so the supervisor builds each away from the stream.
One build serves everyone who asks while it runs: every open board, `/api/snapshot` and the ticket keeper share it, so one change costs one build however many readers there are, and one refresh every 15 seconds serves them all.
A build opens a fleet file only when its size or its modification time changed since the build before, and otherwise keeps what the file gave then; it asks the disk about each path once, and learns of the task records, lifecycle records and monitor records from one listing of each folder.
On 2026-10-02 a build opened 447 files at the live fleet's size and took 3 to 13 seconds on the fleet's machine, where opening a file cost 20 to 80 ms in bursts; an unchanged fleet now opens 4, and a goblin's report one more.
A goblin's `goblins notify` tells the supervisor over its pipe as it returns, without waiting for an answer: the supervisor runs a cycle, which takes in a blocked question or a wait on the Overlord, and tells every board, so a report reaches an open board within a second rather than at the next refresh, and leaves the board's last error as it was.
With no supervisor running, or one too busy to take it, the report shows at the next refresh.
Each stream keeps what its board holds, and sends each build after the first as a change to it: for an object, the keys whose values changed (`keys`) and the keys it lost (`gone`); for a list whose items each carry an id no other item has, the items that changed by id (`items`), and every id in order (`order`) when items came, went or moved; anything else whole (`value`).
When a list only lost items and gained some at its end, as the actions do when the newest comes and the oldest goes, the change names just those ids (`removed` and `appended`) wherever that names fewer ids than the order (#1611).
A build's time and revision, which every build moves, and its meters, which nearly every build moves (`at`, `revision`, `memory`, `disk`, `processors` and `gpu`), are not in its change: they come after it as a change of their own, the `meters` event, which the board lays over the snapshot it shows without parsing the fleet it holds again (#1611).
So a build in which nothing else changed sends only its `meters` event, and a click that waits for a revision sees the change of that revision first.
Each snapshot was the whole fleet, about a megabyte, though between two builds a few goblins' clocks and the meters move: replayed through the stream, a recorded 265 seconds of the live fleet's stream fell from 23.1 MB a minute to 0.26 MB a minute after its first snapshot (#1611).
A snapshot carries of the store's history only what the board shows: of the 512 sessions the store keeps, the CEO's and the one each task names as its own in its generation; of the activity, what happened in the last minute and the presentations still running; and of the CI runs and deploys measured, `ci_usual`, each repository's median time for a run of each kind, which a card paused on one names.
On the live fleet on 2026-10-28 that took a board's first snapshot from 985 KB to 612 KB, a sub-agent's start, which sent all 512 session ids again (22 KB), sends nothing, and a finished CI run, which sent all 200 runs again (40 KB), sends at most the usual times, 217 bytes (#1611).
The Command Center's part (`questions`, `reviews`, `runs`, `credentials`, `browser_signins` and `actions`, with the supervisor's `instance` and `revision`) is read from the store's memory and sent as a change the moment it differs from what that board holds, while a snapshot may still be building.
A build carries the items as they are when it is sent, not as they were when it began, so it never undoes a change sent before it.
A task's progress checks, its `evaluate` actions, are not among the items: on the live fleet they were 234 of the 256 actions, though only the task's Details lists them.
Details reads the task's newest 20 actions from `GET /api/tasks/<id>/actions` when its Activity opens, and again when the task's `actions_at`, the time its newest action changed, moves (#1611).
A change that does not fit what the board holds is told to the CEO, and the board opens its stream again for a whole snapshot.
The Command Center shows one item at a time as a stack, a question, a review item, a run item or a credential request, the CEO's own items first, then goblins in the In progress order, each goblin's by longest wait, then goblins not placed yet by longest wait (the snapshot's `attention` names the placed ones), and a horizontal swipe on touch screens moves between them; the card stands alone, with no edge of the next one behind it, and its text is sized to read at a glance (19 px body, 22 px titles).
A card's own action row holds Back, its place such as 2 of 4, and Next, which leaves the item as it is and shows the next, on the left while more than one item waits, and its answer on the right.
Closing keeps every item for later.
Each local file or folder path a card names is a link, in a step card's reason and steps, a question's text, and a run card's title and the folder it runs in.
A path is a drive letter, a colon and a separator, then everything up to a space or a character no Windows path holds, less the punctuation that ends a sentence, or a whole backticked value that is such a path, spaces and all.
In the desktop window a click sends `POST /api/cards/open-path` with the card's `kind` (`credential`, `question`, `review` or `run`), its `id` and the `path`.
The supervisor finds that card again and starts File Explorer at the path, with a file selected in its folder or a folder opened itself, only when the card's own text names exactly that path, the path is on a drive of this PC, holds no comma and exists.
Anything else is refused and starts nothing, so the browser never names a path, page or command of its own.
In a plain browser, where File Explorer cannot open on his PC, the link copies the path instead; a click that worked turns its folder into a check for a moment, and one that failed says why in a few muted words beside it.
Every open item also has a way out at the bottom, beside its decision buttons, as he asked on 2026-10-15, never at its top:

- A card with no choices or answer box of its own, a command to run, a goblin's wait with no page, or a credential or step card, has **Skip** and a **pencil**.
  Skip clears the item and tells the goblin that asked, in its own terminal, to go on without it: a `review_clear`, a `run_skip`, which closes a command nobody ran in place of running it, or a credential card's close.
  The pencil, **Something else?**, opens a small window with that title and a box where he types or dictates his answer, with its microphone or Ctrl+Shift+Space held anywhere in the window, and pastes pictures.
  The board's green **Send**, offered once the window holds words or a picture, sends them to whoever asked as his word: a `review_answer`, a `run_answer`, which closes the command in place of running it, or a credential card's `"action": "answer"`, which closes it as answered.
  A step card he answers finishes as **Sent** with You wrote: and his words, the line History keeps for it, and closes the goblin's waits on him opened while it was open, as Done does; the goblin hears his words once.
  The window stays open until the board takes his answer, so one the board refuses, such as words shaped like a key, shows why in the window in full with his words still in it, and nothing goes on the card.
  Each pasted picture goes to `POST /api/answer-images?task=<asker>` as it is pasted, which keeps a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP picture of at most 10 MiB in the asking goblin's task folder, or in the state folder's `answer-images` for the CEO, and his answer names each kept picture on a line of its own, `Image: <path>`, for the asker to read.
  Cancel sends nothing.
- A question, which has its own Other for a written answer, and a review with its own answer box, have a plain **Skip** beside Send: a `question_clear`, which also tells a goblin that asked to go on without it, or a `review_clear`.
- A Scrawl review card, a review or a goblin's wait with a page, has a plain **Skip** beside its one button that opens the page, and no box for words at all, neither an answer box nor the pencil, since he answers on the page (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "There does not need to be a text box in when there is a scroll open review page involved").

A command he skipped or answered reads Skipped or Answered, and the CEO hears of either.

Each card sends only its own answer.
The moment the Overlord sends from a card, an answer, a review answer or a Clear, its check draws with Sent (or Opened, Downloaded or Cleared) and three quarters of a second later the next open item follows, passing over any sent in this sitting, while the action is delivered in the background; CEO received or Delivered to <goblin> joins the check if delivery lands while it shows, and with nothing left it shows You're all done, with no Back, and the Command Center closes.
Back to a question he answered and moved past in this sitting, or answered elsewhere, shows History's change card in the stack, with its Back and Next: his answer checked, from the moment he sent it even before the snapshot carries it, and its other choices open, so Change answer sends an `answer_change` once, as from History.
A question he went back to never shows its done card.
A request the board refuses keeps its card on screen with what went wrong, and can be sent again with Retry under the same request identity; a run card stays to show the command's result.
A request refused, or failed before the board took it, after its card moved on or the Command Center closed opens nothing: its item keeps waiting under the badge, and opening it from Waiting on you shows Retry with a few words of what went wrong.
An item the Overlord acted on never comes back by itself: a delivery that fails or never arrives reads as the item's line in History, with its warning mark and the supervisor's `advice` on what to do while the action is still recorded, and it neither reopens the Command Center nor returns the item to Waiting on you.
An item leaves the whole board in the frame he acts on it, not when the next snapshot arrives: its alert and Windows notification, its count on the badge and in the tab's title, its row under Waiting on you, the CEO bar's line and its goblin's Answer button all go with the click, on that board and on every other board open in the same browser, which hear of it on a `BroadcastChannel`.
On 2026-10-02 the live board took 3 to 13 seconds to build a snapshot, and all of those kept asking a question for that long after its card had closed with its check.
A send the board refuses puts the item back, with why, as before.
The supervisor closes the item as it takes the request: an answer, a review answer and a Clear or Dismiss all close it under the store's lock before the request is answered, and the queued action only delivers the answer or tells the CEO what was dismissed, so a dismiss no longer waits behind a delivery for its turn.
An item the board has once seen closed stays closed: a snapshot built before an answer and arriving after it, a reconnect or a second window brings nothing back, and the item shows as the board last saw it closed.
Closed here means the same publication, the item with that `created_at`: an ID the supervisor takes again after it dropped the earlier record is a new item, whose count and CEO bar line show.
An item keeps its publication when the CEO registers again and its identity moves.
The Command Center never opens itself while the Overlord types on the board, with the focus in a text field, a comment box or a terminal (decision 3596, after his 2026-09-28 words that it must not "prevent input when the editor has text annotation input"): a new question then waits under the badge with its alert, and his typing keeps its focus.
A click on the dimmed board outside the card closes the Command Center, and a click anywhere outside the open inbox closes the inbox.
Every time the Command Center opens, from an alert, the counter or a card, it starts at the top of its item, however far down it was left, and moving to another item shows that item from its top; within one item the scroll stays where the Overlord puts it.
A card whose item closes while it is on screen and that sent nothing itself, answered with `goblins answer`, on another board or on its page, or taken back by its goblin or the CEO, finishes as one he sent from does: its check draws with Answered, or Closed for an item nobody answered, over the line its History row carries, and three quarters of a second later the next open item follows.
A card whose answer did not arrive keeps its place and says what to do, and a run card stays to show the command's result.
Drafts survive closing, reconnecting and moving between cards, and the header button, whose badge counts what waits on the Overlord, opens an inbox of those items under Waiting on you, then a history of what he answered, cleared or ran, newest first by when each closed.
The board is sent the items still open and those closed in the last hour, with `history_count`, how many closed items its History lists, at most 20: History, closed until he opens it, reads the newest 20 closed items of every kind from `GET /api/items/closed` when it opens, and again whenever the newest closed item the board holds changes (#1611).
A Change answer for a question the board no longer holds, such as one from his page of an AFK stretch, reads that one question from `GET /api/items/closed?question=<id>`.
Waiting on you lists everything that needs him, and only that: every open question, review item and run item, and each live browser walkthrough its goblin asked him to watch with `goblins present --watch`, as its line with a Watch button (an eye) that opens it, counted in the badge.
A click anywhere on an item's row, or on its button, opens that item's card in the Command Center, so a page opens only from its card (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "the button didn't open up the Command Center modal, it just took me right to the page").
A review he is asked to watch is a review item, never a presentation: [Nonblocking presentation notices](#nonblocking-presentation-notices) says how.
A presentation without `--watch`, such as a goblin's own test walkthrough, stays off the Command Center; its task card still shows **Browser active**.
On every card that shows it, the task card, the canvas's and the phone list's, the CEO's included, **Browser active** is a button of its own, outside the card's, that opens what is active in a new tab: the Screen view while the goblin's own browser runs, and otherwise the latest page the card presents, at the safe address the presentation reported (#2559).
A goblin's wait on him follows a decision only when the goblin declares it, as it raises the wait.
While a decision for its task is open, the CEO's question for that goblin (`goblins question --for <task>`) or the goblin's own question with choices, `goblins notify --waiting-on overlord` takes `--follows <question-id>` when the wait is that decision or `--separate` when it is a card of its own, and refuses a wait that declares neither, recording nothing, naming each open decision by its question ID and first line.
The supervisor records the declared ID on the wait (`follows`) and infers none, so a wait that declares none is its own card whatever is open: a need he never sees stalls its goblin, and an extra card does not.
While that question is open the wait shows nowhere and counts for nothing, so he sees one card, the decision (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "These follow-ups should not be notifications in the Command Center ... But the initial one is critical").
The answer to that question closes the wait in the same change that records it, whoever gives it, his answer from the board or the CEO's, so answering the decision clears both and no open wait is ever hidden behind an answered one; a wait declared to follow a question already answered closes as the supervisor takes it in.
The board hides by that ID alone, never by comparing times.
Only a wait that opens nothing can follow a decision: one with a page, a link, a file its goblin delivered or a command declares `--separate`, and a wait whose decision closed without an answer, such as one he dismissed, is its own card.
A wait with something to open, a page, a link or a file its goblin delivered, always stays its own card.
A goblin panel whose goblin is waiting on the Overlord offers Answer, which opens the stack at that goblin's question or review item.
Once submitted, every tab displays the durable answer rather than an unsent local draft.
Answers retain their question and CEO identity and enter the durable native CEO message queue, never a worker send or gate approval.
Answers he gives to several of the CEO's questions in one go reach the CEO as one message, which says how many there are and lists each question's id, its text and his answer in the order he gave them, as `[1/4]` to `[4/4]`.
Every answer still queued for the CEO goes in that message, whatever kept it queued: a CEO in a turn, one whose input was not empty and ready, one that was closed and has come back, or a supervisor that restarted.
An answer waits for his next one only while another question of the CEO's still waits on him, for up to five seconds after his newest answer, since he answers a stack of them two to four seconds apart.
With no other question waiting on him there is nothing to wait for, so an answer to the CEO's only question goes at once, and so does the last answer of a stack, with the ones before it.
The worker is woken as that wait ends, so an answer alone is never held past it.
What he sends the CEO keeps the order he sent it in: a message of his own, or a review answer for the CEO, queued after an answer ends that answer's wait, so the answers before it go as one message at once, and the answers he gives after it follow it.
An answer alone reads as it always has.
Each answer in a message keeps its own action and its own check, and they are sent, delivered or warned about together, since they were typed once.
On 2026-10-09 four answers given within seven seconds were typed into the CEO as four messages in the middle of a turn, two minutes after it had restarted.
Claude Code, Codex and Pi primary-context guidance routes explicit user decisions through this command.
Publish from the same registered primary shell, continue independent work or finish the turn awaiting the reply, and do not also open a native prompt tool: a normal message cannot answer a correlated native Codex/Pi prompt.
Duplicate HTTP/SSE outcomes cannot cause a second delivery; interrupted delivery becomes uncertain.
The CEO's work on the board follows the home's CEO, not one CEO process, so closing the CEO and opening it again strands nothing.
Once another CEO process registers in the home, its open questions, its review items whose answer has not reached it, its run items not yet ended and the deliveries queued to any of them move to that CEO, and nothing of it is superseded.
While no CEO runs, an answer or review on its way to the CEO waits queued instead of failing, and how a run item ended is kept on the item and told to the CEO once one runs again.
The store bounds question history at 128 records, retires the oldest cleared, answered, failed or unconfirmed (`uncertain`) question first and then the oldest superseded one, and defers overflow only while every held question is pending, has its answer or the Overlord's change of it on its way, or has its page still watched.
The bounded publication inbox is admitted in timestamp order, not hash-filename order, and rollover keeps its cutoff below the incoming timestamp so deferred and same-time questions are not discarded.
Conflicting, corrupt or oversized inbox records leave bounded diagnostics and cannot stop unrelated native events.

## Goblin questions

A goblin's `goblins notify <id> --blocked "<question> options: <answer> (Recommended) | <answer>"` asks the CEO.
It never appears in the Overlord's waiting count, alerts him or opens the Command Center; after it is answered, History shows who answered it.
Once the CEO answers or acknowledges it, its notify still holds the task, blocked or failed with its words, until the goblin reports again.
The CEO publishes a decision that needs the Overlord with `goblins question`.
A question's first choice that ends with `(Recommended)` is shown first and marked, and the mark is stripped from every choice.
`goblins notify` records a question with its choices last: the choices are what follows `options:` on its line, and a detail on a line after them is recorded before the marker, so it never joins a choice.
A question that writes `options:` more than once, such as in a detail, or has fewer than two choices after it on its line, is refused before anything is recorded, since no reader could tell its choices from its words.
The same question sent again while its notify still waits unanswered wakes the CEO once: the goblin is told it already asked it, and once it is answered the same words are a new ask.
Every reader takes the choices after the last marker, so one named in a detail of a notify recorded before that refusal stays part of its question.
The card shows the choices as a plain list of radio buttons, each the answer's own text with no A, B or C, because the Overlord picks an answer, not a letter.
`goblins notify` and `goblins question` refuse, recording nothing, a choice that is only a letter or number, such as `a`, `B)`, `(c)` or `2`, and say to write the answer itself as the choice.
When every choice starts with the goblin's own letter in order, such as `A) `, `b. `, `(c) ` or `D: `, the card drops those letters from what it shows; the answer is still the goblin's choice word for word.
A goblin can attach one review image to each choice with `--image <path>`, repeated in the order of the choices, so the Overlord picks by picture: the card shows a thumbnail for each choice, captioned with its answer, and any thumbnail opens a full-size gallery with its position, side buttons, arrow keys, swipe, click-to-zoom and a Choose this answer button for that image's choice.
While the gallery is open it replaces the card, so a strip of the question's thumbnails under the image jumps straight to any other image.
When the asking goblin has a review page live, the card and its gallery offer it with Open review; [Review items](#review-items) says which page.
An image must be a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP of at most 10 MiB inside the task's worktree, task scratch or data directory, reached without a symlink or junction, and `goblins notify` refuses a wrong count or a bad image before anything is recorded.
The board never sees an image's path: it serves image n of a question at `/api/questions/<id>/images/<n>`, checks the file again on every request, and stops serving once the task restarts or ends.
A blocked notify without an `options:` marker, and every other worker alert, stays in the CEO wake queue only.
Only a process running under the task's own terminal can surface its notify, by the same proof `goblins register` uses: under the program of its native terminal, whose host must answer.
In a native terminal a process whose chain of parents stops short of the program, as Git Bash leaves `timeout 60 goblins notify ...` when it runs timeout, an MSYS program, by replacing its own Windows process, is proven instead by the proof value the terminal's host put in the terminal's environment as `GOBLINS_HOST_PROOF`, beside `GOBLINS_HOST_ID`; the CEO's own registration, its pipe proof and the sender of its sends take the same proof in a native terminal.
The host records only the value's SHA-256 and when the terminal's program started, so the value proves nothing once Windows gives the program's pid to a later process.
Every process the terminal starts inherits the value, so it proves nothing in a Herdr pane, to which a Herdr server started from the terminal would hand it, and `goblins serve` forgets the id and the value of the terminal it was started from before it starts anything.
A notify that fails that proof or offers more than eight choices still wakes the CEO, and `goblins notify` prints why the board could not show it.
Such a question never reaches the board, so once it has had 30 seconds to arrive `goblins answer` sends the CEO's choice straight to the goblin that asked, marks the notify answered and records nothing on the board; it refuses, sending nothing, when the goblin's current generation started after the notify, since that goblin never asked.
The question is bound to the task generation and terminal that asked.
The Overlord's answer goes to that goblin exactly once, never through the CEO and never to a respawned successor: typed into its native terminal the way `goblins send` types, submitted once its composer shows it and delivered once the harness works on it.
The notify then reads answered: `goblins drain` prints the board's answer and acks the record without `--ack-blocking`, and the monitor stops re-asking it.
The CEO still acks it in the ordinary way.
The CEO acks a notify once it answered the goblin itself, such as with `goblins send`, so the board then closes its copy as answered by the CEO (`answered_by: ceo`, no choice recorded), which reads "The CEO answered it" with the check of any answer, and an answer queued before that ack is refused with nothing sent.
`goblins answer --record-only` can still record which choice it was.
One window stays open: if the CEO answers with `goblins send` and the Overlord answers on the board before the CEO acks, the goblin receives both, each labelled with its sender.
The CEO answers a goblin's question in a structured way with `goblins answer <question-id|wake-seq> --option <choice> [--note "<text>"]`, from the registered primary CEO only.
The choice is named in full or by its first word (the `a`, `b` labels goblins give their options), and a label that names two choices, a choice the question does not offer, a notify without choices, one already answered or handled, and a restarted goblin are refused before anything is sent.
The [goblins answer reference](commands/answer.md) owns CLI answer retention, delivery and recording.
Every answer, on the board or through `goblins answer`, is recorded on its question as `answered_option` (the choice; empty for a written answer), `answered_by` (`goblins` or `overlord`) and `answered_at`, so a closed question shows which option was chosen.
History marks who answered each question: a crown for the Overlord, the CEO's face for the CEO, and the CEO's face with a moon for an answer the CEO gave while AFK mode was on (`answered_away`, which the supervisor sets from AFK mode's state when it receives the CEO's answer or closes an acked question, whatever a sender says).
Each row gives when it closed and, on a line of its own, the reason the CEO gave with `--note` or, as Changed from, the answer his change replaced; an answer that failed or is unconfirmed keeps its warning mark.
Every answered question can take a new answer at any time, whoever gave the one it has (the Overlord, the CEO, or the CEO while AFK mode was on) and whatever the asker did since.
History's pencil (named Change the answer to, then the question) opens the question with its answer checked and who chose it and when, and Change answer queues an `answer_change` with his choice or his written answer.
The supervisor admits it for a question with an answer, on its way or delivered (`succeeded`, or his board answer `queued`, `running` or `uncertain`), and refuses a question still pending or whose answer failed.
It records the change on the question the moment it queues it, so every board shows it at once: the answer, `answered_by: overlord`, `answered_at`, `change_id` and `replaced_answer`, the answer it replaces.
A change to the answer the question already has sends nothing: it is recorded as done with nothing sent, so a second tab or a stale card choosing it again is one change, and a retry under the same request ID finds it.
The board sends a change under one request ID until the supervisor has it, so a double press, and a resend after a dropped connection, are the same action.
A change runs only once every earlier answer and change to its question has been handed over, so a change made while the first answer is still on its way, held back or waiting for its reader, follows it.
It reaches whoever acts on the answer once, as a change: "Was: <old>. Now: <new>." with the question, asking them to undo or redo what the first answer started.
The goblin that asked gets it in its own terminal, saved for its Resume while it is paused and taken at its next tool call while it works.
The CEO gets it for its own question, and for a goblin's whose task restarted, ended or cannot be reached, naming the task and the question, so a change never reaches a successor that did not ask.
Once it is handed over the question reads answered, whatever became of the answer before it.
When the asking goblin's pull request already merged, the card says in one line that the change goes as a new instruction.
An answer the Overlord gave somewhere else, such as in chat, closes its card once the registered CEO records it with `goblins answer <question-id> --option <choice> --record-only --in <where>`: the question reads `answered_by: overlord` and `answered_in` naming where, an open card finishes with the Answered check and "You answered in chat · recorded by the CEO", and nothing is sent to anyone.
The CEO's own question takes only this form, since only the Overlord answers it, and `--in` is a few plain words.
A question that closed without an answer, because its goblin restarted or ended, stays listed with its reason until the Overlord clears it (`question_clear`), or until 128 questions are held and it is the oldest superseded one, which makes room for a new question; a pending question is never dropped, so an unanswered decision is never hidden.
The Overlord dismisses a pending question he answered elsewhere or no longer needs with the close icon beside Send decision, named Skip this card, the same `question_clear`: it closes as dismissed, the card finishes with Dismissed, and the CEO is told, since it asked the question or holds the goblin's notify that did, which still waits for it; a question whose answer is on its way cannot be dismissed.

## Working and waiting reports

A goblin that resumes, or waits on something, says so without asking anything:

```powershell
goblins notify <id> --working "wiring the store"
goblins notify <id> --waiting-on <task-id|run-id|overlord|ci|deploy|memory|gate-daemon|docker> "<why>"
```

Both write a status line only, so they wake nobody, and a newer one of them replaces an older blocked or failed reading on the board while the question itself stays in the CEO's queue.
A wait on a run ID names the no-mistakes run the goblin waits on, such as one it started outside its own worktree, and that run's step counts as the goblin's work.
The task reads `working` with the reason, or `waiting` with the reason and `waiting_on` naming the target, unless a newer question, the gate's own decision, or a merge says otherwise.
A wait on another task clears itself once that task reports done, and a wait on CI, a deploy or memory lasts until the goblin reports again; the CEO releases any wait with a `--working` line of its own.
A goblin whose preparation is complete while it waits for another task's delivery or a pull request's merge may report `--prepared-wait` instead, which writes such a waiting line and also authorizes one automatic dependency pause, as [goblins notify](commands/notify.md) says; a plain `--waiting-on` never does.
Waiting on the Overlord is the one wait that wakes the CEO: it also opens a review item for him, named `waiting-<task>-<wake sequence>`, which he can answer or clear, and which stays until he does, the goblin withdraws it with `goblins review --id <id> --task <task> --withdraw "<reason>"` or its task is gone.
Nothing the goblin reports after it closes it, done included, since he may not have seen it yet.
A question the goblin asks while it waits (`--blocked`) replaces no wait: the goblin waits on the Overlord and on the question at once, the item stays, the task reads blocked while the question is open, and once it is answered the task reads the wait it still stands on.
So `--waiting-on overlord` is only for a wait on the Overlord personally: his sign-in, his click, his page.
A card in his Command Center asks him something or lets him act, so a wait on him gets its item only when it names what he acts on: a page (`--lavish`), the page he takes the step on (`--link`), a command (`--run`, whose run card is the wait), a step card the goblin filed, a file it delivered, or an open decision it follows.
One that names none of them wakes the CEO with ` (no card: it names nothing he acts on)` at the end of its line and opens no item, and the goblin is told how to put the step itself in front of him; on 2026-10-18 he met such waits, a goblin's need for two grants among them, as cards with nothing to press but Skip.
A choice the CEO can make, such as whether to start something now or after a reset, is an actual question and uses `--blocked` with options.

Scrawl is the Overlord's name for the review page he annotates and answers on.
The `lavish-axi` command serves it, and the command, the `--lavish` flags and the `lavish` state fields keep their names.
A wait whose answer the Overlord gives on a Scrawl page names the page:

```powershell
lavish-axi .lavish/plan.html --no-open
goblins notify task-id --waiting-on overlord "pick a plan" --lavish .lavish/plan.html
```

The notify opens the page without a browser, and refuses, recording nothing, when the file is not an HTML page or `lavish-axi` cannot show it.
The page's link goes into the wait's line, so the CEO's wake carries it, and onto the wait's review item.
`goblins serve` then polls the page, one bounded `lavish-axi poll` at a time, and is the only one that does: a poll hands the Overlord's feedback to whoever runs it, so nobody, goblin or CEO, polls a page themselves.
It polls until the review on the page settles (his end of the review there, with his answer or without one, an agent's end, or three failed polls in a row) or the goblin's task is retired, whatever becomes of the item meanwhile: the goblin's withdraw of the wait's card, the CEO's answer to the question AFK mode made of the page, or a card he dismissed never ends the watch, and one poller watches a page however many items name it.
What he sends on a page whose item has already closed reaches the goblin in its own terminal as a note on its page (or the CEO, when the goblin cannot take it), the CEO is told, and the page's conversation panel says who has it.
Scrawl sends a pick of an option the page declared, and a note on an element, the moment he sends each, and Send & End as a batch of its own, so the supervisor reads what he sends with whatever more he sends within two seconds as one answer: a note and the Send & End that approves it reach the goblin and the CEO once, as his answer, never as a revision first.
A note reaches the goblin with what it is on and the local path lavish-axi keeps each image he attached to it at, so a screenshot he pastes to show what he means reaches the goblin with his words or without them.
An agent's end of the review inside those two seconds is never read as his: what he sent is passed on as it stands, and the agent's end reaches the CEO as a wake of its own.
A supervisor that stops inside those two seconds passes on what he sent first, since the poll took it and Scrawl holds no other copy.
A poll that fails inside those two seconds is asked once more at once, so a Scrawl that stopped or restarted between his sends still hands over the rest as part of the same answer.
His answer is one that ends the review with Send & End (an empty Send & End approves as shown) or picks an option the page declared.
Every open item naming the page closes the moment the poll reads his answer, and the boards are told at once, so the Command Center shows it done within a second of his sending it, before anyone is told it, and his answer is saved whole under `state/reviews/feedback/`.
His answer then reaches a goblin by itself, beside the poll, so the next poll never waits on a goblin's terminal: the supervisor types it into the goblin's own terminal, as a board answer reaches it, and the CEO's `review` wake, retried until the queue takes it, says the goblin has it.
Only when the goblin cannot take it, such as one whose task ended, does the wake ask the CEO to relay it, and the CEO's own page wakes the CEO to act on it.
Everything he sends on a page reaches its goblin once, in the order he sent it, and nothing is told before what he sent ahead of it (the Overlord, 2026-10-17: "Make sure that even if I hit send and then I Type another message and then hit send and end. The message that was previously sent gets communicated."):
- What its goblin's terminal took nothing of, since the goblin was paused or between sessions and its terminal was not there, waits with what follows it and is typed once the terminal is back, into the session a pause's resume brings.
- What was typed into the terminal and not confirmed by its screen, or what the supervisor stopped in the middle of typing, is never typed again: it waits until the record the goblin's harness keeps of its conversation shows it taken, and is passed on as the goblin's then.
- Only what that record has not shown taken ten minutes after it was typed reaches the CEO, saying when it was typed and to check with `goblins peek` whether it waits in the goblin's composer, and the page's next message goes.
Until 2026-10-17 the first delivery the supervisor could not confirm, such as one to a goblin between sessions, was called uncertain and every later message on that page went to the CEO instead of the goblin, for good.
An answer that does not end the review leaves the page watched until he ends it, so what he sends after it still reaches the goblin as a note on its page.
The review ended with no answer, or a page that cannot be polled three times running, reaches the CEO as a `review` wake in the same way, and then every open item naming the page closes.
What he sends on the page without ending the review that answers nothing, with nothing more within two seconds, is a revision: the goblin gets the revision in its own terminal with how to send its next version, the CEO is told, the item waits on the next version (`revising_since`) and leaves Waiting on you, and the next poll answers him in the page's conversation panel: Revision received, the goblin (or the CEO, for its own page) makes the next version, which replaces this page.
The review goes on and the page is still polled, so a further revision or his answer reaches the goblin the same way.
The goblin's next version is a `goblins notify --waiting-on overlord ... --lavish <the same file>` again: it replaces the page's item in place (same item, its new words, waiting on him again) instead of opening a second one, and the page itself reloads to the new version.
A wait whose revision its goblin is making stands while the goblin reports work on it, and retires once the goblin reports done or failed.
A closed or disconnected review window is not one of them: lavish-axi keeps the session and queues every answer he gives on the page, so the poll goes on, ten seconds after a disconnect, and when he reopens the page the same review resumes and the next poll takes everything he sent, however many answers.
How it closes says who closed it: his answer closes it as answered, by the Overlord (`answered_by: overlord`), on the page (`answered_in: page`), which also ends the goblin's wait because the goblin has it; his own end of the review on the page closes it as his clear (You ended the review on its page.); and it reads withdrawn only when it closed without his word, such as a page that cannot be polled or an agent that ended the review itself (lavish-axi reports `ended_by: agent`).
Only the item gets the page polled, so when it cannot be published the notify says nothing watches the page and fails, telling the goblin to ask in text with `--blocked`; the wait's line is already recorded and still reaches the CEO.

A sweep catches whatever no poller watches.
When `goblins serve` starts and every ten minutes after, it reads every review session in lavish-axi's state file (`LAVISH_AXI_STATE_DIR`, else `~\.lavish-axi`), which takes nothing from any page, and gives each one to the fleet task that presented it: the item that names the page, else the task whose worktree, `data\<task>\` folder, `state\tasktmp\<task>\` folder or scratch folder (`scratch\<task>\`, in the home or on its Dev Drive) holds it, running, paused or retired.
A page that holds his undelivered feedback has it taken and delivered once, to the goblin while it runs and otherwise to the CEO as a `review` wake naming the task, and its conversation panel says who has it.
The open page of a retired goblin is ended (`lavish-axi end`), and the poll after the end takes anything he queued before it and posts why the review closed, so the page says its goblin was retired rather than that nobody listens.
A page the sweep cannot give to a fleet task is left as it is, and the CEO's own page is never ended.
A session whose page file is gone, as when a retired goblin's cleanup files its folders away, counts as ended: lavish-axi resolves a page's real path before anything else, so it can neither end nor poll it, and the sweep asks it nothing about that page.
Any prompt the Overlord left on such a page can no longer be taken, so the CEO is told once, as a `review` wake naming the task and how many prompts were left.

The monitor watches for the mistake this rule prevents.
A `lavish-axi poll` that runs in a goblin's worktree, or under a process that does, such as its harness, and that `goblins` did not start wakes the CEO once, as a `review` wake, for as long as that poll runs.
A live goblin's wake says to have it stop the poll and wait with `goblins notify --waiting-on overlord --lavish`.
A retired goblin's, known by the status log `goblins cleanup` keeps, says to read the page's open questions, put them to the Overlord and stop the poll.
A poll in a worktree whose goblin the home never had is somebody else's and is left alone.
The check reads the command lines of `node` and `lavish-axi` processes, and the folders of a poll and of the processes above it, once per monitor scan.

## Review items

A review item is something that needs the Overlord's attention without blocking anyone, such as a page of mockups, a report or before and after screenshots:

```powershell
goblins review --id mockups-review-1 --task task-id --title "Pick a task list layout" --image grid.png --image list.png --lavish http://127.0.0.1:4387/session/<id>
goblins review --id mockups-review-1 --task task-id --withdraw "Replaced by mockups-review-2"
goblins review --clear mockups-review-1 --reason "Decided: the grid layout ships"
goblins review --id dispatch-review-1 --title "Pick the dispatch order" --lavish .lavish/dispatch-options.html
goblins deliver --id setbacks-1204-oak --title "Setbacks and envelope, 1204 Oak St" --file "$env:USERPROFILE\Desktop\setbacks-1204-oak-st.pdf"
```

A goblin runs it from its own native terminal, proven the way its questions are; the registered primary CEO omits `--task`, and only a goblin's item takes images.
The ID is 8 to 128 letters, digits, dots, dashes or underscores; republishing the same ID with the same content changes nothing, and other content under that ID is refused.
Up to twelve images, each a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP of at most 10 MiB inside the task's worktree, task scratch or data directory, are checked like a question's and copied under `state/reviews`, so the item outlives the worktree and the goblin; `data/` is never used, because it is pushed.
A `--lavish` link follows the presentation URL rule below, and a refusal names the rule it broke.
`--lavish` also takes the page's HTML file, from a goblin or from the CEO: the command opens it without a browser, refusing a file that is not an HTML page or that `lavish-axi` cannot show, and the item carries the page's link.
`goblins serve` then polls that page exactly as it polls a page wait, and whatever becomes of it reaches the CEO as a `review` wake, keyed by the goblin or, on the CEO's own item, by the item's ID, and the item closes.
For another round, publish the page again under a new ID.
An item stays open until the Overlord clears it (`review_clear`), its reporter withdraws it with a reason, or the supervisor retires it; nothing expires it, and a `goblins serve` restart keeps it.
The supervisor retires every item of a task that is gone, such as one cleaned up, on every reconcile, and the item reads Withdrawn: <task> is gone.
Nothing a goblin reports retires its item, done or failed included, because the Overlord may not have seen it yet.
A delivered file is never retired, because its copy outlives the goblin: it stays until the Overlord answers on its page or clears it.
An answer closes exactly the question it answers and nothing else: the CEO's `goblins answer`, an answer the CEO records with `--in`, and his own board answer leave the goblin's waits on the Overlord and its pages open, so a wait only he can clear, such as a sign-in, never closes because someone answered a different question (decision 1001569, after the CEO's answer to Cora's question on 2026-10-13 also closed her unrelated page, which he then never saw).
The one question that is a page is the one AFK mode makes of a goblin's page, which the question records as `asks_page`: the CEO's answer to it, with `goblins answer` or acked after another answer, is the answer to that page, so it also closes that goblin generation's open items showing that same page published before the question, and no other item; a question whose text only names a page is no page.
That question also records the page's HTML file as `asks_page_file`, and the supervisor polls the page, as it polls a wait's, until the review on it settles, so the Overlord's answer there, Send & End or a pick of an option the page declares, answers the question at once as his, answered on its page: its card leaves Waiting on you within a second, its notify reads answered, and the goblin gets his words as from any page.
Once the CEO has answered it, what he sends on the page is a note the goblin gets, and a page another reporter's open item already shows stays that reporter's, so the CEO answers that question; on 2026-10-18 nothing polled such a page, so his Send & End on Kenny's page on local host showed nothing and its question stayed on the board.
The registered primary CEO can clear any open item with `goblins review --clear <id> --reason "<why>"`, such as one a retired goblin left; the item reads Cleared by the CEO: <why>, and `state/reviews.audit` records every clear, the CEO's and the Overlord's, with its time, item, goblin and reason, one per line.
An item the Overlord clears reads Cleared from the Command Center., and when it was a goblin's wait on him the goblin, which still waits, is told in its terminal that the wait is gone from there, and the CEO hears it as a `review` wake that asks nothing.
The Overlord can instead answer it (`review_answer`): his text goes once to the reporter, the goblin's own terminal while it is the same task generation or the CEO that reported it, and the item closes as answered; an answer for a goblin that restarted or ended goes to the current CEO instead, and the item reads `delivered: false`.
An answer the goblin received also reaches the CEO as a `review` wake that asks nothing, so the CEO sees every answer the Overlord gives.
The board sees each item in `snapshot.reviews` with an image count, never a path or a digest, and fetches image n at `/api/reviews/<id>/images/<n>`, checked again on every request.
A new review item waits in the Command Center inbox under the badge instead of opening the stack, and the browser tab's title counts everything waiting, so a board in a background tab shows it too.
An item addressed to the Overlord has one signal on a visible board: Open Command Center and its count.
Only the CEO's own questions (`task` empty) wait on him, and a goblin's question the CEO has left unanswered for ten minutes unless its review page's card shows it; any other goblin's question belongs to the CEO and enters History after it is answered.
A goblin's explicit wait on the Overlord, review page, delivered file, run item and credential request still belong in his queue.
No item shows an in-page toast or opens the Command Center by itself, including while the CEO's terminal is open.
Only an open item that asks the Overlord something alerts (the Overlord, 2026-10-07: "alerts should only be open command center questions"): a goblin blocked, failed or done is said on its card and raises no alert, and neither does routine progress.
A pause or stop the CEO or the Overlord asked for is never drawn as the goblin failing: a stop that did not finish reads Stop did not finish in the action's own colour, a pause that did not finish shows what the goblin is doing, each with its problems behind Details, and its report reaches the CEO as `lifecycle-<phase>`, never as the goblin's own `blocked:` or `failed:` notify, so it holds no question for the CEO and is not counted as one.
The first snapshot establishes the baseline for items.
A goblin's question with choices waits on the CEO; once the CEO has left it unanswered for ten minutes by the snapshot's `at`, it waits in the Overlord's Command Center too, where he can answer it himself, and it raises no alert.
The CEO's bar then says, in place of All quiet, how many such questions wait and how long the oldest has, so it never names a question his Command Center does not show.
The bar counts as at work only the goblins in In progress whose terminals run, never one paused, pausing or resuming, whatever it waits for.
Before an announcement the board claims the item through `POST /api/announce` (`keys`) and announces only what the answer's `claimed` names.
A browser without granted notification permission leaves the claim for another viewer, including the minimized desktop window; AFK mode still records suppressed events without asking for permission.
The supervisor hands each key to the first request and keeps it for thirty days, at most 2048 keys, so a second tab or a restart never announces that item again.
While [AFK mode](#afk-mode) is on it hands out nothing and records what was asked about, so nothing announces while the Overlord is away or repeats when he returns.
A hidden tab waits a second and a half before claiming, giving a visible tab the first chance; with no tab in view the hidden tab can send its Windows notification.
An item's announcement is claimed as `alert:<item key>@<created_at>`, whole, within the endpoint's 512 byte bound; an unreachable supervisor falls back on what the browser remembers.
An item's identity prevents repeats, and the same ask from the same asker within five minutes is one event.
The supervisor takes an item's ID again once it has dropped the record that used it, so an ID published again with another `created_at` is a new item: it is announced again, and the earlier publication's announcement leaves.
The browser remembers the last 100 announcements it showed.
Windows notifications require `document.hidden`: a hidden tab or minimized window, not an unfocused window that remains visible.
A notification is titled with who asks and says the item on one plain line in the asker's words, as the Command Center lists it, never a status or a lifecycle's words.
The browser asks for notification permission once, with the first announcement, and a notification click opens its item in the Command Center.
An announcement and its notification leave when their item closes or is published again under its ID.
A click on an item already closed opens the Command Center's list, never another item's card.
Its card names its goblin by the goblin's name and title, never the task's title, then shows the item's title once, its images as thumbnails that open the same full-size gallery as a question's, and its own page, when the supervisor watches it, as the page itself, with one Open review button that opens it.
The page is drawn from `/api/reviews/<id>/preview`: the supervisor asks the review server its link names for the page's export (`/api/<key>/export` beside `/session/<key>`, which carries the page's own files inline and opens no review window) and serves it under `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts; frame-ancestors 'self'`, so the page's scripts run in an origin of their own and only the board can frame it, and the card draws it in a sandboxed frame, scaled down, that takes no clicks; a redirect from the review server is never followed.
A page given only by its Scrawl link is watched on the page lavish-axi serves at that link, its `/session/<key>` on either of lavish-axi's addresses, as one given its HTML file is; a link to no session lavish-axi keeps shows no preview, and its card opens it from the same one button.
Until 2026-10-17 a link was never watched, so the CEO's `goblins review --lavish <link>` cards stayed after he sent and ended the review on their pages.
A review or a goblin's wait on a page another reporter's open item already shows, the CEO's or another goblin's, by its link or by its file, is refused and records nothing, whichever comes second, since his answer on a page reaches one reporter only.
The refusal names that item and its reporter and says his answer on that page reaches that reporter, so there is nothing to publish; a goblin whose wait is refused this way waits on the CEO instead.
A reporter's newer item for its own page is still allowed, as a wait's next version of its page is.
An item whose page the supervisor watches is answered on that page: it finishes the moment the Overlord sends or ends the review there, a goblin's item and the CEO's alike, and History keeps what he wrote, as You wrote: after who has it; or he closes it with Clear.
No Scrawl card has a box for words, watched or not.
No card opens an ended page: `goblins review` refuses a page whose review session has ended, whether he or an agent ended it, by its link or by its HTML file, recording nothing, with a line naming `--reopen`, which opens it again with lavish-axi's own `--reopen` only when he asked for another look, as a new item under a new `--id`, since publishing an item that has closed again is refused rather than reported published with no card shown; and a card whose page ends while it is open closes itself, as Closed, since the supervisor closes every open item of a page whose review ended (the Overlord, 2026-10-17, who opened a card the CEO had just published and got Scrawl's "Session ended. Return to your agent to continue.").
A page's card shows the page's own words as its body, the page and one Open review button instead of choices, since he answers on the page, and no line that explains how: a closed review window (`window_closed_at`) and a revision (`revising_since`) change nothing on it, since its one button opens the page again.
A goblin's question is never a page's card, whatever page its goblin has open: the question is the CEO's to answer, as every goblin's question is, and a goblin's newer question supersedes its older pending one, so one card shows.
A page and a question close apart: his answer on the page or his Clear of its card closes only that page's items, leaving the goblin's question pending and its notify unanswered, and an answer to the question closes the question and only the waits its goblin declared to follow it (`goblins notify --follows`), never the page.
Older items closed the other way still read as they closed: a question answered on its page (`answered_in: page`) and a question cleared with its page's card (`You cleared its page's card.`).
A card open on screen when he answers on the page finishes as one he answered from it does: a check with Answered and You answered on its page, then the next open item, and History lists it with a double check; a Clear or Dismiss the board refused because the answer had already closed it shows no error, and no card shows a refusal once its item has closed.
Any other item takes a written answer with Send answer or closes with Clear.
`goblins deliver` hands the Overlord a file the same way, as a Scrawl page: the registered primary CEO delivers any file it can read, a goblin only one from its worktree, task scratch or data directory, at most 64 MiB.
The file is copied under the item's own folder in `state\reviews`, as `file\<its name>` beside `page.html`, the page `goblins deliver` writes for it in the board's look and opens with `lavish-axi`, so the item is a page item the supervisor watches and what the Overlord writes there reaches the goblin or the CEO that delivered it, as for any page.
The page shows the file by its content, never its name: a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP picture inline, plain text up to 1 MiB as text, and a PDF up to 16 MiB page by page, drawn by pdf.js 4.10.38 from cdnjs out of the bytes the page carries, since a review page runs sandboxed and no browser shows a PDF in a sandboxed page; offline a PDF shows its name.
`--download` puts Download on the page, for a generated asset or a file the Overlord asked to keep; anything the page cannot show, HTML and SVG included, is only ever offered that way, so it is refused without `--download`.
An unchanged redelivery under the same ID changes nothing and opens no second page, and other content under that ID is refused.
The board serves no copy of the file: its card is the page's card.
A question's card offers Open review only for its asker's most recent live review page, from the same goblin session or the same CEO registration, so it never opens another task's page or one a replaced asker left behind; page links read Open review, never Lavish.
Each History row is one line, the goblin's first name and the item cut short to fit, with its time and a chevron that opens it.
An open row keeps the full name, time and chevron on that line and shows the whole text and how it ended across the row below, each address in its text, how it ended and its reason a link.
A step card whose values are stored opens to its page and steps and each input by its label, Already stored for a secret and Already sent to its asker for a plain value, with no value shown and nothing that stores, replaces or deletes one, since its pencil has nothing to change.
Its **Something else?** opens the same window as the open card's and queues a `message` action to the card's goblin, or to the CEO for a card of no task or one whose goblin has ended, which names the card, and the ended goblin's task, and says its values are stored, and the row says Sent to whoever it went to.
A resend after a lost answer keeps the action's ID only for the same words.
A closed item moves to the inbox history as You wrote: <answer>, Cleared, Opened or Downloaded for a document, the CEO's reason when the CEO cleared it, or Withdrawn: <reason>; an answer still on its way reads not yet delivered, and one whose `review_answer` action failed or became uncertain carries the same warning marks as a question.
Only `delivered` earns two checks: an answer the CEO took over because its goblin was replaced reads not yet delivered to the CEO until the CEO's hook reports taking it, then Sent to the CEO: <answer>, and an undelivered answer whose action has aged out of the snapshot reads delivery no longer recorded instead of being assumed delivered.
Closed items and their copies are pruned a week after they close; open items, answered items whose answer is still on its way, and a closed wait on the Overlord while its goblin's latest report is still that wait are never dropped, and a new item waits in the inbox while all 128 held items are one of these.
The API contract for the board is `data/board-ui/api-contract.md`.

## Run items

A run item is a command the CEO, or a goblin, needs the Overlord to run, such as a PowerShell or Git Bash script, a sign-in or a step that needs administrator rights; he runs it with one click from the Command Center instead of copying and pasting it:

```powershell
goblins run-request --id install-tool-1 --title "Install the tool the build needs" --shell powershell --command-file C:\temp\install.ps1
goblins run-request --id fix-acl-1 --title "Grant the service account access" --shell powershell --admin --command-file C:\temp\acl.ps1
goblins run-request --id gh-sign-in-1 --title "Sign in to GitHub" --shell powershell --command-file C:\temp\sign-in.ps1
goblins notify <task-id> --waiting-on overlord "Sign in to GitHub so I can push" --run C:\work\sign-in.ps1
```

`goblins run-request` hands the item to the supervisor over its named pipe, and the supervisor itself proves the sending process runs under the registered primary CEO, the proof `goblins question` uses: it walks up from that process to the CEO, each ancestor created before its child, since Windows reuses PIDs.
For a CEO in a native terminal, a process whose chain stops short of the CEO is proven instead by the terminal's proof value, which the supervisor reads from that process's own environment.
The sending process must also have started before it connected, so a process that later took its PID proves nothing.
The supervisor drops a client that sends nothing within 10 seconds and gives each request 20 seconds for its proof, and `goblins run-request` waits 30 seconds for the answer.
Every reply on this pipe stays readable after the supervisor has let go of the client, so a client that reads late still gets its whole answer; only a reply larger than the pipe's 64 KiB buffer waits for its reader, for at most 30 seconds or until shutdown, and a client that does not read holds up no other request.
The pipe is the supervisor's own: it creates the first instance of its name, waiting up to two seconds for a stopping supervisor to let go, grants the current Windows user alone, and rejects remote clients.
A supervisor that finds the name still taken serves nothing and lists that among the board's issues, and every command that uses the pipe sends only to the process holding this home's watch lock, so a squatter never receives a request.
The supervisor thus proves the sending process runs under the process `state/primary.json` names: a request from a process outside the CEO's tree or terminal is refused before anything is written, and an item planted in the state directory never reaches the board; a request needs the supervisor (`goblins serve`) running.
Processes of one Windows user are peers, though, and a same-user process that rewrites `state/primary.json`, starts a process with a spoofed parent, or copies the proof value out of a process in the CEO's terminal can still pass the check, so it is not a boundary between processes of the same user.
The Overlord reading the exact command before Run, and Windows UAC for an admin item, remain the final check.
The command file is read once: the supervisor stores its text as `state/runs/<digest>/command.ps1` or `command.sh`, which is what runs, so quoting cannot change it, and the item runs in the CEO home unless `--cwd` names a folder.
The ID follows the review item rule: republishing it with the same text changes nothing while the item waits, and republishing it with other text, or once the item has run or expired, is refused.
The board sees each item in `snapshot.runs` with its exact command, shell, folder and whether it needs administrator rights, and Run sends only the item's ID and identity through the board's action checks (exact Host and Origin plus the per-session token), never command text.
