`state/afk-report.json` is that report: who turned it on and off (`from` and `ended_from`, with his words in `asked` and `ended_asked` for a switch the CEO made), its lines under the headings `goblins afk report` prints, each heading's `group` (`done`, `decided` or `needs`, in that order), what each goblin finished with its task's `title`, its goblin's name and the goblin's last working `note` before it reported done, what waited on him in the Command Center with what became of each item as it stands now, what was still `under_way` with each goblin's latest note, what is `next` in the backlog's queue, and what was spent.
An issue a goblin closed in place of a pull request changed no code, so it is `closed` in place of `pr` in that data, listed under Goblins finished in `goblins afk report`, and left out of what changed on his page of the stretch.
When AFK mode turns off the supervisor makes his page of the stretch from it, once each decision the CEO answered in his place is settled in History: `state/afk-return/<session>/index.html`, beside the board's goblin faces and a copy of each picture a goblin's review item showed in the stretch.
The board opens that report in a modal when it sees AFK turn off, and **Open your last AFK report** in the CEO's Task view reopens it after dismissal (the Overlord, 2026-10-29: it "could be something that you know is part of like the task panel").
Its primary green **Go through what needs you** button opens the Command Center's decision modal on the first pending item, using its existing sequence through each answer to completion.
No Scrawl session or report review card is created.
The report uses the board's theme tokens inside its own frame, with a scrollbar and full wrapping of row titles and status text.
Its files are served only from the report directory, and its content security policy permits embedding only by the board itself.
Needs you draws each item still waiting as the Command Center's waiting row: its lantern box, who asks in lantern as the Command Center names them, and its stepped Answer with the icon of the item's kind.
The whole row carries the item's Command Center key, so a click anywhere on it closes the report and opens the Command Center on that item through the board's existing focus, and neither the frame nor the window navigates.
A step with no card opens its own card in the page.
Each decision's Change opens the question's current answer in History's Change answer card, using its existing `answer_change` submission path.
A decision under `decided` carries `item`, the question in his History where he changes its answer: `question:afk-left-<when it was logged>` for one the CEO answered in his place, and `question:<its ID>` for a goblin's question.
A `his` line the CEO settled is under Settled by the CEO, in `done`, with what became of it in `settled`.
A `his` line still open when AFK mode turns off becomes one card in the Command Center, and the report holds that card among its held items: a line with a card is told by the card and is in no section, so Needs you lists only a step whose card could not be made, as a report kept before cards were made lists them all.
A report kept before nothing was left for him can hold a line the CEO left him unanswered: when it has a held question, it appears only through that question's current state, under Needs you while waiting or Done once handled.
Spent holds each allowance used: its provider and window, the percent used when it turned on and when it turned off, either left out for a reading not taken, and whether the window reset in between, or for a credit balance what was spent of it.
A window at 0% wherever it was read is left out of Spent, and so is a credit balance not read at both ends or that did not fall, in the page and in the text alike.
Its free disk is the drive every start reads, the less free of the home's drive and the Dev Drive, with the bytes free when it turned on and when it turned off, of the drive's total.
It is absent unless free disk was read at both ends on one drive, since a start reads whichever of the home's drive and its Dev Drive has less free.
The board's filtering and presentation are documented in [the README](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/README.md#afk-mode).

`goblins afk log`, `goblins pr merge` and `goblins answer` send a decision as `afk-log`, which the supervisor writes only for a process it proves runs under the registered CEO, the proof a question takes, and only while AFK mode is on.
A merge train's landing is logged by the supervisor, on a request any process of the home may make (`afk-train-landed`, naming the train and the pull request) and only while AFK mode is on: the line is the train's own record of the landing, with the run that proved it, once GitHub says the pull request merged, and it is never twice in a stretch.
The caller's own words are never logged, so a train that does not run under the registered CEO, such as one started detached, has its landings recorded too.
A decision names its kind (`left`, `his`, `merge`, `deploy`, `migration`, `install`, `answer` or `other`), what was decided and the evidence it stands on, and one without evidence is refused.
A `left` line is a decision the CEO would have asked him, answered itself with its recommendation: one without its `answer`, its diagnosis, what was tried or two or more options is refused, since under AFK mode nothing is left for him as a question.
A `his` line is a step only he can take, which the CEO gave a backlog row and worked around, with why the CEO cannot take it as its evidence.
A merge word is logged before the merge and its outcome after it, as a second line for the same pull request, so the report never shows a merge word as a merge.
A merge line the CEO writes with `goblins afk log --kind merge` folds into the merge it names: the pull request it links or is, or the merge train's own pull request whose train landed it, which that train's line names as `train.Evidence` writes it.
Its words ride on that merge as `noted`, in the report data and the CEO's text, and go to the landing just before the line when one train pull request landed twice; a merge word that failed before another landed its pull request folds the same way.
A merge line the CEO writes by hand records no outcome of its own, so the outcome comes from GitHub: `goblins afk log --kind merge` reads the state of the pull request it links or is, once, and records the outcome `merged` on the line when GitHub says it merged.
A pull request that is open, closed unmerged or unread leaves the line without an outcome, so it still reads as not merged.
When AFK mode turns off the report asks GitHub, once for each pull request, about every merge line that has no outcome and names a pull request, within ten seconds and at most six at a time, and a pull request GitHub did not answer for is named in the report's Not read notes.
A stretch whose merge lines all carry their outcome asks GitHub nothing.
A hand line for a pull request that merged is that pull request under Merged with the CEO's words as `noted`, and a second line for the same pull request, or the train's own line for it, carries its words instead of making a second merge.
What did not land is under Not merged: a line the CEO wrote linking its pull request rides on it as what the CEO did about it, a decision it answered in his place that links it rides on it with its answer, and a `his` line that links it rides on it as `It needs you: <the step>`.
The bar's `decided` and `goblins afk status` count a folded line with the merge it names, not as a decision of its own.
Its evidence names the base tip `goblins pr merge` read a moment before merging; `gh pr merge --match-head-commit` holds only the head, so a commit that lands on the base between that reading and the merge is not seen.
Where the base requires a merge queue the word adds the pull request to the queue instead, and its evidence says the queue tests the merge on the base's tip; its outcome is that the pull request joined the queue, not that it merged.

While AFK mode is on, `POST /api/announce` records every key it is asked about and claims none, so the board shows no alert, sends no Windows notification and never opens the Command Center by itself.
It claims none even when the record cannot be saved, as on a full disk: the board reads a refusal as leave to announce, so while he is away the endpoint answers with nothing claimed rather than with the failure.
What a page asked about then stays recorded, so it is not announced once he is back either.
The page holds the same line by itself: while its snapshot says AFK mode is on, a claim it could not ask for hands its alerts and its Command Center nothing, where with AFK mode off it falls back on what the browser remembers.
A page that was not looking while he was away, such as a tab the browser put to sleep, asks about what it missed when it wakes, and gets it if AFK mode is off by then: he is back, and those items wait on him.
[The desktop window](#the-desktop-window) is silenced the same way: what its own reading of `/api/waiting` finds it claims here before it notifies, as the page does, and it is handed nothing.
Each cycle the supervisor records every item that waits on the Overlord as held, once in a stretch: a pending question the CEO asked him before it turned on, with the choice it recommended, an open review item or wait, a run item nobody ran, and an open credential request.
A goblin's question waits on the CEO, which answers it, so it is never held for him, even once the CEO has left it ten minutes and his Command Center shows it (`forOverlord`).
A goblin's Scrawl page filed while it is on is such a question: `goblins notify --waiting-on overlord --lavish` records its wait as `blocked: <why> (page <link>) options: <choices>` in the goblin's status and the CEO's wake queue, and `goblins review --task --lavish` puts the same notify in the queue once for each item ID, with the item's title, and writes no status line.
Neither publishes an item for him, and the goblin's command and what it is told back are the same whether it is on or off.
The choices are the one question the page's `data-lavish-choices` blocks declare, the recommended one marked `(Recommended)`. A page that declares none, several, or choices a question cannot offer (more than eight, one twice, an empty one, one over 500 characters, or one holding `|` or `options:`) is asked with none, and the question still reaches the board, since the page is the question.
A question that ends with its page, `(page <link>)`, offers no choices, so a title or reason that names `options:` in its words offers none either.
`goblins answer` takes the CEO's own words, at most 500 characters, for a question that ends with `(page <link>)` and offers no choices, records it with `answer_kind` `other`, and `goblins drain` says so in place of `goblins send`.
Its `answer` line in `afk.audit` carries the page as `link`, with the goblin's question and the CEO's answer as its evidence.
A page item a goblin filed before it turned on stays his and is held, and closes with the CEO's answer only to the question AFK mode makes of that same page, never with an answer to any other question of the goblin's.
Nothing is asked of him, while he is away or when he is back, as the Overlord ruled on 2026-10-12: `goblins question` refuses while AFK mode is on, and nothing the CEO logs becomes a Command Center question.
A step only he can take is a `his` line.
`goblins afk log` takes a `his` line only as the card `goblins run-request` or `goblins question` makes: a `command` from `--run`, with `admin` from `--admin` to run it as administrator, or `options` from two to eight `--option` with a `recommendation` from `--recommend` that is one of them, whose answer the CEO acts on, and it refuses a line with both a `command` and `options`, one with neither, one with `options` and no `recommendation`, and `admin` without a `command`.
When AFK mode turns off each open one becomes a card, made by the supervisor under the registered CEO's identity: a run item when the line carries a `command` and its `shell`, from `goblins afk log --kind his --run <file>`, elevated through Windows UAC as a `goblins run-request --admin` item is when the line carries `admin`, and otherwise a question titled with the step and its link in backticks, whose choices are the line's `options` with its `recommendation` as the question's `recommended`.
His answer to it is a `ceo_answer` like one to any question of the CEO's own, so the CEO reads the text of the choice he picked, or his own words for Other.
A line an older build logged with neither, or with `options` and no `recommendation`, becomes no card: the CEO's notice names it as a line that could not become a card, with why.
The card's id is `afk-his-` and the line's `at`, so a line is one card however often it is made, and a card that cannot be made leaves its line under Needs you.
When he closes the card, in whichever way, the supervisor appends a settle line to the log for it, once, with how the card ended, and the report counts the step as Settled.
When the stretch ends, each decision the CEO answered in his place is settled: its question goes straight to History as the CEO's own, `afk-left-<when it was logged>`, closed as `succeeded` with the CEO's answer as `answered_option`, `answered_by: ceo`, `answered_away` and `answered_at` when the line was logged, so it never waits on him, his recap lists it under Decided for you, For you, and the CEO is told which it settled and which it could not.
An answer to a goblin's page needs nothing more: the goblin's question is in History already, closed as the CEO's answer while he was away, and his page of the stretch lists it under Decided for you.
His change to either from History is an `answer_change` like any other: it reaches the CEO once for the CEO's own question, and the goblin, or the CEO when that goblin has ended, for a goblin's.
A run item the board made itself, for a credential card's terminal or a connection's repair, is not held: it is ready only for the moment after his own click.
Whether or not it is on, a memory reading under the floor counts toward the memory floor: after two in a row with free memory or commit under 4 GB, and with no start or other change under way, the supervisor pauses the live native goblin lowest in the Overlord's In progress order that is not pausing, paused or failed at the memory floor before, and has no git push, merge, pull, rebase, cherry-pick or am, or `gh pr merge`, running under its host.
It sends the `memory` pause through the same `goblins pause` the allowance floor sends, so the lifecycle keeps its handoff and the scheduler resumes it after two readings at 5 GB once memory and commit, less the about 1 GB its session takes, still meet the floor the machine's heavy line keeps for its next test run; a change under way, the pause included, starts the count again, so one goblin is paused at a time.
It passes over a goblin resumed from a memory pause in the last 10 minutes for the next one it may pause, pauses such a goblin only when no other may be paused, and the pause's evidence says which it passed over or that every goblin it could pause had just resumed.
Each pause at either floor while it is on is logged as a `pause` line with the readings it stood on, then its outcome, and the report lists them under Paused at a floor; they are not decisions, so the bar's count leaves them out.
An answer recorded as the Overlord's (`goblins answer --record-only --in <where>`) is refused while it is on, by the command and by the supervisor for the same request sent straight over the pipe.

A switch that cannot be read is never taken for on or off.
The board announces as usual and the recovery cycle reports the unreadable switch, the digest and `goblins drain` say `AFK MODE: UNREADABLE`, and `goblins pr merge` refuses to merge.
The Overlord's `goblins afk off` is the way back, under the same proof as any switch: the supervisor logs the reset with the reason the switch could not be read, writes it back to off and tells the CEO.
What the switch held is not known then, so no stretch ends and no report is built; the log keeps what was decided.
His `goblins afk on` is refused until the switch reads again, since it would guess at what the switch held.

The report is built from the stretch's lines of the log, the `done: PR <url>`, `done: closed <issue-url>` and `done: report <path>` lines every status log and archived status log holds from that stretch, each held item with what became of it on the board and its goblin's latest report since, and the allowance read when it turned on beside the one read when it turned off, with free disk the same way.
A held question the log holds an answer decision for is a decision, so the report and `goblins afk status` list it there and not as held.
Only the log says so: a held question the board closed as the CEO's with no decision logged stays in the report's `held` data, not waiting, and says no decision was logged for it.
In the report data, a held question the CEO answered after the stretch ended, under the standing rules where nothing is logged, says `answered by the CEO after AFK mode ended` when read again; an answer with no time recorded, or one at or before the end, keeps the word that no decision was logged.
What could not be read, a log line or an allowance, is named in the report rather than left out.

## Nonblocking presentation notices

After a presentation tool succeeds, the goblin that ran it reports the returned safe URL explicitly, from its own terminal:

```powershell
goblins present --id browser-walkthrough-001 --task task-id --kind browser --url http://127.0.0.1:5173/ --ttl 5m
```

A goblin proves itself the way it does for a question: the command must run under the task's own native terminal, so no native hook is needed and a goblin spawned while serve runs can present at once.
Its report names no native session, and the board treats it as live while the task's own runtime evidence is fresh.
`--generation` is optional and refused when it is no longer the task's current generation.
Use `--kind review` for a review surface, and omit the task only from the verified primary CEO's own process ancestry, which a CEO proves in its native terminal as it does for a question.
A URL must be https, or plain http where it never crosses an untrusted network: this machine (127.0.0.1, localhost, ::1) or the tailnet (`*.ts.net` names and 100.64.0.0/10 addresses), whose traffic Tailscale encrypts.
The tailnet URL `lavish-axi` returns is therefore kept exactly as returned and opens on the Overlord's phone through the tailnet as well as on this machine, and every refusal names the rule the URL broke.
For a Scrawl page, use `lavish-axi <file> --no-open`, then report the actual successful session URL; do not republish a user-ended session.
Refresh the same ID only while the activity remains live, and report `--state ended` with the same identity/URL on completion.
A presentation reaches the Overlord's Command Center only when it asks him to watch it, with one line he reads under Waiting on you, at most 300 characters: `--watch "Watch the checkout walkthrough I am running for you"`; a goblin's own test run omits it and stays off his Command Center.
A `--kind review --watch` waits on him until he answers it, so it is published as a review item instead, under the same ID, titled by that line and watched on the page lavish-axi serves at its link, and the same command with `--state ended` withdraws it: as a presentation it knew nothing of the page, so on 2026-10-17 his Send and End there left its line under Waiting on you until it expired.
IDs cannot change recipient or URL, and an ended pending record cannot be reopened by a later refresh.
The store and inbox retain at most 128 receipts, URLs exclude credentials/query/fragment and known sensitive paths, and expiry is limited to thirty minutes.
Only declared safe presentation links belong here, not raw tool arguments or arbitrary browser history.
Task indicators require current generation and fresh runtime evidence; primary notices use their exact verified registration, checked on the existing supervisor cycle at most once per minute.
Command Center offers Open page/Open review and Keep in background without redirecting, opening a modal, controlling the native browser, or pausing work.
This is an explicit reporting integration, not interception of every browser tool or a live browser-mirroring stream.

## Local endpoint boundaries

The listener requires a numeric loopback address.
Host and Origin checks, cross-site rejection, a per-instance mutation token, strict request schemas, and bounded request bodies protect the local control API.
A goblin's hook posts under `/api/goblin-hook/` carry no Origin or token, and its terminal's proof stands in for them ([The seal and the key alarm](#the-seal-and-the-key-alarm)).
These are local browser boundaries, not authentication against another process already running as the same Windows user.
Git previews refuse unsafe revisions, traversal, a symlink, junction or other reparse point anywhere on the file's path, and common credential-bearing paths.
The file itself is opened beneath the task root through `os.Root`, so a link swapped in after those checks still cannot resolve outside it.
Git output, timeouts, cache entries, concurrent previews, and event streams are bounded.
Native terminal frames preserve the actual screen, including anything printed there; avoid displaying secrets in the terminal.
Input bytes and native frames are not logged or persisted by this bridge.
Per-page CSP nonces permit the terminal's generated stylesheets while inline scripts remain blocked.
The separate `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` directive permits CSS style attributes across the page for xterm 6's ANSI truecolor rendering.
This is a page-wide CSS-attribute compatibility allowance; style elements still require the page nonce or a same-origin source, and script, connection and framing restrictions remain unchanged.

`GET /api/actions-spend?month=YYYY-MM` requires the board's current `X-CEO-Token` as well as the existing Host, Origin and cross-site checks; a missing or invalid token returns 403.
Only the one or two calendar months possible across UTC-12 through UTC+14 within five minutes of the real request time, and the month before the earliest of them, are accepted, so a device clock slightly ahead of or behind the supervisor's still gets its month at a month's start and end, and every device gets its last month; malformed or out-of-range months return 400 before any billing read.
`POST /api/actions-spend/repair` accepts the same months.
The snapshot's `actions_spend` remains the UTC month's report; the board selects the device's local calendar month from the existing page clock and requests this endpoint when that month differs, using the same clock to check refresh eligibility.
It asks again an hour after the reading's `checked_at`, kept between the device's next whole minute and an hour after the answer arrived, so a device clock ahead of or behind the supervisor's neither asks on every update nor keeps a reading for hours.
The existing billing reader receives the selected report month separately from the real reading time and requests that exact GitHub monthly report.
A reading's `total` holds the month's Actions `minutes`, `used_usd` (their value at GitHub's list price, its `grossAmount`) and `billed_usd` (what GitHub bills once public repositories and the plan's included minutes are free, its `netAmount`), each dollar figure rounded to the cent, and is null when nothing was read; what GitHub covered is used less billed.
`repositories` lists every repository with Actions use that month, highest use first, with the same three figures, so a repository GitHub covers in full stays listed; only usage in minutes counts as minutes, and storage counts in dollars alone.
The board's bar shows the GitHub mark and the billed total alone (2026-11-04), and its panel shows used, covered and billed, then the repositories, then one line saying when it was read.
The month beside the panel's title opens a menu of this month and last month (#2536); choosing last month asks this endpoint for it and shows its figures and repositories in the same panel, while the bar's billed total stays this month's, and the panel opens on this month again.
A retry shows as one refresh icon named Retry in the panel's title row, with no words beside it, which turns while the read runs, more slowly under reduced motion; no progress text shows meanwhile, and the other recovery actions keep their words.
The supervisor shares its cache by month across devices and its background reader, starts at most one GitHub read per month per hour, and evicts months outside the accepted range.
A month's reading that GitHub answered a day or more after the month ended is settled: a closed month no longer changes, so it is never read again on the hour and never becomes `stale`; a reading made on its first day after closing may miss its last day's use and is read again on the hour.
Concurrent requests for a month share one read bounded to 30 seconds; disconnecting one device does not cancel it, and the supervisor stopping does.
A failed billing refresh preserves that month's previous total as `stale`, with its original `read_at`, the new `checked_at` and the failure's `detail`; without a previous total it remains `unavailable`, and an available reading becomes `stale` after two hours with no `detail` of its own.
The mocked regressions in `internal/actionsbilling/usage_test.go`, `internal/supervisor/actions_spend_test.go` and `frontend/tests/actions-spend.spec.ts` cover the reader, cache, request boundaries and device calendars.

## Build and verification

Use Node 24.13.0 and the committed npm lockfile when editing the board:

```powershell
cd frontend
npm ci
npm run typecheck
npm run lint
npm test
npx playwright install chromium --only-shell
npx playwright install webkit
npm run test:browser
npm run build
cd ..
go build -o goblins.exe ./cmd/goblins
```

Vite generates `internal/boardweb/dist/board`, which git ignores; do not edit or commit it.
`goblins.exe` embeds it, and a checkout that has not built it embeds instead the one committed file under `internal/boardweb/dist`, a placeholder page that says the board was not built and names the commands that build it, so `go build` and `go test` need Go alone.
See [Development](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/README.md#development) for the requirements before installing a source build.
`npm run test:browser` first builds the board and every page under `frontend/tests/fixtures`, bundled as the shipped board is, into a folder of that run's own under the system's temporary folder, and the browser tests read those files, answering each request themselves.
No web server runs behind them: the pages are given the origin `http://127.0.0.1:1`, this machine as the board is really served, on a port the browser refuses to connect to, so no test waits on a port or opens a connection the machine can refuse, and a request a test does not answer itself fails at once.
`tests/iphone-terminal.spec.ts` runs in WebKit, the engine of every browser on an iPhone, at an iPhone's size and under the private board's https address, since WebKit refuses port 1 even to a page a test answers; it takes the idle callback away itself, counts the WebGL contexts the page asks for, and fails on any error the page throws, so an API Safari lacks that the terminal's path reaches, and that Playwright's WebKit lacks too, shows there before it blanks the Overlord's phone.
Outside CI every browser test waits, before it starts, while less than 5 GB of memory is free, and fails saying so after a minute: Windows takes about half a minute to release a test's browser process after the test ends, each still holding about 150 MB, so a fast run holds gigabytes on a machine the fleet shares; on a machine short of memory, run one spec at a time.
One unit test, `src/dev-server.test.ts`, starts the dev server on a port the machine picks and loads the board's page and entry module from it, so development mode stays tested.
The board's HTML template and the placeholder page are pinned to LF in `.gitattributes`: Vite preserves the template's newlines, so a build on Windows embeds the same bytes as one anywhere else.
Both Windows CI and the release workflow install from the lockfile, check the frontend, build the board, and fail when the build is not where `goblins.exe` embeds it from: the release before it compiles Go, and CI in a job of its own beside the Go tests, which then runs `internal/boardweb`'s tests over the build and the supervisor's test that a browser keeps every file the build addresses (#1966).
The runtime executable embeds those assets and requires no Node process.
The board names the build it serves, a hash of its `index.html`, which names every hashed file of the bundle, in the page (`<meta name="ceo-build">`) and in every snapshot, on `/api/snapshot` and on the event stream.
A tab whose loaded build differs from the one served, such as one left open across an install, reloads itself while it is hidden, the event stream is live and the Overlord is not mid-answer (the Command Center closed, no card in it and no comment on a diff keeping an answer not yet sent, no terminal listening to dictation, and no text field holding text), and otherwise shows one line, The board was updated, with Reload; a page loaded without a build, such as from the dev server, never acts.
The terminals are a page the board fetches the first time one opens, and a tab loaded before an install asks for its own build's page, which the new supervisor no longer serves.
Such a page draws nothing in its place, so the rest of the board stays drawn, and the board tells the CEO why.
With the event stream live and the Overlord not mid-answer, the tab then reloads onto the served build once for the build it loaded, and otherwise, or when the page is still missing after that reload, it shows The board was updated with Reload.
For frontend development, `npm run dev` listens at `127.0.0.1:5173` and proxies API calls to a supervisor on `127.0.0.1:4310`, translating only that explicit local development origin.
`BOARD_DEV_PORT` sets the port the dev server listens on; it defaults to `5173`, and the translated origin follows it.
`BOARD_SUPERVISOR` sets the supervisor the dev server proxies API calls to, such as an example fixture; it defaults to `http://127.0.0.1:4310`.
Setting neither keeps the defaults, so nothing changes for a developer who does not need them.
Both are permanent developer tooling: on machines where Docker, WSL or another proxy already owns port 5173, a dev server pinned to 5173 can silently serve or test the wrong build.
On such a machine, set `BOARD_DEV_PORT` to a free port and `BOARD_SUPERVISOR` to the running board you want to develop against.

Before any test step invokes `goblins` or `go test`, clear `GOBLINS_HOME` and `GOBLINS_STATE_OVERRIDE` or point both to an isolated temporary home.
For bounded local checks, use `GOMAXPROCS=2`, `go vet -p 1 ./...`, and `go test -p 1 ./...`.
The opt-in `tests/acceptance/native_board_windows.ps1 -Binary <absolute-built-executable> -HarnessBinary <absolute-harness-executable>` requires `GOBLINS_BOARD_REAL=1` and creates an isolated home, git project, named Herdr session, native CEO and worker processes, hooks, and board.
Build the harness from `./tests/fixtures/native-board-harness` with the basename `codex.exe`.
Validate/scan both supplied executables before running them when required by machine security policy; copying or rebuilding a flagged binary is not clearance.
The fixture's disposable `codex.exe` is a deterministic test process, not Codex and not a model invocation; its basename exercises Herdr's real foreground-process contract.
Only the recorded fixture PIDs/session may be stopped during crash checks.
The fixture uses `serve --example`, allowed only with a temporary CEO home and its own state directory.
That mode labels Example workspace and omits the production machine-wide orphan inventory, which otherwise sees unrelated host processes beside the isolated Herdr session.
It retains task monitoring and custody checks and never clears shared decisions or stops shared processes.
See [the implementation evidence](https://github.com/fpresta0607/code-goblins/blob/main/docs/evidence/cfo-native-board.md) for measured overhead, browser results, and the unresolved earlier Defender detections.

## Source provenance

Native contracts were checked against installed versions and the primary [Codex hooks](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/hooks), [Claude Code hooks](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks), and [Pi extensions](https://pi.dev/docs/latest/extensions) documentation.
The pinned [Cline Kanban source](https://github.com/cline/kanban/tree/abd4912c27ce6b7f18b5a8106c145fd838e90cc4) supplied adapted board, diff, history, and runtime-stream behavior.
Its Apache-2.0 license, copyright, file links, and modification notes are retained in `frontend/public/assets/NOTICE.txt` and the bundled license files; no upstream NOTICE file was present at that revision.
SIQshift's shared brand stylesheet, desktop/web controls, and shared ShiftGroups supplied inspected card/ghost/focus, native-select and disclosure primitives.
The board's palette and glass come from SIQshift's `packages/shared/styles/brand.css` and SIQstack's site (`src/app/brand.css`): the base and hero surfaces, the green, deep green and blue accents, the glass fill, border and shadow, the wordmark gradient, the electric tab ring and the blue focus ring, by their real values.
The user-approved generated mockups supplied the final dark/mint two-view composition and terminal-goblin artwork, and the 2026-09-30 SIQstack restyle, its regenerated workshop background and the goblin mark the tab and app icons are drawn from.
The board self-hosts three OFL faces, so it renders the same offline: Pixelify Sans for headings at weight 400 only, because heavier weights close its C and G into O; Nunito for body text, never below 15 px; and JetBrains Mono for code.
Their licenses sit beside the font files under `/assets/fonts/`.
The supplied code/workflow references informed review and lineage presentation without adding a graph dependency or an automation editor.
